ATA 51-00-55 - Section

1 tasks
51-00-55-200-801Preventive MaintenancePDF p. 59
Open original AMM PDF - page 59 ↗ 
2. Preventive Maintenance
A. References
Reference Title
20-30-80-910-801 General Cleaning of Metal (Series 80) (P/B 201)
51-21-11 P/B 701 PAINT STRIPPING - CLEANING/PAINTING
51-21-31 P/B 701 CORROSION REMOVAL AND CONTROL -
CLEANING/PAINTING
51-21-91 P/B 701 CORROSION INHIBITING COMPOUND -
CLEANING/PAINTING
737 NDT Part 6, 51-00-00,
Procedure 3
Investigation of Fire Damage on Aircraft Structure
B. Consumable Materials
Reference Description Specification
B00081 Solvent - Trichloroethylene ASTM D4080
C. Preventive Maintenance
SUBTASK 51-00-55-020-001
(1) After the fire is out, do these steps:
(a) Remove fire damaged interiors to expose structure. This includes interior trim, seats,
passenger service units, linings, insulations, galleys, etc. Removal of tubing, cables,
electrical wiring, etc., is not necessary if they are unaffected.
(b) Screen or mask off areas not affected.
(c) Open all drains, hatches and doors for drainage and ventilation. Use compressed air to
blow dry all hidden recesses.
SUBTASK 51-00-55-200-001
(2) Examine the structure for these items:
(a) Heat damage, soot, fire extinguishing material residue and corrosion.
51-00-55
(b) Paint discoloration indicates temperature had exceeded 350°F (177°C).
NOTE: Structural analysis required if temperature exceeds 350°F (177°C).
(c) Look for acid contamination of the structure, control cables and wire bundles with litmus
paper.
NOTE: Products of combustion or decomposed fire extinguishants may combine with
moisture to form corrosive chemicals.
(d) Flush the structure with water when necessary to remove contamination.
SUBTASK 51-00-55-030-001
(3) Remove and replace structure damaged beyond repair.
NOTE: Use Investigation of Fire Damage on Aircraft Structure, 737 NDT Part 6, 51-00-00,
Procedure 3 to determine any change in heat-treat of any suspect structure.
SUBTASK 51-00-55-100-001
(4) Remove soot by washing affected areas with solvent, B00081, or an applicable solvent per
TASK 20-30-80-910-801. Pressure gun application is suggested for flushing fay surface joints,
such as stringers to body skins. An absorbent cloth held beneath the area will catch the
contaminated solvent.
SUBTASK 51-00-55-280-001
(5) Inhibit corrosion that could be caused by residue from the dry powder fire extinguisher by
washing affected areas with a 10-percent-by-weight solution of sodium dichromate. The
dichromate solution acts as a mild corrosion inhibitor. Thoroughly dry all treated surfaces
before continuing with any other treatment. Repeat rinse and inhibition treatment if any white
residue shows.
NOTE: Any remaining liquid dichromate solution will weep through seams and fastener holes
during cabin pressurization and could subsequently discolor the exterior paint finish.
Forced hot air used to dry the area may be necessary before further flight if humidity is
high.
SUBTASK 51-00-55-211-001
(6) Look for remaining contamination behind clips, brackets, nutplates.
SUBTASK 51-00-55-200-002
(7) After removal of contamination, do the steps in CORROSION REMOVAL AND CONTROL -
CLEANING/PAINTING, PAGEBLOCK 51-21-31/701 when corrosion is found.
SUBTASK 51-00-55-370-001
(8) When corrosion is removed, do the steps in PAINT STRIPPING - CLEANING/PAINTING,
PAGEBLOCK 51-21-11/701.
SUBTASK 51-00-55-600-001
(9) To apply the corrosion inhibiting compound, do the steps in CORROSION INHIBITING
COMPOUND - CLEANING/PAINTING, PAGEBLOCK 51-21-91/701.

ATA 51-00-56 - Section

2 tasks
51-00-56-211-801Detection and Isolation of Alkaline Contaminated AreaPDF p. 61
Open original AMM PDF - page 61 ↗ 
2. Detection and Isolation of Alkaline Contaminated Area
A. Consumable Materials
Reference Description Specification
G50305 Sheeting - Plastic, Polyethylene
B. Procedure
SUBTASK 51-00-56-211-001
(1) Do this step for detection of alkaline contamination:
(a) Alkalines are clear and are not color detectable.
1) A white powdery deposit (aluminum oxide) would indicate corrosion on aluminum
structure.
NOTE: Around the top of nickel-cadmium battery cells, the alkaline electrolyte that
has overflowed the vent caps reacts with the carbon dioxide in the air and
produce a white powdery deposit. This white deposit is not corrosive and
otherwise harmless but is an indication that electrolyte has spilled or
otherwise escaped.
SUBTASK 51-00-56-160-001
(2) On discovery of alkaline spills, steps should be taken to contain the contaminated area. The
following precautions are advised:
(a) Do not allow alkaline spills to spread to adjacent areas that will not be cleaned.
(b) In battery areas, protection of equipment beneath using plastic sheeting, G50305 is
advised. If equipment is operating, venting should be maintained.
(c) Consider protecting uncontaminated areas by taping down protective material such as
plastic sheeting, G50305.
51-00-56-100-801Alkaline Spillage CleanupPDF p. 62
Open original AMM PDF - page 62 ↗ 
3. Alkaline Spillage Cleanup
A. References
Reference Title
51-21-11 P/B 701 PAINT STRIPPING - CLEANING/PAINTING
51-21-31 P/B 701 CORROSION REMOVAL AND CONTROL -
CLEANING/PAINTING
51-21-91 P/B 701 CORROSION INHIBITING COMPOUND -
CLEANING/PAINTING
B. Consumable Materials
Reference Description Specification
B00636 Acid, Acetic (Vinegar) (5%-10% Acetic Acid) Commercially available
product
G00116 Sponge - Synthetic
G00215 Brush - Soft Bristle Fiber
G01659 Swab - Cotton Or Rayon, (Disposable)
G50256 Water, Regular
G50305 Sheeting - Plastic, Polyethylene
G50316 Cloth - Clean, Dry, Lint-free, White, Cotton
C. Procedure
SUBTASK 51-00-56-913-001
(1) If equipment is adjacent to the treatment area, use plastic sheeting, G50305 to cover the
equipment to prevent inadvertent splashing of alkalines or treatment fluids
SUBTASK 51-00-56-100-001
(2) Wipe up excess fluids with cotton cloth, G50316 and discard cloth into plastic container for
disposal.
SUBTASK 51-00-56-561-001
(3) Do these steps to neutralize alkaline contamination:
(a) Neutralize the treatment area with a 5 percent acid, B00636 solution or full strength
household vinegar applied with a brush, G00215, cotton cloth, G50316 or swab, G01659.
(b) Continue to apply the solution until bubbles cease. Then allow the solution to remain on
the surface for an additional 5 minutes.
(c) Remove the neutralized mixture with a cotton cloth, G50316 or sponge, G00116.
(d) Rinse the affected area with generous quantities of clean water, G50256. Occasionally
agitate the surface with a brush, G00215.
(e) Neutralization of the alkaline fluid should be determined with litmus paper.
(f) Wipe dry with clean cotton cloth, G50316.
SUBTASK 51-00-56-350-001
(4) After removal of contamination, do the steps in CORROSION REMOVAL AND CONTROL -
CLEANING/PAINTING, PAGEBLOCK 51-21-31/701 when corrosion is found.
SUBTASK 51-00-56-370-001
(5) When corrosion is removed, do the steps in PAINT STRIPPING - CLEANING/PAINTING,
PAGEBLOCK 51-21-11/701, if necessary.
51-00-56
SUBTASK 51-00-56-600-001
(6) To apply the corrosion inhibiting compound, do the steps in CORROSION INHIBITING
COMPOUND - CLEANING/PAINTING, PAGEBLOCK 51-21-91/701.
SUBTASK 51-00-56-741-001
(7) Put the Airplane Back to Its Usual Condition.

51-00-57-211-801Detection and Isolation of Acid Contaminated AreaPDF p. 65
Open original AMM PDF - page 65 ↗ 
2. Detection and Isolation of Acid Contaminated Area
A. Consumable Materials
Reference Description Specification
G50305 Sheeting - Plastic, Polyethylene
B. Procedure
SUBTASK 51-00-57-211-001
(1) Do this step for detection of acid contamination:
(a) Examine for discoloration the surface for acid contamination.
1) Black, white, yellow and brown are predominant chemical reaction precipitate
colors.
NOTE: Precipitate colors depend on the acid and the material on which the acid is
spilled
SUBTASK 51-00-57-160-001
(2) On discovery of acid spills, steps should be taken to contain the contaminated area. The
following precautions are advised:
(a) Do not allow acid spills to spread to adjacent areas that will not be cleaned.
(b) In battery areas, protection of equipment beneath using plastic sheeting, G50305 is
advised. If equipment is operating, venting should be maintained.
51-00-57
(c) Consider protecting uncontaminated areas by taping down protective material such as
plastic sheeting, G50305.
51-00-57-100-801Acid Spillage CleanupPDF p. 66
Open original AMM PDF - page 66 ↗ 
3. Acid Spillage Cleanup
A. References
Reference Title
51-21-11 P/B 701 PAINT STRIPPING - CLEANING/PAINTING
51-21-31 P/B 701 CORROSION REMOVAL AND CONTROL -
CLEANING/PAINTING
51-21-91 P/B 701 CORROSION INHIBITING COMPOUND -
CLEANING/PAINTING
B. Tools/Equipment
Reference Description
STD-419 Gloves - Rubber, Elbow Length
STD-1392 Shield - Facial Protection
STD-1393 Aprons
STD-1394 Boots
STD-1395 Head Gear
STD-3904 Goggles - Safety
C. Consumable Materials
Reference Description Specification
B00095 Compound - Sodium Bicarbonate ASTM D928
G00116 Sponge - Synthetic
G00215 Brush - Soft Bristle Fiber
G50256 Water, Regular
G50305 Sheeting - Plastic, Polyethylene
G50316 Cloth - Clean, Dry, Lint-free, White, Cotton
D. Procedure
SUBTASK 51-00-57-160-002
WARNING
DO NOT GET THIS MATERIAL IN YOUR MOUTH, EYES, OR ON YOUR SKIN.
PUT ON EYE PROTECTION (GOGGLES, OR OTHER APPROVED
PROTECTION) AND GLOVES WHEN YOU USE THIS MATERIAL. MAKE SURE
THAT THERE IS SUFFICIENT AIRFLOW. THIS MATERIAL CAN CAUSE
INJURIES TO PERSONNEL OR DAMAGE TO EQUIPMENT .
(1) T ake these precautions when you work with an acid spill condition:
(a) Adequate protective clothing should be worn when handling or working in acid
contaminated areas.
1) elbow length rubber gloves, STD-419
2) safety goggles, STD-3904
3) face shield, STD-1392
4) aprons, STD-1393
51-00-57
5) boots, STD-1394
6) head gear, STD-1395
(b) Wash hands after using acid neutralizing treatment solution and/or materials before
eating or smoking.
(c) Waste materials, solvents, chemical solutions, wiping rags, masking materials, etc., shall
be collected and disposed of safely.
SUBTASK 51-00-57-913-001
(2) If equipment is adjacent to the treatment area, use plastic sheeting, G50305 to cover the
equipment to prevent inadvertent splashing of acids or treatment fluids
SUBTASK 51-00-57-100-001
(3) Wipe up excess fluids with cotton cloth, G50316 and discard cloth into plastic container for
disposal.
SUBTASK 51-00-57-561-001
(4) Do these steps to neutralize acid contamination:
(a) Neutralize the treatment area with a 20 percent sodium bicarbonate solution applied with
a brush or cloth swab. Particular attention should be given to faying surface joints.
Pressure application may be required to flush the joint thoroughly.
NOTE: 16 oz (454 g) of compound, B00095 in 1.00 gal (3.79 l) of water, G50256 will give
the desired mixture.
(b) Continue to apply the solution until bubbles cease. Then allow the solution to remain on
the surface for an additional 5 minutes.
(c) Remove the neutralized mixture with a cotton cloth, G50316 or sponge, G00116.
(d) Rinse the affected area with generous quantities of clean water, G50256. Occasionally
agitate the surface with a brush, G00215.
(e) Neutralization of the acidic fluid should be determined with litmus paper.
(f) Wipe dry with clean cotton cloth, G50316.
SUBTASK 51-00-57-350-001
(5) After removal of contamination, do the steps in CORROSION REMOVAL AND CONTROL -
CLEANING/PAINTING, PAGEBLOCK 51-21-31/701 when corrosion is found.
SUBTASK 51-00-57-370-001
(6) When corrosion is removed, do the steps in PAINT STRIPPING - CLEANING/PAINTING,
PAGEBLOCK 51-21-11/701, if necessary.
SUBTASK 51-00-57-600-001
(7) To apply the corrosion inhibiting compound, do the steps in CORROSION INHIBITING
COMPOUND - CLEANING/PAINTING, PAGEBLOCK 51-21-91/701.
SUBTASK 51-00-57-741-001
(8) Put the Airplane Back to Its Usual Condition.

ATA 51-00-60 - Section

1 tasks
51-00-60-200-801Preventive MaintenancePDF p. 89
Open original AMM PDF - page 89 ↗ 
2. Preventive Maintenance
A. General
(1) The use of airplanes for bulk shipment of live animals necessitates periodic cleaning and
deodorizing of the airplane. This provides an excellent opportunity to perform preventative
maintenance because the cargo linings and insulation blankets are removed.
B. References
Reference Title
25-52-00 P/B 601 CARGO COMPARTMENT - INSPECTION/CHECK
25-52-00-100-801 Cargo Compartment Cleaning (P/B 701)
25-80-00 P/B 401 INSULATION BLANKET - REMOVAL/INSTALLATION
51-21-91-620-802 Application of Corrosion Inhibiting Compound (P/B 701)
51-41 AIRFRAME DRAINAGE
53-05-03 P/B 201 FUSELAGE - STRUCTURAL INSPECTIONS - MAINTENANCE
PRACTICES
C. Procedure
SUBTASK 51-00-60-100-001
(1) When known animal waste spills have occurred, do this task: Cargo Compartment Cleaning,
TASK 25-52-00-100-801.
NOTE: Avoid spreading the spilled fluids through floor clearances.
51-00-60
SUBTASK 51-00-60-160-001
(2) Inspect the cargo compartments.
(a) CARGO COMPARTMENT - INSPECTION/CHECK, PAGEBLOCK 25-52-00/601.
(3) As necessary and when known spills have occurred, inspect the insulation blankets for
indication of liquid in the area of the spill.
(a) If blankets are dry and no indication of liquid is observed, then restore airplane.
(b) If blankets contain liquid then continue with further inspection:
1) Check the inner skin surface and fuselage structure for signs of corrosion, refer to
FUSELAGE - STRUCTURAL INSPECTIONS - MAINTENANCE PRACTICES,
PAGEBLOCK 53-05-03/201 for more details.
2) Make sure that all drains are unobstructed and that there are no trapped liquids
(AIRFRAME DRAINAGE, SECTION 51-41).
3) Replace contaminated insulation blankets (INSULATION BLANKET -
REMOVAL/INSTALLATION, PAGEBLOCK 25-80-00/401).
SUBTASK 51-00-60-600-001
(4) After the airplane has been cleaned and deodorized do these steps:
(a) Apply corrosion inhibiting compound to the inner skin surface and structure (Application
of Corrosion Inhibiting Compound, TASK 51-21-91-620-802) as necessary.
NOTE: In the fuselage crown area, airplanes used for livestock transportation will require
and receive more frequent preventive maintenance than recommended in
Volume 2.
(b) Install the equipment that you removed.

ATA 51-00-62 - Section

1 tasks
51-00-62-200-801Preventive MaintenancePDF p. 93
Open original AMM PDF - page 93 ↗ 
2. Preventive Maintenance
A. General
(1) The use of airplanes for shipment of fish requires periodic cleaning and deodorizing of the
airplane using locally available supplies. As this requires removal of cargo lining and insulation
blankets it also provides an excellent opportunity to perform corrosion preventive maintenance.
B. References
Reference Title
25-52-00-100-801 Cargo Compartment Cleaning (P/B 701)
25-80-00 INSULATION BLANKET
51-05-01 P/B 201 STRUCTURES - CORROSION INSPECTIONS -
MAINTENANCE PRACTICES
51-21-31 P/B 701 CORROSION REMOVAL AND CONTROL -
CLEANING/PAINTING
51-21-91-620-802 Application of Corrosion Inhibiting Compound (P/B 701)
51-41 AIRFRAME DRAINAGE
53-42-11-100-801 Clean the Passenger Cabin and Cargo Compartment Track
(P/B 701)
C. Procedure
SUBTASK 51-00-62-100-002
(1) When known spills have occurred, do this task: Cargo Compartment Cleaning,
TASK 25-52-00-100-801.
NOTE: Avoid spreading the spilled fluids through floor clearances.
SUBTASK 51-00-62-100-001
(2) Do this task to clean spills on seat tracks: Clean the Passenger Cabin and Cargo
Compartment Track, TASK 53-42-11-100-801.
SUBTASK 51-00-62-160-001
(3) As necessary, inspect:
(a) The inner skin surface and fuselage structure for signs of corrosion, refer to
STRUCTURES - CORROSION INSPECTIONS - MAINTENANCE PRACTICES,
PAGEBLOCK 51-05-01/201 and CORROSION REMOVAL AND CONTROL -
CLEANING/PAINTING, PAGEBLOCK 51-21-31/701 for more details.
(b) Make sure that all drains are unobstructed and that there are no trapped liquids
(AIRFRAME DRAINAGE, SECTION 51-41).
(c) Condition of insulation blankets (INSULATION BLANKET , SUBJECT 25-80-00).
1) Replace soaked insulation blankets.
SUBTASK 51-00-62-600-001
(4) After the airplane has been cleaned and deodorized do these steps:
51-00-62
(a) Apply corrosion inhibiting compound to the inner skin surface and structure (Application
of Corrosion Inhibiting Compound, TASK 51-21-91-620-802) as necessary.
(b) Install the equipment that you removed.

ATA 51-05-01 - Section

13 tasks
51-05-01-210-801737-6789 Basic Task DescriptionPDF p. 95
Open original AMM PDF - page 95 ↗ 
1. 737-6789 Basic Task Description
NOTE: This procedure is a scheduled maintenance task.
A. References
Reference Title
51-00-57 P/B 201 PROCEDURE AFTER ACID SPILLS (INCLUDING VACUUM
WASTE SYSTEM SPILLS/LEAKS) - MAINTENANCE
PRACTICES
51-00-58 STANDARD TREATMENT METHODS
B. Consumable Materials
Reference Description Specification
C00174 Compound - Corrosion Preventive, Solvent
Cutback, Cold Application
MIL-PRF-16173
(Supersedes
MIL-C-16173)
C00915 Compound - Organic Corrosion Inhibiting,
Advanced
BMS3-29
G00009 Compound - Organic Corrosion Inhibiting BMS3-23
C. CPCP Basic Task
NOTE: Corrosion that was the result of a vacuum waste system spill/leak or other acidic spill, must
be cleaned and neutralized (refer to PROCEDURE AFTER ACID SPILLS (INCLUDING
VACUUM WASTE SYSTEM SPILLS/LEAKS) - MAINTENANCE PRACTICES,
PAGEBLOCK 51-00-57/201) in all areas where the spill/leak has made contact with the
structure. Make sure to clean and neutralize regions where the material can penetrate into
structural joints. Also, make sure that the cause of the spill/leak is corrected.
SUBTASK 51-05-01-210-022
(1) CPCP Basic T ask Item 1 is not applicable.
SUBTASK 51-05-01-210-133
(2) Do the CPCP Basic T ask item 2 as follows:
(a) Prior to inspection clean the area as required to accomplish CPCP Basic T ask Item 3. It is
not necessary to remove normal amounts of sealant/leveling compound unless it has
deteriorated to the point where moisture can penetrate down to the metal. A light uniform
film of Corrosion Inhibiting Compound (CIC) that has not accumulated dirt or debris, will
normally allow adequate inspection of the structure without removal. CIC may require
removal if there are multiple layers and/or accumulations of dirt or debris.
SUBTASK 51-05-01-210-024
(3) Do the CPCP Basic T ask Item 3 as follows:
(a) Visually inspect all structure listed in the task description. The inspection method is as
specified in each task description. Use Additional non-destructive inspections or visual
inspections following partial disassembly if there are indications of hidden corrosion, such
as bulging skins or corrosion running into splices, or under fittings, etc. In the task area,
check the integrity of any sealant/leveling compound to determine if removal is required,
and any corrosion inhibiting compound, particularly at faying surfaces, to determine if
additional application is required per CPCP Basic T ask Item 6.
51-05-01
SUBTASK 51-05-01-210-025
(4) Do the CPCP Basic T ask item 4 as follows:
(a) Remove all corrosion, evaluate damage and repair or replace all discrepant structure as
required, including application of protective finishes per STANDARD TREATMENT
METHODS, SUBJECT 51-00-58, or 737 Structural Repair Manual (SRM) D634A200,
(-600), D634A201 (-700), D634A210 (-800), D634A211(-900), D634A333 (BBJ), or
related service bulletin, as appropriate. Surface oxidation of ferrous metal fasteners may
be handled by normal or existing maintenance practices.
SUBTASK 51-05-01-210-026
(5) CPCP Basic T ask Item 5 is not applicable.
SUBTASK 51-05-01-210-027
(6) Do the CPCP Basic T ask item 6 as follows:
(a) Apply suitable approved water displacing / anti-corrosion compound as necessary.
1) The minimum requirement for all areas (except as noted in CPCP Basic T ask 6 (a)
3)) is single coat of water displacing / anti-corrosion compound that penetrates
faying surfaces and displaces moisture, e.g. a single coat of compound, C00915
BMS 3-29 or corrosion inhibiting compound, G00009 BMS 3-23, where the initial or
previous coat has been disturbed or removed.
2) Not applicable
3) List of areas / items where water displacing/anti-corrosion compounds should not be
applied:
Water displacing / anti-corrosion compounds should not be applied in the following
areas:
• Cables, pulleys, wiring, plastics, elastomers, oxygen systems.
• Lubricated or T eflon surfaces (E.g. greased joints, sealed bearings).
• Over compound, C00174 Cosmoline 1058 (or Equivalent per MIL-C-16173 Grade
1).
• Adjacent to tears / holes in insulation blankets (water repelling characteristics are
diminished).
• Areas with electrical arc potential.
• Interior materials, including cargo liners (change of flammability properties).
• Engine strut cavities, cowling panels or pod.
• APU or any structure in direct contact with the APU.
• Fiber-glass ducts where temperature exceeds 220 degrees F .
• Fuel tank wet areas.
• Selected areas noted in baseline program.
SUBTASK 51-05-01-210-028
(7) CPCP Basic T ask Item 7 is not applicable.

3. 737-6789 Basic Task Description
NOTE: This procedure is a scheduled maintenance task.
A. References
Reference Title
51-00-58 STANDARD TREATMENT METHODS
B. CPCP Basic Task
SUBTASK 51-05-01-210-036
(1) Do the CPCP Basic T ask Item 1 as follows:
(a) Remove all systems, equipment and interior furnishings, etc. (e.g. toilets, galleys, linings,

SUBTASK 51-05-01-210-037
(2) Do the CPCP Basic T ask item 2 as follows:
(a) Prior to inspection clean the area as required to accomplish CPCP Basic T ask Item 3. It is
not necessary to remove normal amounts of sealant/leveling compound unless it has
deteriorated to the point where moisture can penetrate down to the metal. A light uniform
film of Corrosion Inhibiting Compound (CIC) that has not accumulated dirt or debris, will
normally allow adequate inspection of the structure without removal. CIC may require
removal if there are multiple layers and/or accumulations of dirt or debris.
SUBTASK 51-05-01-210-038
(3) Do the CPCP Basic T ask Item 3 as follows:
(a) Visually inspect all structure listed in the task description. The inspection method is as
specified in each task description. Use Additional non-destructive inspections or visual
inspections following partial disassembly if there are indications of hidden corrosion, such
as bulging skins or corrosion running into splices, or under fittings, etc. In the task area,
check the integrity of any sealant/leveling compound to determine if removal is required,
and any corrosion inhibiting compound, particularly at faying surfaces, to determine if
additional application is required per CPCP Basic T ask Item 6.
SUBTASK 51-05-01-210-039
(4) Do the CPCP Basic T ask item 4 as follows:
(a) Remove all corrosion, evaluate damage and repair or replace all discrepant structure as
required, including application of protective finishes per STANDARD TREATMENT
METHODS, SUBJECT 51-00-58, or 737 Structural Repair Manual (SRM) D634A200,
(-600), D634A201 (-700), D634A210 (-800), D634A211(-900), D634A333 (BBJ), or
related service bulletin, as appropriate. Surface oxidation of ferrous metal fasteners may
be handled by normal or existing maintenance practices.
SUBTASK 51-05-01-210-040
(5) CPCP Basic T ask Item 5 is not applicable.
SUBTASK 51-05-01-210-110
(6) Do the CPCP Basic T ask Item 6 (Not Applicable)
SUBTASK 51-05-01-210-042
(7) CPCP Basic T ask Item 7 is not applicable.

SUBTASK 51-05-01-210-043
(1) Do the CPCP Basic T ask Item 1 as follows:
(a) Remove all systems, equipment and interior furnishings, etc. (e.g. toilets, galleys, linings,
installation) as necessary to accomplish CPCP Basic T ask Item 3. It is not necessary to
remove bushings unless specified in the T ask Description, or if there is an indication of
corrosion, or that the bushing has migrated.
SUBTASK 51-05-01-210-044
(2) Do the CPCP Basic T ask item 2 as follows:
(a) Prior to inspection clean the area as required to accomplish CPCP Basic T ask Item 3. It is
not necessary to remove normal amounts of sealant/leveling compound unless it has
deteriorated to the point where moisture can penetrate down to the metal. A light uniform

SUBTASK 51-05-01-210-046
(4) Do the CPCP Basic T ask item 4 as follows:
(a) Remove all corrosion, evaluate damage and repair or replace all discrepant structure as
required, including application of protective finishes per STANDARD TREATMENT
METHODS, SUBJECT 51-00-58, or 737 Structural Repair Manual (SRM) D634A200,
(-600), D634A201 (-700), D634A210 (-800), D634A211(-900), D634A333 (BBJ), or
related service bulletin, as appropriate. Surface oxidation of ferrous metal fasteners may
be handled by normal or existing maintenance practices.
SUBTASK 51-05-01-210-111
(5) Do the CPCP Basic T ask Item 5 as follows:
(a) Clear any blocked holes or gaps that may hinder drainage, as applicable.
SUBTASK 51-05-01-210-048
(6) Do the CPCP Basic T ask item 6 as follows:
(a) Apply suitable approved water displacing / anti-corrosion compound as necessary.
51-05-01
1) The minimum requirement for all areas (except as noted in CPCP Basic T ask 6 (a)
3)) is single coat of water displacing / anti-corrosion compound that penetrates
faying surfaces and displaces moisture, e.g. a single coat of compound, C00915
BMS 3-29 or corrosion inhibiting compound, G00009 BMS 3-23, where the initial or
previous coat has been disturbed or removed.
2) Not applicable
3) List of areas / items where water displacing/anti-corrosion compounds should not be
applied:
Water displacing / anti-corrosion compounds should not be applied in the following
areas:
• Cables, pulleys, wiring, plastics, elastomers, oxygen systems.
• Lubricated or T eflon surfaces (E.g. greased joints, sealed bearings).
• Over compound, C00174 Cosmoline 1058 (or Equivalent per MIL-C-16173 Grade
1).
• Adjacent to tears / holes in insulation blankets (water repelling characteristics are
diminished).
• Areas with electrical arc potential.
• Interior materials, including cargo liners (change of flammability properties).
• Engine strut cavities, cowling panels or pod.
• APU or any structure in direct contact with the APU.
• Fiber-glass ducts where temperature exceeds 220 degrees F .
• Fuel tank wet areas.
• Selected areas noted in baseline program.
SUBTASK 51-05-01-210-049
(7) CPCP Basic T ask Item 7 is not applicable.

SUBTASK 51-05-01-210-093
(2) Do the CPCP Basic T ask item 2 as follows:
(a) Prior to inspection clean the area as required to accomplish CPCP Basic T ask Item 3. It is
not necessary to remove normal amounts of sealant/leveling compound unless it has
deteriorated to the point where moisture can penetrate down to the metal. A light uniform
film of Corrosion Inhibiting Compound (CIC) that has not accumulated dirt or debris, will
normally allow adequate inspection of the structure without removal. CIC may require
removal if there are multiple layers and/or accumulations of dirt or debris.
SUBTASK 51-05-01-210-094
(3) Do the CPCP Basic T ask Item 3 as follows:
(a) Visually inspect all structure listed in the task description. The inspection method is as
specified in each task description. Use Additional non-destructive inspections or visual
inspections following partial disassembly if there are indications of hidden corrosion, such
as bulging skins or corrosion running into splices, or under fittings, etc. In the task area,
check the integrity of any sealant/leveling compound to determine if removal is required,
and any corrosion inhibiting compound, particularly at faying surfaces, to determine if
additional application is required per CPCP Basic T ask Item 6.
SUBTASK 51-05-01-210-095
(4) Do the CPCP Basic T ask item 4 as follows:
(a) Remove all corrosion, evaluate damage and repair or replace all discrepant structure as
required, including application of protective finishes per STANDARD TREATMENT
METHODS, SUBJECT 51-00-58, or 737 Structural Repair Manual (SRM) D634A200,
(-600), D634A201 (-700), D634A210 (-800), D634A211(-900), D634A333 (BBJ), or
related service bulletin, as appropriate. Surface oxidation of ferrous metal fasteners may
be handled by normal or existing maintenance practices.
SUBTASK 51-05-01-210-096
(5) CPCP Basic T ask Item 5 is not applicable.
51-05-01
SUBTASK 51-05-01-210-118
(6) Do the CPCP Basic T ask Item 6 (Not Applicable)
SUBTASK 51-05-01-210-098
(7) Do the CPCP Basic T ask Item 7 as follows:
(a) Dry wet insulation blankets prior to re-installing, or replace with new, as applicable.

(5) CPCP Basic T ask Item 5 is not applicable.
SUBTASK 51-05-01-210-124
(6) Do the CPCP Basic T ask item 6 as follows:
(a) Apply suitable approved water displacing / anti-corrosion compound as necessary.
1) The minimum requirement for all areas (except as noted in CPCP Basic T ask 6 (a)
3)) is single coat of water displacing / anti-corrosion compound that penetrates
faying surfaces and displaces moisture, e.g. a single coat of compound, C00915
BMS 3-29 or corrosion inhibiting compound, G00009 BMS 3-23, where the initial or
previous coat has been disturbed or removed.
2) Not applicable
3) List of areas / items where water displacing/anti-corrosion compounds should not be
applied:
Water displacing / anti-corrosion compounds should not be applied in the following
areas:
• Cables, pulleys, wiring, plastics, elastomers, oxygen systems.
• Lubricated or T eflon surfaces (E.g. greased joints, sealed bearings).
• Over compound, C00174 Cosmoline 1058 (or Equivalent per MIL-C-16173 Grade
1).
• Adjacent to tears / holes in insulation blankets (water repelling characteristics are
diminished).
• Areas with electrical arc potential.
• Interior materials, including cargo liners (change of flammability properties).
• Engine strut cavities, cowling panels or pod.
• APU or any structure in direct contact with the APU.
• Fiber-glass ducts where temperature exceeds 220 degrees F .
• Fuel tank wet areas.
• Selected areas noted in baseline program.
SUBTASK 51-05-01-210-125
(7) CPCP Basic T ask Item 7 is not applicable.

C. Prepare the surface
SUBTASK 51-21-00-100-001
(1) Before you apply finish to a surface, you must correctly prepare the surface, as follows:
CAUTION
DO NOT USE PAINT STRIPPER ON COMPOSITE MATERIALS. IF YOU
USE PAINT STRIPPERS ON COMPOSITE MATERIALS, THE
COMPOSITE MATERIALS WILL BE STRUCTURALLY DAMAGED.
(a) To remove the damaged finish, do this task: Paint Stripping, TASK 51-21-11-150-801.
51-21-00
1) Use approved compounds to remove the damaged finishes.
a) Solvents are used to remove paint from small areas.
b) Stripping Compounds are used to remove paint from large areas or
disassembled parts.
c) Mechanical Strippers are used to remove paint from composite materials.
(b) To remove the corrosion, do one of the following tasks:
1) for Aluminum and Aluminum Alloys, do this task: Removal and Control of Corrosion
for Aluminum and Aluminum Alloys, TASK 51-21-31-350-801.
2) for Magnesium Alloys, do this task: Removal and Control of Corrosion for
Magnesium Alloys, TASK 51-21-31-350-802.
3) for Carbon Steel, do this task: Removal and Control of Corrosion for Carbon Steel,
TASK 51-21-31-350-803.
4) for Stainless Steel and Nickel-Chromium Alloys, do this task: Removal and Control
of Corrosion for Stainless Steel and Nickel-Chromium Alloys,
TASK 51-21-31-350-804.
5) for Titanium Alloys, do this task: Removal and Control of Corrosion for Titanium
Alloys, TASK 51-21-31-350-805.
6) for Plated or Phosphated Surfaces, do this task: Removal and Control of Corrosion
for Plated or Phosphated Surfaces, TASK 51-21-31-350-806.
7) Listed below are more than one approved method of corrosion removal:
a) chemical treatments
b) hand sanding with abrasive paper or metal wool
c) mechanical sanding with abrasive mats
d) dry abrasive blasting.
8) The procedures you use must agree with the type of material and the quantity of the
corrosion.
(c) Clean the surface with one or more of these approved methods:
NOTE: To get satisfactory results, you must make sure the surface is fully clean before
you apply paints or finishes.
1) Use solvent, B00184, or
2) Use cleaner, B00402, or
NOTE: The following cleaners are optional to cleaner, B00402: Cee Bee 280
cleaner, B00005, Oakite 204 cleaner, B00008 and Calla 301A cleaner,
B00015.
51-21-00
CAUTION
BE CAREFUL WHEN YOU USE ABRADING MATERIAL. ABRADING
MATERIAL CAN EXPOSE OR DAMAGE FIBERGLASS, ARAMID, OR
GRAPHITE FIBERS. DAMAGED OR EXPOSED FIBERS WILL CAUSE
THE COMPONENT TO BE REJECTED.
3) Use 150 grit or finer abrasive, G02088 by hand.
NOTE: You can also do dry abrasive blasting with abrasive, G02222 if necessary.
NOTE: For composites, the recommended abrasive is No. 240 Scotch-Brite. Use of
alternative media blasting procedures for finish removal is structurally
satisfactory. The procedure must agree with the requirements given in
D6–55564, “Requirements for Alternative Paint Stripping Processes” or
SAE MA4872 “Paint Stripping of Commercial Aircraft - Evaluation of
Materials and Processes.” Refer to SOPM 20–30–02, or BAC5725
“Stripping Organic Materials” for more information on paint stripping with
blast media.
4) Scrub the area thoroughly with a slurry of pumice abrasive, B00166 powder 4F and
water.
(d) Apply a conversion coating (on some surfaces).
1) Some conversion coatings are applied to make a protective layer on metal surfaces
to prevent damage.
2) The layer is formed by a chemical procedure which will change the surface metal
into an oxide layer.
3) Different conversion coating applications are used with different metals.
4) Chromic acid or an equivalent oxidizing agent will make a thin tight layer and
prevent corrosion.

Reference Description Specification

Wiper (Cheesecloth, Gauze)
AMS3819 Class 1
Grade A or B Form 1

B. Procedure
SUBTASK 51-21-00-210-003
(1) Examine the condition of the aluminum surface:
(a) Make sure that all areas that are to be electrically bonded are clean.
(b) Remove oil, grease, dirt, and paint from the surface.
(c) Make sure you remove all corrosion material from the surfaces or parts, if necessary.
1) Failure to remove all corrosion material can permit corrosion to start again.
2) For limits of corrosion removal, refer to the Structural Repair Manual.
SUBTASK 51-21-00-160-004
(2) Do the abrasive cleaning for the aluminum surface as follows:
(a) Select and use one of the abrasive materials that follow:
1) abrasive mat, G00251.
2) abrasive cloth, B00102.
3) 240 grit or finer abrasive paper, G50077.
(b) Sand the surface with the abrasive in a circular motion.
1) Sand the surface until all surface finishes are removed.
2) Sand the surface until a bright aluminum bonding surface is visible throughout the
required area.
SUBTASK 51-21-00-160-005
(3) Do the final solvent cleaning for the aluminum bonding surface as follows:
(a) Select one of the solvents that follow:
1) alcohol, B00067
2) solvent, B00148.
3) solvent, B00062
4) solvent, B00666.
5) solvent, B00184.
6) alcohol, B00130
7) solvent, B01054
8) solvent, B50085
(b) Add the solvent to a cotton wiper, G00034.
(c) Rub surface to be cleaned with the solvent soaked wiper.
1) Replace the dirty wiper for a clean one if necessary.
51-21-00
(d) Immediately wipe dry with a clean, dry wiper.
1) Do not allow solvent to evaporate to dryness.
NOTE: It may be necessary to remove residue with a second dry wipe when using
slow-evaporating solvents.
(e) Do the steps again until the wiper shows no soil.

51-21-11-370-801Prepare for Paint StrippingPDF p. 123
Open original AMM PDF - page 123 ↗ 
2. Prepare for Paint Stripping
A. General
(1) The purpose of alkaline cleaning is to prepare the painted surfaces of the aircraft for paint
stripping. Paint strippers can be more effective if the aircraft surface is cleaned prior to paint
stripping.
51-21-11
CAUTION
DO NOT USE RESTRICTED-USE PAINT STRIPPERS ON HIGH-STRENGTH
STEEL, OR MAGNESIUM COMPONENTS. THESE STRIPPERS WILL CAUSE
DAMAGE TO COMPONENTS WITH THESE MATERIALS.
(2) Restricted-use paint strippers are for removing organic finishes from metallic substrates but
cannot be used on high-strength steel or magnesium.
B. References

E. Prepare for Paint Stripping
SUBTASK 51-21-11-580-001
(1) Ground the airplane (AIRPLANE GROUNDING - MAINTENANCE PRACTICES,
PAGEBLOCK 20-40-11/201).
SUBTASK 51-21-11-020-001
(2) Remove windshield wipers, as required.
SUBTASK 51-21-11-950-002
(3) Apply Scotch Flatback Masking T ape 250, G00270, or Scotch General Purpose Masking T ape
234, A50454, to areas that you will not strip, or to areas that can be damaged easily by the
paint strippers.
(a) Pitot static probes and ports.
1) Attach a "PITOT PROBES COVERED" tag, G02447, and "STATIC PORTS
COVERED" tag, G02444, to the left control wheel in the flight deck.
(b) Total Air T emperature (TAT) probe.
(c) Cooling air exhaust duct.
(d) Windshield wiper mounts.
(e) Cargo door and entry door hinges and thresholds.
(f) Mask all gaps containing mechanical (silicone rubber) seals.
NOTE: This is to prevent seal degradation.
(g) Mask unsealed Section 48 seams and other open joints that cannot be thoroughly rinsed.
(h) Put the applicable tape or covers on lubricated parts, rubber parts, laminated plastics,
fiberglass, aerodynamic smoother, and metal-bonded edges.
(i) Apply tape a minimum of 1 in. (25 mm) wide on the skin to all access doors, fairings, wing
Leading Edge (LE)s and Trailing Edge (TE)s.
(j) Apply tape on the drain holes and vents.
(k) Apply tape on the pressurized drain valves.
(l) Acrylic parts.
1) Do not use Scotch Flatback Masking T ape 250, G00270, on acrylic surfaces.
51-21-11
(m) Glass parts.
1) Do not use Scotch Flatback Masking T ape 250, G00270, on glass surfaces.
SUBTASK 51-21-11-950-052
(4) If you will use restricted strippers (Table 701), do the steps that follow:
(a) Mask beyond the areas of sealant that follow:
NOTE: This is to prevent penetration to the joint.
1) Lap joint sealant.
2) Circumferential joint sealant.
3) Aerodynamic window sealants.
(b) Double-mask windows, high-strength steel cargo doors and hinges.
1) Apply a second layer of aluminum tape that is offset from the initial application of
tape by 50%.
SUBTASK 51-21-11-840-001
(5) Install the Angle of Attack (AOA) sensor angle of attack vane cover, COM-2499
(recommended), or AOA vane protective cover, SPL-14189 (alternate).
SUBTASK 51-21-11-950-053
(6) Mask the AOA sensors.
(a) Attach a red tag with wire to the left control wheel in the flight deck.
1) Write “AOA SENSORS COVERED” on the tag.
(b) Cover the AOA sensors with PG-777 solvent-resistant masking tape.
1) Apply PG-777 over the blade, plate, and blade-to-plate gap (Figure 703).
NOTE: This is to allow easier removal of 3M 425 Aluminum Foil T ape, G00291.
2) Cover the PG-777 solvent-resistant masking tape with 3M 425 Aluminum Foil T ape,
G00291.
a) Shingle the aluminum foil tape from the blade-to-plate gap up to the blade tip
until all of the masking tape is covered.
SUBTASK 51-21-11-950-054
(7) Put applicable plastic coated paper, G00365, covers on areas which are easily damaged by
strippers.
NOTE: You can use equivalent plastic sheeting that is resistant to paint strippers.
(a) Use the applicable material below to protect the surface:
1) Paper masking tape.
2) Plastic-coated paper.
3) Plastic sheeting.
4) Wrapping paper, chemically neutral.
(b) Cover the areas that follow with the applicable cover:
1) Wings.
a) Put the flaps in the full up position (Retract the Trailing Edge Flaps,
TASK 27-51-00-860-804, Leading Edge Flaps and Slats Retraction,
TASK 27-81-00-860-804).
2) Tires.
51-21-11
3) Engine and Auxiliary Power Unit (APU) inlets and exhaust.
4) Ram air inlets.
SUBTASK 51-21-11-950-003
(8) Cover the passenger and flight deck windows with the steps that follow (Figure 702):
(a) Use Protex 10VS tape, G50394, Protex 20V tape, G00139, pre-cut plastic adhesive
window mask paper (8216-2L), or Polyethylene-coated Kraft paper.
1) Make sure that the polyethylene-coated Kraft paper is at least 0.001 in. (0.025 mm)
thick.
2) Make sure that the polyethylene side is exposed.
(b) Use masking tape, G51270, or 3M 5501A tape, G51487, to attach the paper to the
airplane surface.
1) Use a hand series squeegee applicator, STD-10211, to make sure the tape adheres
to the surface.
(c) Apply Scotch 427 Aluminum Foil T ape, G50737, over the masking tape.
1) Make sure that the aluminum foil tape extends beyond the window onto the frame or
forging.
2) Use a hand series squeegee applicator, STD-10211, to make sure the tape adheres
to the surface.
(d) Apply an aluminum cover to the windows.
1) Make sure that the aluminum cover is 0.01 in. (0.25 mm) to 0.04 in. (1.02 mm) thick.
2) Use 2 in. (51 mm) wide masking tape, G51270, or 3M 5501A tape, G51487, to
attach the aluminum cover.
a) Apply the masking tape in strips that are shingled (bottom to top in overlapping
layers, see Figure 702).
NOTE: This will help to stop fluids from seeping under the tape.
3) Put Scotch 427 Aluminum Foil T ape, G50737, on top of the masking tape.
(e) If you will strip paint in an area within 2 in. (51 mm) of a window pane, or if you will use a
restricted use paint stripper, do the steps that follow:
1) Double-mask the windows.
2) Do the paint stripping procedure twice.
3) See Paint Stripping, TASK 51-21-11-150-801, for a list of restricted use paint
strippers.
SUBTASK 51-21-11-950-004
CAUTION
DO NOT USE RESTRICTED-USE PAINT STRIPPERS ON HIGH-STRENGTH
STEEL, OR MAGNESIUM COMPONENTS. THESE STRIPPERS WILL CAUSE
DAMAGE TO COMPONENTS WITH THESE MATERIALS.
(9) Drape-mask landing gear and wheel wells.
NOTE: Make sure to lay down the polyethylene before you roll the airplane into the hangar.
This will make sure the entire wheels and tires are masked.
(a) Use polyethylene film, G00252.
(b) Use PG-777 masking tape, G51270, to attach the polyethylene film.
1) Make sure that the Polyken tape does not come into contact with the aircraft.
51-21-11
(c) Use Scotch 427 Aluminum Foil T ape, G50737, on top of the masking tape and Polyken
tape.
1) Make sure that the aluminum tape is 2 in. (51 mm) wide.
(d) Use a hand series squeegee applicator, STD-10211, to make sure that the tape adheres
to the surface.
SUBTASK 51-21-11-950-007
(10) Mask the composite areas listed below using the steps that follow if they will not be stripped of
paint.
(a) Composite surfaces (Figure 701):
1) Nose radome
2) All antennas
3) Nose landing gear doors
4) Thrust reversers
5) Strut fairings
6) Wing-to-body fairings
7) Flap track canoe fairings
8) Strakelet and wing LE panels
9) Wing TE panels
10) Spoilers
11) Flaps
12) Ailerons
13) Winglets
14) Dorsal fin
15) Vertical fin tip and close-out panels rudder
16) Stabilizer tip and panels
17) Elevators
18) T ail cone.
(b) Cover the composite surface with Polyethylene Coated Kraft paper.
1) Make sure that the paper is at least 0.001 in. (0.025 mm) thick.
2) Make sure you put the paper such that the polyethylene side is exposed.
3) Use masking tape, G51270, or 3M 5501A tape, G51487, to attach the paper to the
airplane surface.
a) Use a hand series squeegee applicator, STD-10211, to make sure the tape
adheres to the surface.
(c) Cover the polyethylene-coated Kraft paper with polyethylene film, G00252.
1) Use masking tape, G51270, or 3M 5501A tape, G51487, to attach the film to the
polyethylene-coated Kraft paper.
a) Use a hand series squeegee applicator, STD-10211, to make sure the tape
adheres to the surface.
(d) Use Scotch 427 Aluminum Foil T ape, G50737, or 3M 425 Aluminum Foil T ape, G00291,
over the second layer of tape, G02321.
51-21-11
1) Make sure that the aluminum tape is at least 2 in. (51 mm) wide.
2) Use a hand series squeegee applicator, STD-10211, to make sure the tape adheres
to the surface.
3) Make sure the aluminum tape completely covers the masking tape.
NOTE: This is to protect it from chemical paint strippers.
a) Extend the aluminum tape over the masking tape to protect it from chemical
paint strippers.
SUBTASK 51-21-11-950-008
(11) Mask the lights and light bases listed below using the steps that follow:
(a) Directional and warning fuselage lights.
(b) Logo illumination lights.
(c) Cover the lights and light bases with Scotch 427 Aluminum Foil T ape, G50737, or 3M 425
Aluminum Foil T ape, G00291.
1) Use a hand series squeegee applicator, STD-10211, to make sure the tape adheres
to the surface.
(d) If the light is larger than the width of the aluminum foil tape, do the steps that follow:
1) Cover the surface with polyethylene film.
a) Make sure that the polyethylene film is 4.0 mils (0.1 mm) to 7.0 mils (0.2 mm)
thick and fire retardant.
2) Use Scotch 427 Aluminum Foil T ape, G50737, or 3M 425 Aluminum Foil T ape,
G00291, to attach the film to the surface.
SUBTASK 51-21-11-160-096
(12) Do the steps that follow to clean the area:
(a) Remove the loose dirt, grease, and oil with MIL-PRF-680 degreasing solvent, B00074, or
aliphatic naphtha solvent, B00083.
NOTE: Be careful to not put solvent on a larger area than is necessary.
(b) Clean the surface with a cotton wiper, G00034, paint brush, STD-1080, or a sponge,
G00116, that is moist with MIL-PRF-680 degreasing solvent, B00074, or aliphatic
naphtha solvent, B00083.
1) Rub the surface with a clean cotton wiper, G00034.
(c) Remove all unwanted solvent.
(d) Let the surface dry.
SUBTASK 51-21-11-160-097
(13) Do the steps that follow to clean the surface again:
(a) Use a spray bottle to apply the aliphatic naphtha solvent, B00083, on the surface.
(b) Clean the surface with a clean cotton wiper, G00034, until you remove all large pieces of
soil.
1) Use a soft bristle brush, G00215, to clean the surface, if necessary.
(c) Clean the surface again with a new cotton wiper, G00034, until you can see no signs of
soil on the cheesecloth.
NOTE: It will be necessary to change the cheesecloth frequently when you clean the
surface.
51-21-11
(d) Dry the area with a clean cotton wiper, G00034.
1) Make sure that you do not let drops of solvent dry on the surface.
SUBTASK 51-21-11-580-002
(14) Dry the aircraft.
(a) If the actuator components or bearings were cleaned, lubricate those components as
necessary.
1) Make sure to lubricate the actuator components or bearings outside the paint
hangar.
NOTE: This is to avoid paint contamination.
51-21-11-150-801Paint StrippingPDF p. 134

3. Paint Stripping
A. General
(1) This task has the following steps:
(a) Brush-on or paint-on paint stripping
(b) A removal of the epoxy adhesives and adhesive primers
(c) A removal of the rain erosion coating from fiberglass
(d) Selection of paint strippers.
CAUTION
DO NOT LET PAINT STRIPPERS TOUCH FIBREGLASS, ACRYLIC WINDOWS,
SEALANT FILLETS, TEFLON LINED SELF-LUBRICATED BEARINGS,
ELECTRICAL TERMINAL PLUGS, NYLON COATED WIRES AND NYLON
BUSHINGS. THE CHEMICAL COMPOUNDS IN THE PAINT STRIPPERS WILL
CAUSE DAMAGE TO THE COMPONENTS.
(2) Before you clean, you must protect the surfaces and parts which are easily damaged by paint
strippers.
(a) Make sure that the paint strippers do not fall on the joints.
(b) Do not use paint strippers in the fuel tanks.
(3) Before you remove the paint with the stripping procedure, make sure that the surface is clean
and dry.
(a) Make sure that the ambient temperature is between 50°F (10°C) and 100°F (38°C).
1) Do not use the stripping procedure in hot or wet weather.
(b) Make sure that the paint stripping equipment are fully clean of chemical residue.
NOTE: Refer to equipment manufacturer's instructions to clean paint stripping
equipment.
CAUTION
DO NOT USE PAINT STRIPPER ON COMPOSITE MATERIALS. PAINT
STRIPPERS CAN CAUSE DAMAGE TO THE STRUCTURE OF THE PART .
(4) Brush-on or spray-on stripping methods with paint strippers or solvents can be used on metal
surfaces.
(a) You can use a pressure sprayer gun, STD-447, or a brush to apply the paint strippers.
1) The brush-on procedure is frequently used for line maintenance.
CAUTION
DO NOT USE THE SAME PUMPS AND HOSES WHEN MIXING DRUMS
OF DIFFERENT PAINT STRIPPERS THROUGH RECIRCULATION.
MIXING AND CROSS-CONTAMINATION CAN BE HIGHLY CORROSIVE
TO METAL SURFACES. THIS CAN CAUSE SKIN PITTING DAMAGE.
(b) Make sure that the components in the paint stripper are fully mixed.
1) Do not use the same pumps and hoses when drums of different paint strippers are
mixed through recirculation.
a) Use dedicated pump and hose for different paint stripper type when drums of
paint striper are mixed through recirculation.
51-21-11
2) Keep the container of paint stripper fully closed when it is not being used.
CAUTION
DO NOT APPLY ACID-ACTIVATED STRIPPER ONTO ANY AIRPLANE
SURFACE THAT HAS NOT HAD THE PEROXIDE-ACTIVATED STRIPPER
COMPLETELY AND THOROUGHLY RINSED OFF WITH WATER AND
VICE-VERSA. IF YOU MIX AND CROSS-CONTAMINATE, IT CAN BE
HIGHLY CORROSIVE TO METAL SURFACES. THIS CAN CAUSE SKIN
PITTING DAMAGE.
(c) Do not mix strippers of different manufacturers.
1) Make sure that airplane is completely and thoroughly rinsed down with water to
remove all residue of the initial paint stripper when using different paint stripper.
2) Do not apply acid-activated stripper onto any airplane surface that has not had the
peroxide-activated stripper completely and thoroughly rinsed off with water and
vise-versa.
NOTE: If necessary, a mild alkaline cleaner solution can be used to neutralize the
hydrogen-peroxide paint based stripper. You can use Super Bee 300LF
cleaner, B50394 [CP2615] or other equivalent cleaners approved for aircraft
exteriors to remove paint and stripper residue.
(d) Strippers that you apply with a brush or with a spray gun contain agents that make a
solution thick.
1) Apply these strippers on the vertical and horizontal surfaces.
(e) Strippers that you apply with a cloth are used to remove temporary finishes, waxes, and
oversprays.
(f) specifications BAC5725 and BAC5771 provide procedures for paint stripping.
(g) Restricted strippers are not compatible with high-strength steel and magnesium. When
you use restricted strippers, make sure that all high-strength steel and magnesium parts
are masked.
(h) Acid strippers will discolor clad aluminum.
(i) You can usually use a solvent to remove small quantities of paint.
(j) Do not use paint strippers on the surfaces that follow:
NOTE: Only use approved solvents on these materials.
1) Plastic laminates
2) Fiberglass
3) Aerodynamic smoother
4) Edges of metal bonds
5) Rubber parts
6) Lubricated parts
7) Composite surfaces or parts.
(5) Abrasive stripping methods can be used on the surfaces that follow:
(a) Ferrous Alloys
(b) Titanium
(c) Titanium Alloys
(d) Titanium-Cadmium
51-21-11
(e) Nickel
(f) Nickel Alloys
(g) Magnesium Alloys
(h) Magnesium
(i) Aluminum Alloys
(j) Cadmium
(k) Zinc-Nickel
(l) Chromium
(m) Copper-Plating
(n) Composite Parts.

tools shown are alternates to each other within the same airplane series. Tool part numbers
that are replaced or non-procurable are preceded by "Opt:", which stands for Optional.

D. Brush-on or Spray Paint Stripping
SUBTASK 51-21-11-150-022
(1) Prepare the surface for the paint stripping, do this task: Prepare for Paint Stripping,
TASK 51-21-11-370-801.
NOTE: The purpose of alkaline cleaning is to prepare the painted surfaces of the aircraft for
paint stripping. Washing the aircraft may mitigate interactions between chemical
strippers and enviormental contaminants such as salt. Paint strippers will be more
effective if the aircraft surface is cleaned prior to paint stripping.
SUBTASK 51-21-11-150-001
WARNING
MANY ORGANIC FINISH STRIPPERS ARE FLAMMABLE, TOXIC OR
CORROSIVE. REVIEW THE RELEVANT FIRE, INDUSTRIAL, HYGIENE, AND
SAFETY PROCEDURES FOR SAFE OPERATION. AVOID PROLONGED OR
REPEATED BREATHING OF THE STRIPPER AND SOLVENT VAPORS. WEAR
PROTECTIVE CLOTHING AND GOGGLES OR PLASTIC FACE SHIELDS.
AVOID CONTACT WITH SKIN AND EYES. WASH IMMEDIATELY WITH WATER
AND SEEK MEDICAL ATTENTION.
(2) Do the paint stripping.
NOTE: Strip only an area that can be worked at one time.
(a) Refer to Table 701 for the approved stripping materials.
(b) Make sure that you double-strip windows.
CAUTION
DO NOT APPLY ACID-ACTIVATED STRIPPER ONTO ANY AIRPLANE
SURFACE THAT HAS NOT HAD THE PEROXIDE-ACTIVATED STRIPPER
COMPLETELY AND THOROUGHLY RINSED OFF WITH WATER AND
VICE-VERSA. IF YOU MIX AND CROSS-CONTAMINATE, IT CAN BE
HIGHLY CORROSIVE TO METAL SURFACES. THIS CAN CAUSE SKIN
PITTING DAMAGE.
(c) Fully mix the strippers.
NOTE: Refer to the manufacturer's instructions to mix the paint stripper.
1) Make sure that airplane is completely and thoroughly rinsed down with water to
remove all residue of the initial paint stripper when using a different paint stripper.
2) Do not apply acid-activated stripper onto any airplane surface that has not had the
peroxide-activated stripper completely and thoroughly rinsed off with water and
vise-versa.
NOTE: If necessary, a mild alkaline cleaner solution can be used to neutralize the
hydrogen-peroxide paint based stripper. You can use Super Bee 300LF
cleaner, B50394 [CP2615] or other equivalent cleaners approved for aircraft
exteriors to remove paint and stripper residue.
(d) Apply large quantities of the stripper on the paint.
NOTE: Keep the surface wet with the stripper.
1) Put the stripper on with a natural bristle paint brush, STD-1080, or a non-atomizing
spray.
2) If it is necessary, add more layers of the stripper.
(e) Let the stripper stay on the surface a sufficient time for the finish to become soft and
become wrinkled.
51-21-11
CAUTION
DO NOT USE ABRASIVE PADS (SCOTCH-BRITE), OR ABRASIVE PAPER
ON THE ALUMINUM SURFACE UNLESS THE SCRIBE LINE
INSPECTIONS WERE MADE. ABRASIVE PADS CAN SMOOTH THE
ALUMINUM SURFACE AND HIDE THE SCRIBE LINE MARKS. IF YOU
USE ABRASIVE PADS TO REMOVE THE PAINT OR PRIMER BEFORE
YOU DO THE SCRIBE LINE INSPECTIONS, YOU CAN BE REQUIRED TO
DO REPEAT INSPECTIONS BASED ON THE SCRIBE LINE INSPECTION
SERVICE BULLETIN.
CAUTION
ONLY USE APPROVED SCRAPERS ON THE AIRPLANE SKIN.
SCRAPERS THAT ARE NOT APPROVED CAN MAKE SCRATCHES ON
THE SKIN, AND CAUSE FATIGUE CRACKS.
(f) Remove the loosened material with a bristle paint brush, STD-1080, or with a sealant
removal tool, COM-2481, with sealant removal tool handle, COM-4746, before the
stripper becomes dry.
NOTE: If you use abrasive pads before you do scribe line inspections, you can be
required to do repeat inspections based on the scribe line inspection Service
Bulletin 737-53A1289.
(g) If it is necessary, apply the stripper again to remove all unwanted adhesive or the primer.
(h) Rinse the surface with sponge, G00116, that is soaked with clean water.
WARNING
DO NOT GET SOLVENTS IN YOUR MOUTH, YOUR EYES, OR ON
YOUR SKIN. DO NOT BREATHE THE FUMES FROM SOLVENTS.
SOLVENTS ARE DANGEROUS MATERIALS. SOLVENTS CAN BE
FLAMMABLE. OBEY THE MATERIAL SAFETY DATA SHEETS (MSDS)
FOR SOLVENTS. OBEY LOCAL REGULATIONS FOR THE CORRECT
PROCEDURES TO USE OR DISCARD SOLVENTS. SOLVENTS CAN
CAUSE INJURIES TO PERSONNEL AND DAMAGE TO EQUIPMENT .
1) As an alternative to water, you can use Series 84 solvent, B01004, to clean the
surface.
(i) Dry the surface with cotton wiper, G00034.
1) Make sure that the surface is fully dry.
E. Remove the Epoxy Adhesives and Adhesive Primers
SUBTASK 51-21-11-150-023
(1) Prepare the surface for paint striping, do this task: Prepare for Paint Stripping,
TASK 51-21-11-370-801.
SUBTASK 51-21-11-150-002
CAUTION
MAKE SURE THAT YOU REFER TO THE MANUFACTURER'S INSTRUCTIONS
WHEN YOU APPLY THE STRIPPER THE SURFACE. IF YOU DO NOT
FOLLOW THE MANUFACTURER'S INSTRUCTIONS, DAMAGE CAN OCCUR
TO THE SURFACE.
51-21-11
(CAUTION PRECEDES)
CAUTION
MAKE SURE THAT THE STRIPPER DOES NOT TOUCH THE SURFACE FOR
MORE THAN 2 HOURS. DAMAGE TO THE SURFACE CAN OCCUR IF THE
STRIPPER TOUCHES THE SURFACE FOR A LONG TIME.
(2) Do the epoxy adhesive and adhesive primer stripping.
(a) Refer to Table 701 for the approved stripping materials.
(b) Fully mix the strippers.
NOTE: Refer to the manufacturer's instructions to mix the adhesive stripper.
(c) Apply a small quantity of stripper on the adhesive or the primer with a paint brush,
STD-1080.
1) Make sure to keep the surface wet with stripper.
2) Let the stripper absorb the adhesive or the primer for not more than 30 minutes.
3) Make sure that the stripper does not touch or fall on aluminum surfaces.
CAUTION
DO NOT USE ABRASIVE PADS (SCOTCH-BRITE), OR ABRASIVE PAPER
ON THE ALUMINUM SURFACE UNLESS THE SCRIBE LINE
INSPECTIONS WERE MADE. ABRASIVE PADS CAN SMOOTH THE
ALUMINUM SURFACE AND HIDE THE SCRIBE LINE MARKS. IF YOU
USE ABRASIVE PADS TO REMOVE THE PAINT OR PRIMER BEFORE
YOU DO THE SCRIBE LINE INSPECTIONS, YOU CAN BE REQUIRED TO
DO REPEAT INSPECTIONS BASED ON THE SCRIBE LINE INSPECTION
SERVICE BULLETIN.
CAUTION
ONLY USE APPROVED SCRAPERS ON THE AIRPLANE SKIN.
SCRAPERS THAT ARE NOT APPROVED CAN MAKE SCRATCHES ON
THE SKIN, AND CAUSE FATIGUE CRACKS.
(d) Remove the stripper and loosened adhesive, or the primer with cotton wiper, G00034, or
a sealant removal tool, COM-2481, with sealant removal tool handle, COM-4746.
NOTE: If you use abrasive pads before you do scribe line inspections, you can be
required to do repeat inspections based on the scribe line inspection Service
Bulletin 737-53A1289.
(e) If it is necessary, apply the stripper again to remove all unwanted adhesive or the primer.
(f) Rinse the surface with sponge, G00116, that is soaked with clean water.
WARNING
DO NOT GET SOLVENTS IN YOUR MOUTH, YOUR EYES, OR ON
YOUR SKIN. DO NOT BREATHE THE FUMES FROM SOLVENTS.
SOLVENTS ARE DANGEROUS MATERIALS. SOLVENTS CAN BE
FLAMMABLE. OBEY THE MATERIAL SAFETY DATA SHEETS (MSDS)
FOR SOLVENTS. OBEY LOCAL REGULATIONS FOR THE CORRECT
PROCEDURES TO USE OR DISCARD SOLVENTS. SOLVENTS CAN
CAUSE INJURIES TO PERSONNEL AND DAMAGE TO EQUIPMENT .
1) As an alternative to water, you can use Series 84 solvent, B01004, to clean the
surface.
(g) Dry the surface fully with a clean dry cotton wiper, G00034.
51-21-11
F . Remove the Rain Erosion Coating from Fiberglass
SUBTASK 51-21-11-150-024
(1) Prepare the surface for the paint stripping, do this task: Prepare for Paint Stripping,
TASK 51-21-11-370-801.
SUBTASK 51-21-11-150-005
(2) Do the stripping to remove the rain erosion coating.
WARNING
TOLUENE THINNER IS AN AGENT THAT IS FLAMMABLE, EXPLOSIVE,
POISONOUS, AND AN IRRITANT . MAKE SURE ALL PERSONS OBEY ALL
OF THE PRECAUTIONS WHEN TOLUENE THINNER IS USED.
• GAS/AIR MIXTURES MORE THAN THE LOWER EXPLOSIVE LIMIT
(LEL) CAN CAUSE AN EXPLOSION IF HIGH HEAT , SPARKS, OR
FLAMES SUPPLY IGNITION.
• USE IN AN AREA OPEN TO THE AIR.
• CLOSE THE CONTAINER WHEN NOT USED.
• DO NOT GET TOLUENE THINNER IN THE EYES, ON THE SKIN, OR
ON YOUR CLOTHES.
• DO NOT BREATHE THE GAS.
(a) Make sure that you put on the following protection before using toluene solvent, B00094:
1) Protective splash goggles
2) Gloves.
(b) Put clean cloths that are moist with toluene solvent, B00094, on the rain erosion coating.
CAUTION
MAKE SURE THAT THE TOLUENE DOES NOT STAY ON THE
FIBERGLASS FOR MORE THAN 2 HOURS. DAMAGE TO THE SURFACE
CAN OCCUR IF THE STRIPPER STAYS ON THE SURFACE FOR MORE
THAN 2 HOURS.
(c) If it is necessary, add more toluene solvent, B00094, to keep the cloths moist.
1) Permit sufficient time to loosen the coating from the fiberglass (20 minutes to 1
hour).
a) Make sure that the toluene does not stay on the fiberglass surface for more
than 2 hours.
CAUTION
DO NOT USE ABRASIVE PADS (SCOTCH-BRITE), OR ABRASIVE PAPER
ON THE ALUMINUM SURFACE UNLESS THE SCRIBE LINE
INSPECTIONS WERE MADE. ABRASIVE PADS CAN SMOOTH THE
ALUMINUM SURFACE AND HIDE THE SCRIBE LINE MARKS. IF YOU
USE ABRASIVE PADS TO REMOVE THE PAINT OR PRIMER BEFORE
YOU DO THE SCRIBE LINE INSPECTIONS, YOU CAN BE REQUIRED TO
DO REPEAT INSPECTIONS BASED ON THE SCRIBE LINE INSPECTION
SERVICE BULLETIN.
51-21-11
(CAUTION PRECEDES)
CAUTION
MAKE SURE THAT YOU ONLY USE APPROVED SCRAPERS ON THE
AIRPLANE SKIN. SCRAPERS THAT ARE NOT APPROVED CAN MAKE
SCRATCHES ON THE SKIN AND CAUSE FATIGUE CRACKS.
(d) Remove the loosened rain erosion coating with a bristle brush, STD-133, or a sealant
removal tool, COM-2481, with sealant removal tool handle, COM-4746, before toluene
solvent, B00094, dries.
NOTE: If you use abrasive pads before you do scribe line inspections, you can be
required to do repeat inspections based on the scribe line inspection Service
Bulletin 737-53A1289.
(e) Lightly abrade (sand) the surface with abrasive cloth, B00102, or 240 grit (minimum)
abrasive paper.
(f) Clean the area with xylene solvent, B00344.
(g) Dry the surface fully with a clean, dry cotton wiper, G00034.

*[8]
50°F (10°C) to 80°F (27°C)
*[1] Mixing or cross-contamination of acid-activated strippers with hydrogen peroxide-activated strippers can be highly
corrosive to metal surfaces. Before applying any paint strippers, special attention must be taken to:
1) Thoroughly clean paint stripping equipment (spray guns, pots, pumps, pressure hoses, etc.) of any chemical
residue from prior usage per equipment manufacturer’s instructions. It is recommended to recirculate water through
the pump, hose, and spray gun at least 5X the volume of the hose and pump. After recirculation, check the pH of the
rinsate to verify it is the same pH as the water source. Scrub clean inner diameter of pump/hose/gun from any
material buildup and thoroughly clean outer diameter of spray gun. Clean squeegees and all tools contacting airplane
surface.
2) Ensure stripper is homogeneous by agitating material for a minimum of 30 minutes** (e.g with a drum roller or
recirculating material with cleaned pump/hose/gun). **this depends on if using a 55-gallon drum or a large tote.
3) If one type of paint stripper (e.g. peroxide-activated) is used on an aircraft and it is determined that a different type
of stripper is required (e.g. acid-activated), then the aircraft must be completely and thoroughly rinsed down with
water to remove all residue of the initial paint stripper. Do not apply acid-activated stripper onto any aircraft surface
that has not had the peroxide-activated stripper completely and thoroughly rinsed off with water.
*[2] Do not allow these peroxide-based strippers to come in contact directly with unpainted steel alloys. A thin layer of
these paint strippers can cause corrosion. In using these strippers, expose paint chips and spent strippers to a fine
spray of soap during clean-up after stripping, or rinse paint chips with water to remove paint stripper prior to
packaging in vented containers before disposal. Dispense the stripper through plastic or stainless steel equipment.
Collecting wastes in rusty drums, mixing conversion coating rinse water with stripper residues, or throwing rags,
gloves, or other fabrics into the waste drum may cause combustion. Used material contaminated with paint flakes and
organic materials may undergo spontaneous pressurization. The paint stripper produces water and oxygen during the
paint removal process. This process will continue after the stripper is removed from the aircraft. To prevent pressure
from building up in waste containers, do not cap containers for 24 hours after collecting the stripper residue and paint
chips.
*[3] Do not use this stripper on polysulfides. An example of a polysulfide is sealants.
*[4] Not for use on high-strength steel or magnesium parts. Make sure that all high-strength steel and magnesium parts
are masked. Restricted-used strippers are not compatible with high-strength steel and magnesium. Restricted-used
strippers are not allowed on inspar wing surfaces. Restricted-used strippers will discolor clad aluminum.
*[5] Maximum of five paint stripping cycles are allowed during the life of the airplane.
*[6] Maximum of four paint stripping cycles are allowed during the life of the airplane.
*[7] Complete removal of organic materials by abrasive methods damages conversion coatings and may remove plating.
Strip conversion coatings and re-finish. Inspect plating (except chrome) for compliance with requirements and
supplementary chromate treatment requirements and re-finish as required.
*[8] This solution damages plating and conversion coating (for example, chemical conversion coating and anodized).
Rinse as soon as possible after stripping to stop etching. Plating and conversion coating exposed to the stripper shall
be stripped and refinished.

4. Ferrous Alloys - Paint Stripping
A. General
(1) This task gives paint stripping instructions using the abrasive stripping methods that follow:
(a) Conventional Abrasive Blasting Method
(b) Plastic Media Blasting Method
(c) Wheat Starch Blasting Method
(d) Nanocomposite Blasting Method.
(2) For metal surfaces, you can remove paint by abrasive stripping, or by brush-on or paint-on with
paint strippers or solvents. For procedures to remove paint with paint strippers or solvents, see
Paint Stripping, TASK 51-21-11-150-801.
(3) Make sure that the areas that follow are covered:
(a) Lubricated parts
(b) Rubber parts
(c) Laminated plastic surfaces or parts
(d) Fiberglass surfaces or parts
(e) Aerodynamic smoothers
(f) Metal bonded parts
(g) Composite parts
(h) Adjacent access doors, fairings, and wing Leading Edge (LE)s and Trailing Edge (TE)s
(i) All areas adjacent to the area being stripped
(j) Drain holes and vents
(k) Pitot static probes and ports
(l) Angle of Attack (AOA) vanes
(m) Cargo door and entry door hinges and thresholds
(n) Cooling air exhaust duct
(o) Tires
(p) Ram air inlets
(q) Engines and the Auxiliary Power Unit (APU) inlets and exhausts
(r) Pressurized drain valves
(s) Joints.
(4) Make sure that the ambient temperature is between 50°F (10°C) and 100°F (38°C).
NOTE: Do not use the stripping procedure in hot or wet weather.
B. Consumable Materials
Reference Description Specification
B50268 Solvent - General Cleaning of Metals (AMM
20-30-80) - Series 80
E50010 Media - EnviroStrip 12/30 Wheat Starch Media
E50011 Media - Plastic Media For Removal Of Organic
Coatings (20/30 Mesh)
MIL-P-85891
51-21-11
(Continued)
Reference Description Specification
E50012 Media - Magic II 30/60 Composite Blast Media
(Type VIII)
MIL-P-85891

Wiper (Cheesecloth, Gauze)
AMS3819 Class 1
Grade A or B Form 1
(Supersede BMS15-5
CL A)
G00215 Brush - Soft Bristle Fiber
G50822 Abrasive - Walnut Shells A-A-1722 Type II
G50823 Abrasive - Apricot Pit Shells A-A-1722 Type I
C. Prepare to Strip the Surface
SUBTASK 51-21-11-150-007
(1) Prepare to strip the surface, do this task: Prepare for Paint Stripping, TASK 51-21-11-370-801.
SUBTASK 51-21-11-110-003
(2) Clean the surface.
(a) Remove loose soil, excess grease, hydraulic fluid, oil, and metal shavings using a cotton
wiper, G00034.
1) Use an oil absorbing wiper to remove oil, if necessary.
(b) Clean the surface with a cotton wiper, G00034, or brush, G00215, wet with a Series 80
solvent, B50268.
(c) Immediately wipe the solvent from the surface using a clean, dry cotton wiper, G00034.
1) Make sure that the solvent does not dry on the surface.
(d) Clean the surface again until the surface is free from all soil, excess grease, hydraulic
fluid, oil, and metal shavings.
D. Paint Stripping - Conventional Abrasive Blasting Method
SUBTASK 51-21-11-950-011
(1) Make sure that the areas that will not be stripped are masked.
SUBTASK 51-21-11-110-004
(2) Make sure that the areas that will be stripped are clean and dry.
SUBTASK 51-21-11-120-001
(3) Abrasive blast the area to be stripped with apricot pit shell abrasive, G50823, or walnut shells
abrasive, G50822.
(a) Make sure that the nozzle air pressure is 90 psig (621 kPa) to 100 psig (689 kPa).
(b) Make sure that the nozzle is approximately 6 in. (152 mm) from the surface.
(c) Abrade the surface equally down to the primer surface.
NOTE: It is not necessary to remove the primers for the reapplication of primer, enamels,
or topcoat.
SUBTASK 51-21-11-160-002
(4) Clean the surface using clean, compressed air.
E. Paint Stripping - Plastic Media Blasting Method
SUBTASK 51-21-11-950-012
(1) Make sure that the areas that will not be stripped are masked.
51-21-11
SUBTASK 51-21-11-160-003
(2) Make sure that the areas that will be stripped are clean and dry.
SUBTASK 51-21-11-120-002
(3) Plastic media blast using plastic media, E50011, Type V, Grade A, to strip the paint.
(a) Screen the plastic media, E50011, to remove undersized media and contaminants.
NOTE: Examples of contaminants are paint flakes, metallic particles, and degraded
media.
(b) Make sure that the nozzle air pressure is 20 psig (138 kPa) to 40 psig (276 kPa).
(c) Make sure that the nozzle is approximately 6 in. (152 mm) or more away from the
surface.
(d) Make sure that the angle of the nozzle is at 45° to the surface.
(e) Abrade the surface equally down to the primer surface.
NOTE: It is not necessary to remove the primers for the reapplication of primer, enamels,
or topcoat.
SUBTASK 51-21-11-160-004
(4) Clean the surface.
(a) Rinse the surface with water.
(b) Dry the surface with a clean, cotton wiper, G00034.
F . Paint Stripping - Wheat Starch Blasting Method
SUBTASK 51-21-11-950-013
(1) Make sure that the areas that will not be stripped are masked.
SUBTASK 51-21-11-160-005
(2) Make sure that the areas that will be stripped are clean and dry.
SUBTASK 51-21-11-120-003
(3) Strip the surface using EnviroStrip 12/30 wheat starch media, E50010.
NOTE: Complete removal of organic coatings by wheat starch media blasting can damage
surface treatments on the base metal (for example: chemical conversion coating and
anodized coating) and may remove plating.
(a) Make sure that the nozzle air pressure is between 30 psig (207 kPa) and 50 psig
(345 kPa).
(b) Make sure that the nozzle is at least 6 in. (152 mm) from the surface.
(c) Make sure that the nozzle angle is 20° to 60° from the surface.
(d) If the conversion coating was damaged, remove the remaining coating and reapply, as
necessary.
(e) If the plating was removed, inspect the plating.
1) Make sure that the plating meets the requirements and re-finish, as necessary.
SUBTASK 51-21-11-160-006
(4) Clean the surface.
(a) Rinse the surface with water.
(b) Dry the surface with a clean, cotton wiper, G00034.
51-21-11
G. Paint Stripping - Nanocomposite Blasting Method
SUBTASK 51-21-11-950-014
(1) Make sure that the areas that will not be stripped are masked.
SUBTASK 51-21-11-160-007
(2) Make sure that the areas that will be stripped are clean and dry.
SUBTASK 51-21-11-120-004
(3) Strip the surface using one of the nanocomposite abrasives that follow:
(a) For general purpose stripping, use Magic 1 Nanocomposite Abrasive Media, 30/60 Mesh.
(b) For an increased strip rate, use Magic II Composite Blast Media, E50012.
(c) To remove cured sealant, use Magic 3 Nanocomposite Abrasive Media, 10/60 Mesh.
(d) Make sure that the nozzle air pressure is less than 32 psig (221 kPa).
(e) Make sure that the nozzle is at least 6 in. (152 mm) from the surface.
(f) Make sure that the angle of the nozzle is 40° to 60° to the surface.
(g) Abrade the surface equally down to the primer surface.
NOTE: It is not necessary to remove the primers for the reapplication of primer, enamels,
or topcoat.
SUBTASK 51-21-11-160-008
(4) Clean the surface.
(a) Rinse the surface with water.
(b) Dry the surface with a clean, cotton wiper, G00034.
51-21-11-150-803Titanium - Paint StrippingPDF p. 151

5. Titanium - Paint Stripping
A. General
(1) This task gives paint stripping instructions using the abrasive stripping methods that follow:
(a) Plastic Media Blasting Method
(b) Wheat Starch Blasting Method
(c) Nanocomposite Blasting Method.
(2) For metal surfaces, you can remove paint by abrasive stripping, or by brush-on or paint-on with
paint strippers or solvents. For procedures to remove paint with paint strippers or solvents, see
Paint Stripping, TASK 51-21-11-150-801.
(3) Make sure that the areas that follow are covered:
(a) Lubricated parts
(b) Rubber parts
(c) Laminated plastic surfaces or parts
(d) Fiberglass surfaces or parts
(e) Aerodynamic smoothers
(f) Metal bonded parts
(g) Composite parts
(h) Adjacent access doors, fairings, and wing Leading Edge (LE)s and Trailing Edge (TE)s
51-21-11
(i) All areas adjacent to the area being stripped
(j) Drain holes and vents
(k) Pitot static probes and ports
(l) Angle of Attack (AOA) vanes
(m) Cargo door and entry door hinges and thresholds
(n) Cooling air exhaust duct
(o) Tires
(p) Ram air inlets
(q) Engines and the Auxiliary Power Unit (APU) inlets and exhausts
(r) Pressurized drain valves
(s) Joints.
(4) Make sure that the ambient temperature is between 50°F (10°C) and 100°F (38°C).
NOTE: Do not use the stripping procedure in hot or wet weather.

C. Prepare to Strip the Surface
SUBTASK 51-21-11-150-008
(1) Prepare to strip the surface, do this task: Prepare for Paint Stripping, TASK 51-21-11-370-801.
SUBTASK 51-21-11-160-009
(2) Clean the surface.
(a) Remove loose soil, excess grease, hydraulic fluid, oil, and metal shavings using a cotton
wiper, G00034.
1) Use an oil absorbing wiper to remove oil, if necessary.
(b) Clean the surface with a cotton wiper, G00034, or brush, G00215, wet with a Series 80
solvent, B50268.
(c) Immediately wipe the solvent from the surface using a clean, dry cotton wiper, G00034.
1) Make sure that the solvent does not dry on the surface.
(d) Clean the surface again until the surface is free from all soil, excess grease, hydraulic
fluid, oil, and metal shavings.
51-21-11
D. Paint Stripping - Plastic Media Blasting Method
SUBTASK 51-21-11-950-015
(1) Make sure that the areas that will not be stripped are masked.
SUBTASK 51-21-11-160-010
(2) Make sure that the areas that will be stripped are clean and dry.
SUBTASK 51-21-11-120-005
(3) Plastic media blast using plastic media, E50011, Type V, Grade A, to strip the paint.
(a) Screen the plastic media, E50011, to remove undersized media and contaminants.
NOTE: Examples of contaminants are paint flakes, metallic particles, and degraded
media.
(b) Make sure that the nozzle air pressure is 20 psig (138 kPa) to 40 psig (276 kPa).
(c) Make sure that the nozzle is approximately 6 in. (152 mm) or more away from the
surface.
(d) Make sure that the angle of the nozzle is at 45° to the surface.
(e) Abrade the surface equally down to the primer surface.
NOTE: It is not necessary to remove the primers for the reapplication of primer, enamels,
or topcoat.
SUBTASK 51-21-11-160-011
(4) Clean the surface.
(a) Rinse the surface with water.
(b) Dry the surface with a clean, cotton wiper, G00034.
E. Paint Stripping - Wheat Starch Blasting Method
SUBTASK 51-21-11-950-016
(1) Make sure that the areas that will not be stripped are masked.
SUBTASK 51-21-11-160-012
(2) Make sure that the areas that will be stripped are clean and dry.
SUBTASK 51-21-11-120-006
(3) Strip the surface using EnviroStrip 12/30 wheat starch media, E50010.
NOTE: Complete removal of organic coatings by wheat starch media blasting can damage
surface treatments on the base metal (for example: chemical conversion coating and
anodized coating) and may remove plating.
(a) Make sure that the nozzle air pressure is between 30 psig (207 kPa) and 50 psig
(345 kPa).
(b) Make sure that the nozzle is at least 6 in. (152 mm) from the surface.
(c) Make sure that the nozzle angle is 20° to 60° from the surface.
(d) If the conversion coating was damaged, remove the remaining coating and reapply, as
necessary.
(e) If the plating was removed, inspect the plating.
1) Make sure that the plating meets the requirements and re-finish, as necessary.
SUBTASK 51-21-11-160-013
(4) Clean the surface.
(a) Rinse the surface with water.
51-21-11
(b) Dry the surface with a clean, cotton wiper, G00034.
F . Paint Stripping - Nanocomposite Blasting Method
SUBTASK 51-21-11-950-017
(1) Make sure that the areas that will not be stripped are masked.
SUBTASK 51-21-11-160-014
(2) Make sure that the areas that will be stripped are clean and dry.
SUBTASK 51-21-11-120-007
(3) Strip the surface using one of the nanocomposite abrasives that follow:
(a) For general purpose stripping, use Magic 1 Nanocomposite Abrasive Media, 30/60 Mesh.
(b) For an increased strip rate, use Magic II Composite Blast Media, E50012.
(c) To remove cured sealant, use Magic 3 Nanocomposite Abrasive Media, 10/60 Mesh.
(d) Make sure that the nozzle air pressure is less than 32 psig (221 kPa).
(e) Make sure that the nozzle is at least 6 in. (152 mm) from the surface.
(f) Make sure that the angle of the nozzle is 40° to 60° to the surface.
(g) Abrade the surface equally down to the primer surface.
NOTE: It is not necessary to remove the primers for the reapplication of primer, enamels,
or topcoat.
SUBTASK 51-21-11-160-015
(4) Clean the surface.
(a) Rinse the surface with water.
(b) Dry the surface with a clean, cotton wiper, G00034.

6. Titanium Alloys - Paint Stripping
A. General

(a) Conventional Abrasive Blasting Method
(b) Plastic Media Blasting Method
(c) Wheat Starch Blasting Method
(d) Nanocomposite Blasting Method.
(2) For metal surfaces, you can remove paint by abrasive stripping, or by brush-on or paint-on with
paint strippers or solvents. For procedures to remove paint with paint strippers or solvents, see
Paint Stripping, TASK 51-21-11-150-801.
(3) Make sure that the areas that follow are covered:
(a) Lubricated parts
(b) Rubber parts
(c) Laminated plastic surfaces or parts
(d) Fiberglass surfaces or parts
(e) Aerodynamic smoothers
(f) Metal bonded parts
51-21-11

(l) Angle of Attack (AOA) vanes

(n) Cooling air exhaust duct
(o) Tires
(p) Ram air inlets
(q) Engines and the Auxiliary Power Unit (APU) inlets and exhausts
(r) Pressurized drain valves
(s) Joints.
(4) Make sure that the ambient temperature is between 50°F (10°C) and 100°F (38°C).

(3) Make sure that the areas that follow are covered:
(a) Lubricated parts
(b) Rubber parts
(c) Laminated plastic surfaces or parts
(d) Fiberglass surfaces or parts
(e) Aerodynamic smoothers
(f) Metal bonded parts
(g) Composite parts
(h) Adjacent access doors, fairings, and wing Leading Edge (LE)s and Trailing Edge (TE)s
(i) All areas adjacent to the area being stripped
(j) Drain holes and vents
(k) Pitot static probes and ports
(l) Angle of Attack (AOA) vanes
(m) Cargo door and entry door hinges and thresholds
(n) Cooling air exhaust duct
(o) Tires
(p) Ram air inlets
(q) Engines and the Auxiliary Power Unit (APU) inlets and exhausts
(r) Pressurized drain valves
(s) Joints.
(4) Make sure that the ambient temperature is between 50°F (10°C) and 100°F (38°C).
NOTE: Do not use the stripping procedure in hot or wet weather.
51-21-11

Reference Description Specification
B50268 Solvent - General Cleaning of Metals (AMM
