C. Prepare to Strip the Surface
SUBTASK 51-21-11-150-012
(1) Prepare to strip the surface, do this task: Prepare for Paint Stripping, TASK 51-21-11-370-801.
SUBTASK 51-21-11-160-039
(2) Clean the surface.
(a) Remove loose soil, excess grease, hydraulic fluid, oil, and metal shavings using a cotton
wiper, G00034.
1) Use an oil absorbing wiper to remove oil, if necessary.
(b) Clean the surface with a cotton wiper, G00034, or brush, G00215, wet with a Series 80
solvent, B50268.
(c) Immediately wipe the solvent from the surface using a clean, dry cotton wiper, G00034.
1) Make sure that the solvent does not dry on the surface.
(d) Clean the surface again until the surface is free from all soil, excess grease, hydraulic
fluid, oil, and metal shavings.
D. Paint Stripping - Conventional Abrasive Blasting Method
SUBTASK 51-21-11-950-028
(1) Make sure that the areas that will not be stripped are masked.
SUBTASK 51-21-11-160-040
(2) Make sure that the areas that will be stripped are clean and dry.
SUBTASK 51-21-11-120-015
(3) Abrasive blast the area to be stripped with apricot pit shell abrasive, G50823, or walnut shells
abrasive, G50822.
(a) Make sure that the nozzle air pressure is 90 psig (621 kPa) to 100 psig (689 kPa).
(b) Make sure that the nozzle is approximately 6 in. (152 mm) from the surface.
(c) Abrade the surface equally down to the primer surface.
NOTE: It is not necessary to remove the primers for the reapplication of primer, enamels,
or topcoat.
SUBTASK 51-21-11-160-041
(4) Clean the surface using clean, compressed air.
51-21-11
E. Paint Stripping - Wheat Starch Blasting Method
SUBTASK 51-21-11-950-029
(1) Make sure that the areas that will not be stripped are masked.
SUBTASK 51-21-11-160-042
(2) Make sure that the areas that will be stripped are clean and dry.
SUBTASK 51-21-11-120-016
(3) Strip the surface using EnviroStrip 12/30 wheat starch media, E50010.
NOTE: Complete removal of organic coatings by wheat starch media blasting can damage
surface treatments on the base metal (for example: chemical conversion coating and
anodized coating) and may remove plating.
(a) Make sure that the nozzle air pressure is between 30 psig (207 kPa) and 50 psig
(345 kPa).
(b) Make sure that the nozzle is at least 6 in. (152 mm) from the surface.
(c) Make sure that the nozzle angle is 20° to 60° from the surface.
(d) If the conversion coating was damaged, remove the remaining coating and reapply, as
necessary.
(e) If the plating was removed, inspect the plating.
1) Make sure that the plating meets the requirements and re-finish, as necessary.
SUBTASK 51-21-11-160-043
(4) Clean the surface.
(a) Rinse the surface with water.
(b) Dry the surface with a clean, cotton wiper, G00034.

(3) Plastic media blast using plastic media, E50011, Type V, Grade A, to strip the paint.
(a) Screen the plastic media, E50011, to remove undersized media and contaminants.
NOTE: Examples of contaminants are paint flakes, metallic particles, and degraded
media.
(b) Make sure that the nozzle air pressure is 20 psig (138 kPa) to 40 psig (276 kPa).
(c) Make sure that the nozzle is approximately 6 in. (152 mm) or more away from the
surface.
(d) Make sure that the angle of the nozzle is at 45° to the surface.
(e) Abrade the surface equally down to the primer surface.
NOTE: It is not necessary to remove the primers for the reapplication of primer, enamels,
or topcoat.

(c) To remove cured sealant, use Magic 3 Nanocomposite Abrasive Media, 10/60 Mesh.
(d) Make sure that the nozzle air pressure is less than 32 psig (221 kPa).
(e) Make sure that the nozzle is at least 6 in. (152 mm) from the surface.

12. Aluminum Alloys - Paint Stripping
A. General
(1) This task gives paint stripping instructions using the abrasive stripping methods that follow:
(a) Plastic Media Blasting Method
1) This method is applicable to non-clad (bare) aluminum alloys with a gage thickness
of 0.125 in. (3.175 mm).
(b) Wheat Starch Blasting Method
1) This method is applicable to the following:
a) Aluminum alloys with a gage thickness of 0.032 in. (0.813 mm) or greater,
including both clad and non-clad (bare) alloys.
b) Bonded honeycomb sandwich parts with aluminum facesheets 0.012 in.
(0.305 mm) thick or greater.
(c) Nanocomposite Blasting Method.
1) This method is applicable to the following:
a) Aluminum alloys with a gage thickness of 0.040 in. (1.016 mm) or greater.
b) Bonded honeycomb sandwich parts with aluminum facesheets 0.012 in.
(0.305 mm) thick or greater.
(2) For metal surfaces, you can remove paint by abrasive stripping, or by brush-on or paint-on with
paint strippers or solvents. For procedures to remove paint, with paint strippers or solvents,
see Paint Stripping, TASK 51-21-11-150-801.
(a) Abrasions and scratches are satisfactory if a fingernail does not catch while pulled across
the abrasion or scratch, and the abrasion or scratch is not less than 0.10 in. (0.25 cm)
from a lap joint.
1) It is not necessary to do supplemental inspections or to record the location of the
damage.
(b) If the abrasion or scratch catches the fingernail at the two worst areas, make sure that
the clad exists. If the clad exists, it is not required to do additional inspection or to record
the location of the damage.
(c) If the abrasion or scratch is not satisfactory, then refer to the Structural Repair Manual
(SRM) for instruction.
(3) Make sure that the areas that follow are covered:
(a) Lubricated parts
(b) Rubber parts
(c) Laminated plastic surfaces or parts
(d) Fiberglass surfaces or parts
(e) Aerodynamic smoothers
(f) Metal bonded parts
(g) Composite parts
(h) Adjacent access doors, fairings, and wing Leading Edge (LE)s and Trailing Edge (TE)s
51-21-11
(i) All areas adjacent to the area being stripped
(j) Drain holes and vents
(k) Pitot static probes and ports
(l) Angle of Attack (AOA) vanes
(m) Cargo door and entry door hinges and thresholds
(n) Cooling air exhaust duct
(o) Tires
(p) Ram air inlets
(q) Engines and the Auxiliary Power Unit (APU) inlets and exhausts
(r) Pressurized drain valves
(s) Joints.
(4) Make sure that the ambient temperature is between 50°F (10°C) and 100°F (38°C).
NOTE: Do not use the stripping procedure in hot or wet weather.

(1) Make sure that the areas that will not be stripped are masked.
SUBTASK 51-21-11-160-064
(2) Make sure that the areas that will be stripped are clean and dry.
SUBTASK 51-21-11-120-025
(3) Plastic media blast using plastic media, E50011, Type V, Grade A, to strip the paint.
(a) Screen the plastic media, E50011, to remove undersized media and contaminants.
NOTE: Examples of contaminants are paint flakes, metallic particles, and degraded
media.
(b) Make sure that the nozzle air pressure is 20 psig (138 kPa) to 40 psig (276 kPa).
(c) Make sure that the nozzle is approximately 6 in. (152 mm) or more away from the
surface.
(d) Make sure that the angle of the nozzle is at 45° to the surface.
(e) Abrade the surface equally down to the primer surface.
NOTE: It is not necessary to remove the primers for the reapplication of primer, enamels,
or topcoat.
SUBTASK 51-21-11-160-065
(4) Clean the surface.
(a) Rinse the surface with water.
(b) Dry the surface with a clean, cotton wiper, G00034.
E. Paint Stripping - Wheat Starch Blasting Method
SUBTASK 51-21-11-950-039
(1) Make sure that the areas that will not be stripped are masked.
SUBTASK 51-21-11-160-066
(2) Make sure that the areas that will be stripped are clean and dry.
SUBTASK 51-21-11-120-026
(3) Strip the surface using EnviroStrip 12/30 wheat starch media, E50010.
NOTE: Complete removal of organic coatings by wheat starch media blasting can damage
surface treatments on the base metal (for example: chemical conversion coating and
anodized coating) and may remove plating.
(a) Make sure that the nozzle air pressure is between 30 psig (207 kPa) and 50 psig
(345 kPa).
(b) Make sure that the nozzle is at least 6 in. (152 mm) from the surface.
(c) Make sure that the nozzle angle is 20° to 60° from the surface.
(d) If the conversion coating was damaged, remove the remaining coating and reapply, as
necessary.
(e) If the plating was removed, inspect the plating.
1) Make sure that the plating meets the requirements and re-finish, as necessary.
SUBTASK 51-21-11-160-067
(4) Clean the surface.
(a) Rinse the surface with water.
51-21-11
(b) Dry the surface with a clean, cotton wiper, G00034.
F . Paint Stripping - Nanocomposite Blasting Method
SUBTASK 51-21-11-950-040
(1) Make sure that the areas that will not be stripped are masked.
SUBTASK 51-21-11-160-068
(2) Make sure that the areas that will be stripped are clean and dry.
SUBTASK 51-21-11-120-027
(3) Strip the surface using one of the nanocomposite abrasives that follow:
(a) For general purpose stripping, use Magic 1 Nanocomposite Abrasive Media, 30/60 Mesh.
(b) For an increased strip rate, use Magic II Composite Blast Media, E50012.
(c) To remove cured sealant, use Magic 3 Nanocomposite Abrasive Media, 10/60 Mesh.
(d) Make sure that the nozzle air pressure is less than 32 psig (221 kPa).
(e) Make sure that the nozzle is at least 6 in. (152 mm) from the surface.
(f) Make sure that the angle of the nozzle is 40° to 60° to the surface.
(g) Abrade the surface equally down to the primer surface.
NOTE: It is not necessary to remove the primers for the reapplication of primer, enamels,
or topcoat.
SUBTASK 51-21-11-160-069
(4) Clean the surface.
(a) Rinse the surface with water.
(b) Dry the surface with a clean, cotton wiper, G00034.

14. Zinc-Nickel - Paint Stripping
A. General
(1) This task gives paint stripping instructions using the abrasive stripping methods that follow:
(a) Plastic Media Blasting Method
(b) Wheat Starch Blasting Method
(c) Nanocomposite Blasting Method.
(2) For metal surfaces, you can remove paint by abrasive stripping, or by brush-on or paint-on with
paint strippers or solvents. For procedures to remove paint with paint strippers or solvents, see
Paint Stripping, TASK 51-21-11-150-801.
(3) Make sure that the areas that follow are covered:
(a) Lubricated parts
(b) Rubber parts
(c) Laminated plastic surfaces or parts
(d) Fiberglass surfaces or parts
(e) Aerodynamic smoothers
(f) Metal bonded parts
(g) Composite parts
51-21-11
(h) Adjacent access doors, fairings, and wing Leading Edge (LE)s and Trailing Edge (TE)s
(i) All areas adjacent to the area being stripped
(j) Drain holes and vents
(k) Pitot static probes and ports
(l) Angle of Attack (AOA) vanes
(m) Cargo door and entry door hinges and thresholds
(n) Cooling air exhaust duct
(o) Tires
(p) Ram air inlets
(q) Engines and the Auxiliary Power Unit (APU) inlets and exhausts
(r) Pressurized drain valves
(s) Joints.
(4) Make sure that the ambient temperature is between 50°F (10°C) and 100°F (38°C).
NOTE: Do not use the stripping procedure in hot or wet weather.

C. Prepare to Strip the Surface
SUBTASK 51-21-11-150-017
(1) Prepare to strip the surface, do this task: Prepare for Paint Stripping, TASK 51-21-11-370-801.
SUBTASK 51-21-11-160-070
(2) Clean the surface.
(a) Remove loose soil, excess grease, hydraulic fluid, oil, and metal shavings using a cotton
wiper, G00034.
1) Use an oil absorbing wiper to remove oil, if necessary.
(b) Clean the surface with a cotton wiper, G00034, or brush, G00215, wet with a Series 80
solvent, B50268.
(c) Immediately wipe the solvent from the surface using a clean, dry cotton wiper, G00034.
1) Make sure that the solvent does not dry on the surface.
(d) Clean the surface again until the surface is free from all soil, excess grease, hydraulic
fluid, oil, and metal shavings.

(5) Definitions:
(a) Light Soils: atmospheric soils and dust.
(b) Moderate Soils: drawing or cutting oil and dust.
(c) Heavy soils: grease or the soil on areas subjected to exhaust blast.

(d) Clean the surface again until the surface is free from all soil, excess grease, hydraulic
fluid, oil, and metal shavings.
D. Paint Stripping - Conventional Abrasive Method
SUBTASK 51-21-11-950-050
(1) Make sure that the areas that will not be stripped are masked.
SUBTASK 51-21-11-160-092
(2) Make sure that the areas that will be stripped are clean and dry.
SUBTASK 51-21-11-120-037
(3) Abrade the area to be stripped with 150 grit abrasive paper, G02428, or finer.
(a) Abrade the surface equally down to the primer surface.
NOTE: It is not necessary to remove the primers for the reapplication of primer, enamels,
or topcoat.
SUBTASK 51-21-11-120-038
(4) Abrade the area to be stripped with 240 grit or finer abrasive paper, G50077.
(a) Make sure that you feather the primer and topcoat.
(b) Make sure that you do not damage the fibers of the composite surface.
(c) If the surface has conductive coating, do the steps that follow:
1) Remove all of the conductive coating.
NOTE: This is to obtain uniform conductivity after re-application.
2) Re-apply the conductive coating.
SUBTASK 51-21-11-160-093
(5) Clean the surface using clean, compressed air.
E. Paint Stripping - Wheat Starch Blasting Method
SUBTASK 51-21-11-950-051
(1) Make sure that the areas that will not be stripped are masked.
51-21-11
SUBTASK 51-21-11-160-094
(2) Make sure that the areas that will be stripped are clean and dry.
SUBTASK 51-21-11-120-039
(3) Strip the surface using EnviroStrip 12/30 wheat starch media, E50010.
(a) Make sure that the nozzle air pressure is between 18 psi (124 kPa) to 22 psi (152 kPa).
(b) Make sure that the nozzle is between 2 in. (51 mm) and 6 in. (152 mm) from the surface.
(c) Make sure that the nozzle angle is 15° to 25° from the surface.
SUBTASK 51-21-11-160-095
(4) Clean the surface with clean, dry, compressed air.
(a) Make sure that the pressure of the compressed air is not more than 30 psi (207 kPa).
SUBTASK 51-21-11-110-005
(5) Clean the surface with a general alkaline cleaner specified in Table 702.

G02222 Abrasive - Dry, Blasting BAC5748
D. Prepare the Surface
SUBTASK 51-21-12-120-001
(1) Dry abrasive blast the damaged area to bare, clean metal with a clean (preferably new) 80,
100, or 120 grit aluminum oxide abrasive, G02222.
(a) Blow the area with clean dry air to remove the remaining abrasive material.
(b) Do not touch the clean parts with your bare hands to prevent contamination of the
surface.
51-21-12
(c) Protect the parts that were abrasive blasted until the parts are coated with coating,
C00250.
NOTE: The BMS14-4 Type I must be applied in less than four hours after the dry
abrasive blast.
SUBTASK 51-21-12-120-002
(2) Use abrasive cloth, B00102 or abrasive mat, G00251 to abrade (sand) the area to feather the
edges of the damaged coating.
(a) Scuff an area approximately 1/2 inch in width around the edge of the bare metal.
SUBTASK 51-21-12-100-001
WARNING
DO NOT GET SOLVENTS IN YOUR MOUTH OR EYES, OR ON YOUR SKIN.
DO NOT BREATHE THE FUMES FROM SOLVENTS. PUT ON A RESPIRATOR,
PROTECTIVE SPLASH GOGGLES, AND GLOVES WHEN YOU USE
SOLVENTS. KEEP THE SOLVENTS AWAY FROM SPARKS, FLAME, AND
HEAT . SOLVENTS ARE POISONOUS AND FLAMMABLE. SOLVENTS CAN
CAUSE INJURIES TO PERSONNEL AND DAMAGE TO EQUIPMENT .
(3) Clean the surface with Series 84 solvent, B01004 (TASK 20-30-84-910-801).
E. Apply the coating, BMS14-4, Type I
NOTE: Use spray painting equipment that continuously stirs the material to be sprayed and which is
free from organic contaminates. Coat the parts with the BMS14-4, Type I material to get an
equal, smooth, wet coating.
SUBTASK 51-21-12-390-001
(1) Apply the coating, C00250 with a spray paint spray gun with interchangeable nozzles,
STD-1133 or a paint brush, STD-1080.
(a) Apply the coating to the surface with one of the steps that follow:
1) Apply the coating with one pass of the spray gun.
2) Apply two or three thin coats in 1 to 5 minutes of the other, at right angles to the
previous pass.
NOTE: This is necessary to prevent cracks or sags, and to keep the orange peel to
a minimum.
(b) The coating may be applied to the areas of the part that are not easy to spray paint with
the items listed below followed immediately by spray coating the remaining areas of the
part with the spray gun.
1) stencil gun
2) airbrush
3) hand brush.
(c) Do not apply too much of the coating, C00250 because it will cause, runs, beads, drips,
or curtains.
1) The conditions above can cause blistering, pitting, flaking, glazing, or heavy green
discoloration during curing.
(d) Make sure the coating is not too dry when it is applied because it will give a rough or
sandy texture.
1) These rough areas are porous and can cause decreased corrosion protection and a
decrease in the coating bond.
51-21-12
(e) Dry the parts coated with coating, C00250 for at least 15 minutes and then dry for 15 to
30 minutes at 175 ±25°F .
1) The coating must be smooth and not have any fish eyes.
2) The coating must have a smooth grey color after it is dry.
3) Cure the coating for 4 hours at 300 ±50°F .
4) You must see no lumps or runs in the cured coating.
5) The total coating must be 0.0015 to 0.0035 inches thick.
NOTE: When is not at this specified thickness, you must apply more coats of the
BMS14-4 Type I to get the specified thickness.
(f) To apply the specified overcoat systems, do this task: Decorative Exterior Paint System
Application, TASK 51-21-99-300-801.

3. Repair the BMS14-4 Type II Heat and Corrosion Resistant Coating
A. References
Reference Title
20-30-84-910-801 Final Cleaning of Metal Prior to Painting (Series 84) (P/B 201)
B. Tools/Equipment
Reference Description

Reference Description Specification

C00251 Coating - Inorganic, Heat, Weather And Oil
Resistant Protective Coating (Room
T emperature Catalytic Cure)
BMS14-4 Type II
D. Prepare the Surface
SUBTASK 51-21-12-120-003
(1) Use aluminum oxide abrasive cloth, B00102 to abrade (sand) the damaged area to bare, clean
metal with 180 grit or finer.
(a) Abrade (sand) the area if it is necessary to feather the edges of the not damaged coating.
SUBTASK 51-21-12-100-002
(2) Clean the surface with Series 84 solvent, B01004 (TASK 20-30-84-910-801).
51-21-12
E. Apply the coating, BMS14-4, Type II
SUBTASK 51-21-12-390-002
WARNING
DO NOT GET SOLVENTS IN YOUR MOUTH OR EYES, OR ON YOUR SKIN.
DO NOT BREATHE THE FUMES FROM SOLVENTS. PUT ON A RESPIRATOR,
PROTECTIVE SPLASH GOGGLES, AND GLOVES WHEN YOU USE
SOLVENTS. KEEP THE SOLVENTS AWAY FROM SPARKS, FLAME, AND
HEAT . SOLVENTS ARE POISONOUS AND FLAMMABLE. SOLVENTS CAN
CAUSE INJURIES TO PERSONNEL AND DAMAGE TO EQUIPMENT .
(1) Apply the coating, C00251 with a spray paint spray gun with interchangeable nozzles,
STD-1133 or a paint brush, STD-1080 as follows.
(a) Areas of the part that are not easy to spray paint can be done with a stencil gun, airbrush,
or hand brush first.
1) Follow this immediately by spray coating the remaining areas of the part with a
spray gun.
(b) Do not apply too much of the coating, C00251 because it will cause, runs, beads, drips,
or curtains.
1) The conditions above can cause blistering, pitting, flaking, glazing, or heavy green
discoloration during curing.
(c) Make sure the coating is not too dry when it is applied because it will produce a rough or
sandy texture.
1) These rough areas are porous and can cause decreased corrosion protection and a
decrease in the coating bond.
(d) Dry the coated parts for 30 to 45 minutes at ambient temperature.
1) The coating must be smooth and not have any fish eyes.
2) The coating must have a equal gray color after it is dry.
(e) Apply one coat of coating, C00251 catalyst to the coated area.
1) Let the catalyst cure for a minimum of 16 hours at ambient temperature.
NOTE: Do not pre-mix the coating with the catalyst, and do not let the catalyst
touch the adjacent surfaces.
a) As an alternative, you can cure the catalyst for 4 hours at 300 ±50°F .
2) You must see no lumps or runs in the cured coating.
3) The total coating must be 0.0015 to 0.0035 inches thick.
NOTE: When is not at the specified thickness, you must apply more coats to get
the specified thickness.

D. Clean the Surface
SUBTASK 51-21-21-110-001
(1) Do the steps that follow to clean the surface:
NOTE: Use solvent, B00083, or solvent, B00074, for this step.
WARNING
DO NOT GET ALIPHATIC NAPHTHA OR DRY CLEANING SOLVENT IN
YOUR MOUTH, YOUR EYES, OR ON YOUR SKIN. DO NOT BREATHE
THE FUMES FROM THE ALIPHATIC NAPHTHA OR DRY CLEANING
SOLVENT . PUT ON PROTECTIVE SPLASH GOGGLES AND GLOVES
WHEN YOU USE ALIPHATIC NAPHTHA OR DRY CLEANING SOLVENT .
KEEP ALIPHATIC NAPHTHA OR DRY CLEANING SOLVENT AWAY FROM
SPARKS, FLAME, AND TEMPERATURES ABOVE THE FLASH POINT .
ALIPHATIC NAPHTHA OR DRY CLEANING SOLVENT IS A POISONOUS
AND FLAMMABLE SOLVENT WHICH CAN CAUSE INJURY TO PERSONS
AND DAMAGE TO EQUIPMENT .
(a) Remove the loose dirt, grease, and oil with cotton wiper, G00034, that is moist with
solvent, B00074, or solvent, B00083.
1) Do not put solvent on an area that is larger than necessary.
(b) Clean the surface with a cotton wiper, G00034, soft paint brush, STD-1080, or a sponge,
G00116, that is moist with solvent, B00074, or solvent, B00083.
1) Rub the surface to remove all of the dirt.
NOTE: You can use a spray gun to help clean the surface.
(c) Flush the surface with clean solvent, B00074, or solvent, B00083.
(d) Remove all unwanted solvent with a cotton wiper, G00034.
51-21-21
(e) Let the surface dry.
(f) Attach the tags that follow to the left control wheel in the flight deck.
1) "PITOT PROBES COVERED" tag, G02447
2) "STATIC PORTS COVERED" tag, G02444
3) A red tag labeled “AOA SENSORS COVERED.”
(g) Put a pitot probe cover, COM-1503, on the pitot probes.
(h) Use Scotch Brand No.471 tape, G02219, and barricade tape, G02443, to cover the static
ports.
(i) Do the steps that follow to cover the Angle of Attack (AOA) sensors.
1) Cover the AOA sensors with PG-777 solvent-resistant masking tape.
a) Apply PG-777 over the blade, plate, and blade-to-plate gap.
NOTE: This is to allow easier removal of 3M 425 Aluminum Foil T ape,
G00291.
2) Cover the PG-777 solvent-resistant masking tape with 3M 425 Aluminum Foil T ape,
G00291.
a) Shingle the 3M 425 Aluminum Foil T ape, G00291, from the blade-to-plate gap
up to the blade tip until all of the masking tape is covered.
SUBTASK 51-21-21-110-002
(2) Do the steps that follow to clean the area again:
WARNING
DO NOT GET ALIPHATIC NAPHTHA IN YOUR MOUTH, YOUR EYES, OR
ON YOUR SKIN. DO NOT BREATHE THE FUMES FROM THE ALIPHATIC
NAPHTHA. PUT ON PROTECTIVE SPLASH GOGGLES AND GLOVES

AWAY FROM SPARKS, FLAME, AND TEMPERATURES ABOVE THE
FLASHPOINT . ALIPHATIC NAPHTHA IS A POISONOUS AND FLAMMABLE
SOLVENT WHICH CAN CAUSE INJURY TO PERSONS AND DAMAGE TO
EQUIPMENT .
CAUTION
YOU MUST FULLY CLEAN THE SURFACE BEFORE YOU APPLY PAINTS
OR ADHESIVES. USE ALIPHATIC NAPHTHA IN THE LAST STEP OF THE
PROCEDURE TO CLEAN THE SURFACE. DO NOT USE P-D-680 TYPE I
FOR THE LAST STEP OF THE PROCEDURE TO CLEAN THE SURFACE.

THE LAST STEP .
(a) Use a spray bottle to apply the Aliphatic Naphtha solvent, B00083, to the surface.
(b) Clean the surface with a clean cotton wiper, G00034, until all large pieces of dirt are
removed.
NOTE: Use a soft bristle brush to clean if it is necessary.
(c) Clean the surface again until no signs of the dirt are found on a clean cotton wiper,
G00034.
(d) Dry the area with a clean dry cotton wiper, G00034.
1) Do not let drops of solvent dry on the surface.

A. References

(1) Do these steps to put covers on the components:
(a) Make sure that covers are put on the components that follow:
1) Install the pitot probe cover, COM-1503, on the pitot probes.
NOTE: The pitot static probe cover removal/installation pole, SPL-2498, can be
used to install the cover.
a) Attach a "PITOT PROBES COVERED" tag, G02447, to the left control wheel
in the flight deck.
2) Install the angle of attack vane cover, COM-2499, on the angle of attack sensor.
a) Attach a red tag with wire to the left control wheel in the flight deck.
<1> Write “AOA SENSORS COVERED” on the tag.
3) Use Scotch Brand No.471 tape, G02219, and barricade tape, G02443, to cover the
static ports.
a) Attach a "STATIC PORTS COVERED" tag, G02444 to the left control wheel in
the flight deck.
SUBTASK 51-21-21-110-003

51-21-21
(a) The recommended procedure to prepare the metals surfaces listed below is to solvent
clean:
1) Aluminum - Anodized or chemical treated
2) Aluminum (metal sprayed) - chemical treated
3) Magnesium - Dow treated
4) Titanium - Phosphate fluoride treated
5) Steel (except Corrosion Resistant Steel (CRES)) - plated or phosphated.

material.

(b) coating, C00260
(c) coating, C00307

51-21-21
(f) primer, C00175
(g) Do the Procedure 1 as follows:
1) Manual solvent clean the surface.
2) Use abrasive cloth, B00102, 240 grit or finer, to abrade (sand) the surface when it is
necessary.
a) Make sure that you do not cause the substrate to show.
WARNING
DO NOT GET SOLVENTS IN YOUR MOUTH, EYES, OR SKIN AND
DO NOT BREATHE THE FUMES FROM SOLVENTS. SOLVENTS ARE
HAZARDOUS MATERIALS. REFER TO PRODUCT MATERIAL
SAFETY DATA SHEETS (MSDS) AND LOCAL REQUIREMENTS FOR
CORRECT HANDLING PROCEDURES. IF YOU DO NOT OBEY ,
INJURY TO PERSONNEL CAN OCCUR.
3) Manually solvent clean the surface with Series 85 solvent, B01005
(TASK 20-30-85-910-801).
(h) Do the Procedure 2 as follows:
1) Use the cleaners compound, B00314, or cleaner, B00402. Dilute the cleaners to 1
part cleaner to 10 parts of water by volume.
NOTE: The following cleaners are optional for cleaner, B00402: Cee Bee 280
cleaner, B00005, Oakite 204 cleaner, B00008, and Calla 301A cleaner,
B00015.
2) Clean the surface with cotton wiper, G00034, soft paint brush, STD-1080, or a
sponge, G00116, that is moist with cleaners compound, B00314, or cleaner,
B00402.
a) Do not clean too large an area at one time.
3) Let the cleaner soak for approximately 5 minutes.
a) Do not let the cleaner dry on the surface because it can cause the surface to
be stained.
4) Flush the area thoroughly with clean warm water (120°F (49°C) maximum).
a) Apply the water by pressure spray if it is available.
b) Flush the area to remove cleaner residue.
5) Make sure that all of the cleaner is removed.
6) Reclean the area if it is necessary to make sure that all of the dirt has been
removed.
a) Pressure clean with a non-atomizing spray gun.
<1> You must use a minimum impingement time of 5 seconds, with a
maximum stand time of 15 minutes.
<2> Do not let the cleaner dry on the surface.
7) Flush the surface thoroughly with clean water (hot water preferred) to remove all of
the cleaner residues.
NOTE: Pressure spray or an air-water rinse is recommended.
a) Make sure that all of the cleaner has been removed with a check of the run-off
with pH paper.
NOTE: The pH must be the same as tap water.
51-21-21
WARNING
DO NOT GET SOLVENTS IN YOUR MOUTH, EYES, OR SKIN AND
DO NOT BREATHE THE FUMES FROM SOLVENTS. SOLVENTS ARE
HAZARDOUS MATERIALS. REFER TO PRODUCT MATERIAL
SAFETY DATA SHEETS (MSDS) AND LOCAL REQUIREMENTS FOR
CORRECT HANDLING PROCEDURES. IF YOU DO NOT OBEY ,
INJURY TO PERSONNEL CAN OCCUR.
8) Manual solvent clean the surface with Series 85 solvent, B01005
(TASK 20-30-85-910-801).
9) Less than 48 hours after you have cleaned the surface with alkaline cleaners, clean
the surface with one of the steps that follow:
NOTE: Use a 1 to 1 solution of De Soto Oxsolv and water (or an applicable
equivalent).
a) Manually clean the surface.
b) Pressure clean the surface with a non-atomizing spray gun.
<1> You must use a minimum impingement time of 5 seconds, with a
maximum stand time of 2 minutes.
<2> Do not let the cleaner dry on the surface.
10) Use cotton wiper, G00034, to remove wet solvent.
11) Solvent clean with PR-148 (clear) Adhesion Promoter solvent, B00635, with one of
the steps that follow:
a) Manually clean the surface.
b) Pressure clean the surface with a non-atomizing spray gun.
<1> You must use a minimum impingement time of 5 seconds, with a
maximum stand time of 2 minutes.
12) Blow dry the surface with filtered compressed air.
(i) Do the Procedure 3 as follows:
1) Clean the surface.
NOTE: Abrasive pads (Scotch-brite) are recommended as an aid to remove
stubborn dirt.
a) Flush the surface with water until you get a water break-free surface.
NOTE: A water break-free surface will let a continuous layer of water stay on
the surface for 30 seconds.
b) Make sure that you flush all unwanted material from the joints and fasteners.
2) Thoroughly flush the surface with clean water to remove all remaining sanded and
cleaner material.
a) If you do not get a water-break-free surface, clean the surface again.
3) If the substrate shows, use the applicable specifications to apply a new surface
layer.
4) Thoroughly dry the surface before it is painted.
SUBTASK 51-21-21-100-004
(5) Prepare all adhesive primers with one of the three procedures that follow:
(a) Do the Procedure 1.
51-21-21

a) Make sure that you do not cause the substrate to show.

(b) Do the Procedure 2 as follows:
1) Use the cleaners MIL-C-43616 or cleaner, B00402. Dilute the cleaners with 1 part

NOTE: The following cleaners are optional for cleaner, B00402: Cee Bee 280
cleaner, B00005, Oakite 204 cleaner, B00008, and Calla 301A cleaner,
B00015.
2) Clean the surface with a cotton wiper, G00034, soft paint brush, STD-1080, or a
sponge, G00116, that is moist with cleaner compound, B00314, or cleaner, B00402.
a) Do not clean too large an area at one time.
3) Let the cleaner soak for approximately 5 minutes.
a) Do not let the cleaner dry on the surface because it can cause the surface to
be stained.
4) Flush the area thoroughly with clean warm water (120°F (49°C) maximum).
a) Apply the water by pressure spray if it is applicable.
b) Flush the area a minimum of 3 times.
5) Make sure that all of the cleaner is removed.
6) Reclean the area if it is necessary to make sure that all of the dirt has been
removed.
a) Pressure clean with a non-atomizing spray gun.
<1> You must use a minimum impingement time of 5 seconds, with a
maximum stand time of 15 minutes.
<2> Do not let the cleaner dry on the surface.
7) Flush the surface thoroughly with clean water (hot water preferred) to remove all of
the cleaner residues.
NOTE: Pressure spray or an air-water rinse is recommended.
a) Make sure that all of the cleaner has been removed with a check of the run-off
with pH paper.
NOTE: The pH must be the same as tap water.

D. Cleaning and Preparation of External Plastic Surfaces
SUBTASK 51-21-21-100-007
(1) Prepare the Carbon Epoxy and Fiberglass/Epoxy surfaces for paint.
(a) Mask the areas adjacent to the area that will be painted.
1) Attach the tags that follow to the left control wheel in the flight deck.
a) "PITOT PROBES COVERED" tag, G02447
b) "STATIC PORTS COVERED" tag, G02444
c) A red tag labeled “AOA SENSORS COVERED.”
2) Put a pitot probe cover, COM-1503, on the pitot probes.
3) Use Scotch Brand No.471 tape, G02219, and barricade tape, G02443, to cover the
static ports.
4) Do the steps that follow to cover the Angle of Attack (AOA) sensors.
a) Cover the AOA sensors with PG-777 solvent-resistant masking tape.
<1> Apply PG-777 over the blade, plate, and blade-to-plate gap.
NOTE: This is to allow easier removal of 3M 425 Aluminum Foil T ape,
G00291.
b) Cover the PG-777 solvent-resistant masking tape with 3M 425 Aluminum Foil
T ape, G00291.
<1> Shingle the 3M 425 Aluminum Foil T ape, G00291, from the
blade-to-plate gap up to the blade tip until all of the masking tape is
covered.

(b) Clean the surface with a clean Series 87 solvent, B01007 (TASK 20-30-87-910-801).
(c) Abrade (sand) the surface with one of the steps that follow:

material removal to the first paint topcoat layer.
(d) Remove the sanding residue with one of the steps that follow:

1) Clean the surface with a clean cotton wiper, G00034, that is moist with Series 87

a) Use compressed air to help remove the remaining loose material before the

2) Clean the surface again if it is necessary.

(e) Examine the surface to determine if a pin hole filler or surfacer is necessary.

if it is necessary.
NOTE: Only use Static Conditioner Filler 28-C-1 (or an applicable equivalent) to fill the
pinholes.
1) Do not apply a continuous layer of pinhole filler because it will cause an
unsatisfactory paint bond.
(g) If it is necessary, apply 825X466 primer-surfacer, A50597 (or an applicable equivalent) or
Surfacer adhesive, A00028, to fill:
NOTE: The surfacers are to be used only in the area of the pits in the surface or
depressions. The surfacers are usually not used on the full part.
1) pits in the surface,
2) areas with resin missing, and
3) other minor depressions that cannot be filled with the pinhole fillers.

E. Cleaning and Preparation of Internal Plastic Surfaces
SUBTASK 51-21-21-100-008
(1) Solvent clean the internal plastic surfaces.
(a) Clean the surface with a clean cotton wiper, G00034, that is moist with solvent, B00148,
solvent, B00062, or alcohol.
1) Clean BMS8-317 surfaces with presealing cleaning solvent, B00184.
SUBTASK 51-21-21-370-002
(2) Prepare the internal decorative surfaces for paint.
(a) Manually abrade (sand) the internal decorative surfaces with 150 grit or finer abrasive
cloth, B00102.
(b) Remove the sanding residue with the step that follows:
1) Clean the surface with a clean cotton wiper, G00034, that is moist with solvent,
B00148, solvent, B00062, or alcohol.
(c) Examine the surface to determine if a surfacer is necessary.
(d) Apply the Static Conditioner Filler 28-C-1 (or an applicable equivalent) to fill the pinholes
if it is necessary.
NOTE: Only use Static Conditioner Filler 28-C-1 (or an applicable equivalent) to fill the
pinholes.
1) Do not apply a continuous layer of pinhole filler because it will cause an
unsatisfactory paint bond.
(e) If it is necessary, apply 825X466 primer-surfacer, A50597 (or an applicable equivalent) or
Surfacer adhesive, A00028, to fill:
NOTE: The surfacers are to be used only in the area of the pits in the surface or
depressions. Usually, surfacers are not used on the full part.
1) Pits in the surface.
a) Use Scotch-Weld EC-3501 Epoxy Adhesive, A50362, for defects more than
0.002 in. (0.051 mm) deep.
2) Areas with resin missing.
3) Other minor depressions that cannot be filled with the pinhole fillers.
(f) Do the final cleaning of the surface with a clean cotton wiper, G00034, that is moist with
solvent, B00148, solvent, B00062, or alcohol.
51-21-21
(g) Fully dry the surface with a clean cotton wiper, G00034, before the cleaner becomes dry.
SUBTASK 51-21-21-370-003
(3) Prepare the internal non-decorative surfaces for paint.
(a) Manually abrade (sand) the internal non-decorative surfaces with 150 grit or finer
abrasive cloth, B00102.
(b) Remove the sanding residue with the step that follows:
1) Clean the surface with a clean cotton wiper, G00034, that is moist with solvent,
B00148, solvent, B00062, or alcohol.
(c) Examine the surface to determine if a surfacer is necessary.
(d) Apply the Static Conditioner Filler 28-C-1 (or an applicable equivalent) to fill the pinholes
if it is necessary.
NOTE: Only use Static Conditioner Filler 28-C-1 (or an applicable equivalent) to fill the
pinholes.
1) Do not apply a continuous layer of pinhole filler because it will cause an
unsatisfactory paint bond.
(e) If it is necessary, apply 825X466 primer-surfacer, A50597 (or an applicable equivalent) or
Surfacer adhesive, A00028, to fill:
NOTE: The surfacers are to be used only in the area of the pits in the surface or
depression. Usually, surfacers are not used on the full part.
1) Pits in the surface.
2) Areas with resin missing.
3) Other minor depressions that cannot be filled with the pinhole fillers.
(f) Do the final cleaning of the surface with a clean cotton wiper, G00034, that is moist with
solvent, B00148.
(g) Fully dry the surface with a clean cotton wiper, G00034, before the cleaner becomes dry.

D. Prepare For The Removal Of Corrosion
SUBTASK 51-21-31-160-002
(1) Make sure that all areas that are prepared for corrosion inhibiting compounds are clean.
(2) Remove oil, grease, dirt, and paint from the surface or the part.
(3) Make sure you remove all corrosion material from the surfaces or parts. Failure to remove all
corrosion material can permit corrosion to start again. For limits of corrosion removal, refer to
the Structural Repair Manual.
(4) If you cannot immediately remove corrosion from an area, do this task: Surface Preparation for
Corrosion Inhibiting Compound, TASK 51-21-91-620-801.
E. Procedure (Chemical)
SUBTASK 51-21-31-913-001
(1) Do the steps that follow to prepare the airplane for chemical corrosion removal from the
external surface of the airplane:
(a) If necessary, put the airplane in an area where you can fully flush all the surfaces with
water, G50256 quickly.
(b) Apply 3M 425 Aluminum Foil T ape, G00291 tape to hinges, mating surfaces, access
doors and other openings that can allow the corrosion removing compound, E50003 to
damage other metal types.
NOTE: This can be the pitot static probes, static ports, angle of attack (AOA) vanes, and
total air temperature (TAT) probe.
(c) Apply 3M 425 Aluminum Foil T ape, G00291 to areas of magnesium, steel and cadmium
plated parts.
NOTE: This will prevent the accidental corrosion of different metal types.
SUBTASK 51-21-31-150-001
(2) Do the steps that follow to remove aluminum corrosion from the external surface of the
airplane with corrosion removing compound, E50003:
51-21-31
(a) Apply corrosion removing compound, E50003 with any of the items that follow:
1) Use a brush, G00268 to apply the compound.
2) Use a sponge, G00116 to apply the compound.
3) Use a lint-free cloth, G00834 to apply the compound.
(b) Apply the compound at the lowest area and work up into a circular movement.
NOTE: This will give a good layer and dwell time for the compound on the surface.
(c) Let the compound to stay on the surface for approximately 12 minutes and do the step
that follows:
NOTE: Compound applied warm (140°F (60°C) maximum temperature) on the surface
gives better results.
1) Rinse with water, G50256.
2) Do again this procedure to remove more corrosion, if necessary.
3) Dry the treated surface and do the steps in CHEMICAL CONVERSION COATING -
CLEANING/PAINTING, PAGEBLOCK 51-21-41/701.
SUBTASK 51-21-31-150-002
(3) Do the steps that follow, to remove small quantities of aluminum corrosion from the external
surface of the airplane with acid, B00046:
NOTE: This procedure is optional.
(a) Fully flush the surface with water.
WARNING
DO NOT ADD WATER TO ACID. ALWAYS ADD ACID TO WATER. ACID IS
EXPLOSIVE WHEN WATER IS ADDED TO IT . IF YOU ADD WATER TO
ACID, INJURIES TO PERSONNEL AND DAMAGE TO EQUIPMENT CAN
OCCUR.
(b) Add 1 part (by volume) of the corrosion remover, phosphoric acid, B00046 to 3 parts (by
volume) of water, G50256.
(c) Apply corrosion remover phosphoric acid, B00046 on the surface.
(d) Fully flush all of the prepared area and the adjacent areas, with water, G50256.
NOTE: Make sure that you flush out all the phosphoric acid corrosion remover from the
areas that follow: the skin seams, and all openings or holes on the surface.
(e) Do again this procedure to remove more corrosion, if necessary.
(f) Dry the treated surface and do the steps in CHEMICAL CONVERSION COATING -
CLEANING/PAINTING, PAGEBLOCK 51-21-41/701.
F . Procedure (Mechanical Method)
SUBTASK 51-21-31-913-002
(1) Do the steps that follow to prepare the airplane for mechanical corrosion removal from the
external surface of the airplane:
(a) If necessary, put the airplane in an area where you can fully flush all the surfaces with
water, G50256 quickly.
(b) Apply 3M 425 Aluminum Foil T ape, G00291 tape to hinges, mating surfaces, access
doors and other openings that can allow the corrosion by-products to damage other metal
types.
51-21-31
(c) Apply 3M 425 Aluminum Foil T ape, G00291 to areas of magnesium, steel and cadmium
plated parts.
SUBTASK 51-21-31-160-003
(2) Do any of the steps that follow to remove aluminum corrosion from the external surface of the
airplane (Non-powered tools and materials):
(a) Use a abrasive mat, G00251 to rub away corrosion.
(b) Use a abrasive cloth, B00102 to rub away corrosion.
(c) Use a 240 grit or finer abrasive paper, G50077 to rub away corrosion.
(d) Use aluminum wool, G50590 to rub away corrosion.
NOTE: Metallic wools are available in five grades, ranging from very fine to extra
coarse.
(e) Use a brush, G02414 to brush away corrosion.
CAUTION
DO NOT USE ABRASIVE PADS (SCOTCH-BRITE), OR ABRASIVE
PAPER ON THE ALUMINUM SURFACE UNLESS THE SCRIBE LINE
INSPECTIONS WERE MADE. ABRASIVE PADS CAN SMOOTH THE
ALUMINUM SURFACE AND HIDE THE SCRIBE LINE MARKS. IF YOU
USE ABRASIVE PADS TO REMOVE THE PAINT OR PRIMER
BEFORE YOU DO THE SCRIBE LINE INSPECTIONS, YOU CAN BE
REQUIRED TO DO REPEAT INSPECTIONS BASED ON THE SCRIBE
LINE INSPECTION SERVICE BULLETIN.
1) Remove the corrosion with a linear motion; do not crosshatch. This will
unnecessarily damage the surface.
2) After wire brushing soft metal (such as aluminum or magnesium) the surface areas
must be polished with a abrasive mat, G00251 or abrasive cloth, B00102.
(f) Use pumice abrasive, B00166 to brush away corrosion.
1) Mix with water, G50256 and rub over the area with a soft cotton cloth, G50138.
2) After drying, the pumice abrasive, B00166 powder is wiped off.
CAUTION
DO NOT USE ABRASIVE PADS (SCOTCH-BRITE), OR ABRASIVE PAPER
ON THE ALUMINUM SURFACE UNLESS THE SCRIBE LINE
INSPECTIONS WERE MADE. ABRASIVE PADS CAN SMOOTH THE
ALUMINUM SURFACE AND HIDE THE SCRIBE LINE MARKS. IF YOU
USE ABRASIVE PADS TO REMOVE THE PAINT OR PRIMER BEFORE
YOU DO THE SCRIBE LINE INSPECTIONS, YOU CAN BE REQUIRED TO
DO REPEAT INSPECTIONS BASED ON THE SCRIBE LINE INSPECTION
SERVICE BULLETIN.
CAUTION
ONLY USE APPROVED SCRAPERS ON THE AIRPLANE SKIN.
SCRAPERS THAT ARE NOT APPROVED CAN MAKE SCRATCHES ON
THE SKIN, AND CAUSE FATIGUE CRACKS.
(g) Scrapers are used primarily for the initial removal of heavy corrosion deposits in corners
and crevices that cannot be reached with other equipment.
51-21-31
1) A sealant removal tool, COM-2481 and sealant removal tool handle, COM-4746
may be used on any metal surface.
NOTE: If you use abrasive pads before you do scribe line inspections, you can be
required to do repeat inspections based on the scribe line inspections
Service Bulletin 737-53A1289.
a) If necessary, do these steps to sharpen the scraper:
<1> Sharpen the scraper with sandpaper, G50260 or finer by hand or by
metal sharpening wheel (scraper sharpener, COM-4747).
<2> If you sharpen the scraper by hand, rub the sharpened scraper on
wiper, G50262 to remove loose sanding particles.
<3> If you use a metal sharpening wheel, rub the sharpened scraper on
sandpaper, G50261 or finer aluminum oxide paper to remove debris,
and then rub it on wiper, G50262 to remove loose sanding particles.
2) Aluminum scrapers shall be used only on aluminum or magnesium surfaces.
3) Steel scrapers shall be used only on steel surfaces.
SUBTASK 51-21-31-160-004
(3) After the removal of the corrosion, do the steps that follow:
(a) Rinse with water, G50256.
(b) Dry the treated surface and do the steps in CHEMICAL CONVERSION COATING -
CLEANING/PAINTING, PAGEBLOCK 51-21-41/701.

3. Removal and Control of Corrosion for Magnesium Alloys
(Table 701, Table 702)
A. References
Reference Title

51-21-21-100-801 Clean the Surface to be Painted (P/B 701)
SRM 51-10-02 Structural Repair Manual
B. Tools/Equipment

Reference Description Specification
B00047 Acid - T echnical Grade, Nitric (61%-68.2%
Purity)
A-A-59105
B00102 Abrasive - Aluminum Oxide Coated Cloth
B00137 Abrasive - Garnet Coated Paper
B00166 Abrasive - pumice
C00167 Acid, Chromic (Chromium Trioxide) A-A-55827
51-21-31
(Continued)
Reference Description Specification
G00034 Cotton Wiper - Process Cleaning Absorbent
Wiper (Cheesecloth, Gauze)
AMS3819 Class 1
Grade A or B Form 1
(Supersede BMS15-5
CL A)
G02222 Abrasive - Dry, Blasting BAC5748

D. Procedure
SUBTASK 51-21-31-350-003
WARNING
SMALL PARTICLES AND FINE SHAVINGS OF MAGNESIUM START FIRES
EASILY AND ARE EXTREMELY FLAMMABLE. MAGNESIUM DUST CAN
CAUSE EXPLOSIONS. WATER IN CONTACT WITH BURNING MAGNESIUM IS
A STEAM EXPLOSION HAZARD. EXTINGUISH FIRES WITH VERY DRY TALC,
CALCIUM CARBONATE, SAND, OR GRAPHITE BY APPLYING THE POWDER
TO A DEPTH OF 1/2 INCH OR MORE OVER THE BURNING METAL. DO NOT
USE FOAM, WATER, CARBON TETRACHLORIDE, OR CARBON DIOXIDE TO
EXTINGUISH FIRES OF MAGNESIUM. MAGNESIUM FIRES CAN CAUSE
INJURY TO PERSONS OR DAMAGE TO EQUIPMENT .
(1) Do these steps to mechanically remove magnesium alloy corrosion from the airplane:
(a) Make sure the metal is a magnesium alloy.
(b) During the removal process, protect the surrounding area from the corrosion dust.
(c) Remove any grease, oil, or paint from the area; do this task: Clean the Surface to be
Painted, TASK 51-21-21-100-801.
(d) Measure the corroded area to find the amount of corrosion damage.
NOTE: Light corrosion will measure a maximum depth of 0.001 inch (0.025 mm).
Moderate corrosion will measure a maximum depth of 0.010 inch (0.254 mm).
Heavy corrosion will measure deeper than 0.010 inch (0.254 mm).
(e) Remove loose corrosion with aluminum wool.
(f) Remove the corrosion, based on the amount of damage:
CAUTION
DO NOT USE A CARBON STEEL BRUSH OR STEEL WOOL OR
SILICONE CARBIDE ABRASIVE ON MAGNESIUM SURFACES. TINY
DISSIMILAR METAL PARTICLES WILL CAUSE FURTHER
CORROSION.
1) Remove light corrosion by hand sanding with an aluminum oxide abrasive cloth,
B00102 or a garnet abrasive, B00137.
2) Remove light corrosion with pumice abrasive, B00166 paste.
a) Mix the pumice abrasive, B00166 with water to make the paste.
b) Use a clean cotton wiper, G00034 to apply and gently rub the pumice paste on
the surface.
c) When the pumice abrasive, B00166 is a dry, white powder, wipe off with a
clean, dry cotton wiper, G00034.
d) Remove the remaining corrosion with #600 grit abrasive cloth, B00102 and
water.
51-21-31
CAUTION
DO NOT USE EXCESSIVE RUBBING. HEAVY , CONTINUOUS
RUBBING (SUCH AS WITH POWER-DRIVEN WIRE BRUSHES) CAN
MAKE ENOUGH HEAT TO CAUSE CHANGES IN METALLIC
PROPERTIES. CHANGES IN METALLIC PROPERTIES CAN CAUSE
MORE CORROSION.
3) Use one of these steps to remove moderate and heavy corrosion :
a) #400 grit abrasive cloth, B00102
b) Use a brush, G02414 with bristles no more than 0.003 inch (0.076 mm)
diameter and no less than 1.0 inch (25.4 mm) long.
NOTE: After using the brush, polish the surface with #400 grit abrasive cloth,
B00102 and then #600 grit abrasive cloth, B00102.
CAUTION
DO NOT USE TOO MUCH PRESSURE FOR ABRASIVE
BLASTING. AIR PRESSURE FOR ABRASIVE BLASTING ON
MAGNESIUM ALLOYS MUST BE 10 TO 35 PSI (69-241 KPA).
TOO MUCH PRESSURE CAN CAUSE DAMAGE TO PARTS.
c) Glass bead dry blasting abrasive, G02222 (170 to 400 grit).
WARNING
DO NOT GET CHROMIC ACID IN YOUR MOUTH, OR YOUR EYES, OR
ON YOUR SKIN. PUT ON AN APRON, SPLASH GOGGLES, AND GLOVES
WHEN YOU USE CHROMIC ACID. CHROMIC ACID CAN CAUSE INJURY
TO PERSONS OR DAMAGE TO EQUIPMENT .
WARNING
DO NOT ADD WATER TO ACID. ALWAYS ADD ACID TO WATER. IF YOU
ADD WATER TO ACID, YOU CAN CAUSE INJURY TO PERSONS OR
DAMAGE TO EQUIPMENT .
(g) After removing all corrosion that can be seen, apply a corrosion treating solution of
chromium trioxide acid, C00167 or sodium dichromate with nitric acid, B00047, (SOPM
20-43-02).
(h) Rinse the part well with clean water.
(i) Smooth out repaired areas using Corrosion Damage and Rework Limits (SRM 51-10-02).
(j) Clean the repaired area.
(k) Measure the depth of the blends using Corrosion Damage and Rework Limits (SRM
51-10-02).
(l) Treat the repaired area using Standard Treatment Methods (STANDARD TREATMENT
METHODS, SUBJECT 51-00-58).
SUBTASK 51-21-31-350-004
(2) Do the following steps to chemically remove the magnesium alloy corrosion from the external
surface of the airplane:
(a) Make sure the metal is a magnesium alloy.
(b) Remove any grease, oil, and loose paint from the area, do this task: Clean the Surface to
be Painted, TASK 51-21-21-100-801.
(c) Protect the areas surrounding the corroded area.
NOTE: Protect rubber parts, bearings, cast or pressed inserts, cracks, and plated steel.
51-21-31
WARNING
DO NOT GET CHROMIC ACID IN YOUR MOUTH, OR YOUR EYES, OR
ON YOUR SKIN. PUT ON AN APRON, SPLASH GOGGLES, AND GLOVES
WHEN YOU USE CHROMIC ACID. CHROMIC ACID CAN CAUSE INJURY
TO PERSONS OR DAMAGE TO EQUIPMENT .
WARNING
DO NOT ADD WATER TO ACID. ALWAYS ADD ACID TO WATER. IF YOU
ADD WATER TO ACID, YOU CAN CAUSE INJURY TO PERSONS OR
DAMAGE TO EQUIPMENT .
(d) Prepare one of these chromic acid solutions according to manufacturer's directions,
(SOPM 20-43-02):
1) Chromium trioxide acid, C00167, or
2) Sodium dichromate with nitric acid, B00047.
(e) Apply with acid resistant medium nylon bristle brush, STD-3911.
NOTE: Start from the lower surfaces and work upward to prevent runs and streaks.
Apply with circular movement.
(f) Keep the solution on the surface for 15 minutes. Wipe dry.
NOTE: While the solution is on the surface, continue to brush over the surface again with
a short fiber brush to prevent the solution from drying and streaking.
(g) Rinse with water while scrubbing with brush. Wipe dry.
NOTE: Repeat this sequence until the bright metallic color of the metal is shown.
(h) Smooth out repaired areas using Corrosion Damage and Rework Limits (SRM 51-10-02).
(i) Measure the depth of the repaired area using Corrosion Damage and Rework Limits
(SRM 51-10-02).
(j) Treat the repaired area using Standard Treatment Methods (STANDARD TREATMENT
METHODS, SUBJECT 51-00-58).

4. Removal and Control of Corrosion for Carbon Steel
(Table 701, Table 702)
A. References
Reference Title
51-00-58 STANDARD TREATMENT METHODS
51-21-21-100-801 Clean the Surface to be Painted (P/B 701)
SRM 51-10-02 Structural Repair Manual
B. Consumable Materials
Reference Description Specification
B00046 Acid - Corrosion Removing, Metal
Conditioning, Phosphoric
MIL-C-10578
B00102 Abrasive - Aluminum Oxide Coated Cloth
G02222 Abrasive - Dry, Blasting BAC5748
G02414 Brush - Stainless Steel
51-21-31
C. Procedure
SUBTASK 51-21-31-350-005
(1) Do these steps to mechanically remove corrosion from carbon steel parts of the airplane:
NOTE: When the heat-treat value of a part is unknown, repair the part as if it was heat-treated
over 220,000 psi (1516847 kPa).
(a) Make sure the metal is carbon steel and find its heat treat value.
(b) During the removal process, protect the surrounding area from the corrosion dust.
(c) Remove any grease, oil, and loose paint from the area; do this task: Clean the Surface to
be Painted, TASK 51-21-21-100-801.
WARNING
YOU SHOULD BE VERY CAREFUL WHEN USING TOOLS ON HIGH
STRENGTH STEEL SO THAT THE STEEL SURFACE DOES NOT GET
TOO HOT . HOT STEEL CAN CAUSE INJURY TO PERSONS.
(d) Remove corrosion by any of these methods:
NOTE: Hand-held power tools must mot be used on high strength steels heat-treated to
220,000 psi (1516847 kPa) and above.
1) Use a brush, G02414 with bristles no larger than 0.010 inch (0.254 mm) in diameter,
NOTE: After using the brush, polish the surface with #400 grit abrasive cloth,
B00102 and then #600 grit abrasive cloth, B00102.
2) Grinding with an abrasive wheel,
CAUTION
AIR PRESSURE MUST BE 40 TO 70 PSI (276-483 KPA) FOR
ABRASIVE BLASTING ON CARBON STEEL HEAT-TREATED TO
220,000 PSI (1516847 KPA) AND ABOVE. THIS CAN CAUSE
DAMAGE TO EQUIPMENT .
3) Glass bead dry blasting abrasive, G02222, or
4) Motor-driven wire brush.
(e) Make sure all corrosion is removed.
(f) Smooth out repaired areas using Corrosion Damage and Rework Limits (SRM 51-10-02).
(g) Measure the depth of the repaired areas using Corrosion Damage and Rework Limits
(SRM 51-10-02).
(h) Treat the repaired area using Standard Treatment Methods (STANDARD TREATMENT
METHODS, SUBJECT 51-00-58).
SUBTASK 51-21-31-350-006
(2) Do the following steps to chemically remove corrosion from carbon steel parts of the airplane:
NOTE: When the heat-treat value of a part is unknown, repair the part as if it was heat-treated
over 220,000 psi (1516847 kPa).
NOTE: The use of acid can cause hydrogen embrittlement on steel parts heat-treated above
220,000 psi (1516847 kPa). The use of acid is not allowed on steel parts heat-treated
above 220,000 psi (1516847 kPa).
(a) Make sure the metal is carbon steel and find its heat treat value.
(b) During the removal process, protect the surrounding area from the corrosion dust.
51-21-31
(c) Remove any grease, oil, and loose paint from the area, do this task: Clean the Surface to
be Painted, TASK 51-21-21-100-801.
(d) Remove heavy rust by hand using a chipper or a brush, G02414.
WARNING
DO NOT GET PHOSPHORIC ACID IN YOUR MOUTH, OR YOUR EYES,
OR ON YOUR SKIN. WEAR AN APRON, GOGGLES, AND ACID
RESISTANT GLOVES WHEN YOU USE PHOSPHORIC ACID.
PHOSPHORIC ACID CAN CAUSE INJURY TO PERSONS OR DAMAGE
TO EQUIPMENT .
WARNING
DO NOT ADD WATER TO ACID. ALWAYS ADD ACID TO WATER. IF YOU
ADD WATER TO ACID, YOU CAN CAUSE INJURY TO PERSONS OR
DAMAGE TO EQUIPMENT .
(e) Use one of the following methods for chemical removal of corrosion:
1) Inhibited phosphoric acid, B00046 - (Brush-on Method)
NOTE: Acid type removers are used for removing rust or black oxide.
2) Inhibited phosphoric acid, B00046 - (Soak-in Method)
NOTE: Acid type removers are used for removing rust or black oxide.
NOTE: This method is used for removing heavy rust from parts removed from the
airplane and where the corroded area can be put fully into the acid base.
WARNING
THE ALKALI RUST REMOVER IS HIGHLY ALKALINE AND HARMFUL
TO SKIN AND EYES. WEAR RUBBER GLOVES, APRONS, AND
GOGGLES WHEN WORKING WITH THIS MATERIAL. MAKE SURE
THAT THE AREA WHERE YOU WILL USE THIS MATERIAL HAS
GOOD AIRFLOW. THE ALKALI RUST REMOVER CAN CAUSE
INJURY TO PERSONS OR DAMAGE TO EQUIPMENT .
3) Alkali Type Rust Remover - (Soak-in Method)
NOTE: Alkali type removers are used for removing red rust.
(f) If there is any remaining corrosion, do the rust removing method again.
(g) Smooth out repaired areas using Corrosion Damage and Rework Limits (SRM 51-10-02).
(h) Measure the depth of the repaired areas using Corrosion Damage and Rework Limits
(SRM 51-10-02).
(i) Treat the repaired area using Standard Treatment Methods (STANDARD TREATMENT
METHODS, SUBJECT 51-00-58).

5. Removal and Control of Corrosion for Stainless Steel and Nickel-Chromium Alloys
(Table 701, Table 702)
A. Procedure
SUBTASK 51-21-31-350-007
CAUTION
ABRASIVE BLASTING OR GRINDING SHALL NOT BE USED ON THIN WALL
TUBES OR THIN WEBS LESS THAN 0.0625 INCH (1.5875 MM) THICK. THESE
METHODS CAN DAMAGE THE STRUCTURE.
(1) Remove corrosion mechanically by using the same general procedures as for carbon steel; do
this task: Removal and Control of Corrosion for Carbon Steel, TASK 51-21-31-350-803.
SUBTASK 51-21-31-350-008
(2) Remove corrosion chemically by using the same general procedures as for carbon steel; do
this task: Removal and Control of Corrosion for Carbon Steel, TASK 51-21-31-350-803.
NOTE: Only use chemical removers on stainless steel and nickel-chromium alloys in very
corroded areas and where the chemicals will not be trapped in cracks, lap joints,
crevices, and other such areas.

(Table 701, Table 702)

C. Procedure
SUBTASK 51-21-31-350-009
WARNING
SMALL CHIPS OR SLIVERS OF A TITANIUM ALLOY , CAUSED BY A
MACHINING PROCESS ARE VERY FLAMMABLE. EXTINGUISH THESE
FIRES WITH VERY DRY TALC, CALCIUM CARBONATE, SAND, OR
GRAPHITE. DO NOT USE WATER, CARBON DIOXIDE, CARBON
TETRACHLORIDE OR THE ORDINARY CHEMICAL FIRE EXTINGUISHER.
TITANIUM FIRES CAN CAUSE INJURY TO PERSONS OR DAMAGE TO
EQUIPMENT .
(1) Remove corrosion mechanically from a titanium alloy by these steps:
(a) Make sure the metal is a titanium alloy.
51-21-31
(b) Remove any grease, oil, or paint from the area, do this task: Clean the Surface to be
Painted, TASK 51-21-21-100-801.
(c) Apply aluminum polish by hand with a cotton wiper, G00034. Do this until all corrosion is
removed.
(d) Remove the polish with a clean cotton wiper, G00034.
(e) Treat repaired area using Standard Treatment Methods (STANDARD TREATMENT
METHODS, SUBJECT 51-00-58).
SUBTASK 51-21-31-350-010
(2) Removal of corrosion by chemicals is not allowed on titanium alloys.
NOTE: Titanium can become brittle when exposed to acid solutions. Chemical removers are
not allowed.

7. Removal and Control of Corrosion for Plated or Phosphated Surfaces
(Table 701, Table 702)
A. References
Reference Title
51-00-58 STANDARD TREATMENT METHODS
51-21-21-100-801 Clean the Surface to be Painted (P/B 701)
B. Consumable Materials
Reference Description Specification
B00046 Acid - Corrosion Removing, Metal
Conditioning, Phosphoric
MIL-C-10578
C00167 Acid, Chromic (Chromium Trioxide) A-A-55827
G02414 Brush - Stainless Steel
C. Procedure
SUBTASK 51-21-31-350-011
(1) Remove corrosion from cadmium plated surfaces by the following steps:
(a) During the removal process, protect the surrounding area from the corrosion dust and
contamination.
(b) Remove any grease, oil, or paint from the area, do this task: Clean the Surface to be
Painted, TASK 51-21-21-100-801.
(c) If corrosion is only on the plated surface, repair as follows (if corrosion has gone through
the plated surface, refer to steps below):
1) Rub gently with stainless steel wool.
NOTE: Removal work on plated surfaces should be kept to a minimum. This will
make sure of the preservation of the adjacent plating.
WARNING
DO NOT GET CHROMIC ACID IN YOUR MOUTH, OR YOUR EYES,
OR ON YOUR SKIN. PUT ON AN APRON, SPLASH GOGGLES, AND
GLOVES WHEN YOU USE CHROMIC ACID. CHROMIC ACID CAN
CAUSE INJURY TO PERSONS OR DAMAGE TO EQUIPMENT .
51-21-31
(WARNING PRECEDES)
WARNING
DO NOT ADD WATER TO ACID. ALWAYS ADD ACID TO WATER. IF
YOU ADD WATER TO ACID, YOU CAN CAUSE INJURY TO
PERSONS OR DAMAGE TO EQUIPMENT .
2) Apply the solution of chromic acid, C00167 and allow to stay on the corroded
surface for 30 to 60 seconds.
NOTE: The solution strength shall be 4 ounces (.12 liter) of chromium trioxide to 1
U.S. gallon (3.8 liters) of water.
3) Rinse part with clean water and air dry.
(d) If corrosion has gone through the plated surface, repair as follows:
1) Decide if the part should be repaired or replaced.
2) If the part can be repaired, identify the base metal and remove corrosion according
to the base metal's applicable procedure.
(e) Treat repaired area using Standard Treatment Methods (STANDARD TREATMENT
METHODS, SUBJECT 51-00-58).
SUBTASK 51-21-31-350-012
(2) Remove corrosion from plated surfaces (that are NOT cadmium plated) by the following steps:
(a) During the removal process, protect the surrounding area from the contaminants.
(b) Remove any grease, oil, and loose paint from the area, do this task: Clean the Surface to
be Painted, TASK 51-21-21-100-801.
(c) Remove heavy rust by hand using a chipper or a brush, G02414 .
WARNING
DO NOT GET PHOSPHORIC ACID IN YOUR MOUTH, OR YOUR EYES,
OR ON YOUR SKIN. PUT ON AN APRON, SPLASH GOGGLES, AND
GLOVES WHEN YOU USE PHOSPHORIC ACID. PHOSPHORIC ACID
CAN CAUSE INJURY TO PERSONS OR DAMAGE TO EQUIPMENT .
WARNING
DO NOT ADD WATER TO ACID. ALWAYS ADD ACID TO WATER. IF YOU
ADD WATER TO ACID, YOU CAN CAUSE INJURY TO PERSONS OR
DAMAGE TO EQUIPMENT .
(d) Apply phosphoric acid, B00046 to corroded area.
NOTE: Allow the acid solution to stay on the corroded surface only long enough to
remove the corrosion.
NOTE: Mix the acid on a 1:1 ratio.
(e) Rinse the part with clean water and air dry.
(f) Treat repaired area using Standard Treatment Methods (STANDARD TREATMENT
METHODS, SUBJECT 51-00-58).
SUBTASK 51-21-31-350-013
(3) To repair phosphated cadmium surfaces refer to the procedures for cadmium plated surfaces.
SUBTASK 51-21-31-350-014
(4) To repair phosphated ferrous alloy surfaces refer to the procedures for carbon steel.

D. Prepare the Surface for Coating
SUBTASK 51-21-41-950-001
(1) Apply masking tape, G51270, or 3M 425 Aluminum Foil T ape, G00291, to all adjacent areas.
NOTE: Do not let the chemical conversion coating solution fall on surfaces that have no tape.
NOTE: Do not apply tape on anodized surfaces.
(a) Attach the tags that follow to the left control wheel in the flight deck.
1) "PITOT PROBES COVERED" tag, G02447
2) "STATIC PORTS COVERED" tag, G02444
3) A red tag labeled “AOA SENSORS COVERED”.
(b) Put a pitot probe cover, COM-1503, on the pitot probe.
(c) Apply masking tape, G51270, or 3M 425 Aluminum Foil T ape, G00291, on the static
ports.
(d) Do the steps that follow to cover the Angle of Attack (AOA) sensors.
1) Cover the angle-of-attack sensors with solvent resistant masking tape, G51270.
a) Apply solvent resistant masking tape, G51270, over the blade, plate, and
blade-to-plate gap.
NOTE: This is to allow easier removal of 3M 425 Aluminum Foil T ape,
G00291.
2) Cover the solvent resistant masking tape, G51270, with 3M 425 Aluminum Foil
T ape, G00291.
51-21-41
a) Shingle the aluminum foil tape from the blade-to-plate gap up to the blade tip
until all of solvent resistant masking tape, G51270, is covered.
SUBTASK 51-21-41-110-005
(2) For polished surfaces, do these steps:
(a) Do these steps until a water-break free condition is achieved:
1) Clean surface with cotton wiper, G00034, sponge, G00116, or cleaning pad,
G51318, moist with a dilute alkaline cleaner, B50094.
2) Use cotton wiper, G00034, or sponge, G00116, soaked with water to remove
alkaline cleaner, B50094, residue.
3) Apply a film of water to the part surface and inspect for a water-break free condition.
(b) Dry the surface prior to conversion coating.
(c) Continue directly with the topic: Procedure - Apply Bonderite M-CR 1001 Solution.
SUBTASK 51-21-41-390-001
(3) Seal all holes and clearances on assemblies that have honeycomb or foam plastic.
(a) Use the applicable sealing or caulking material or use rubber plugs to prevent entrance of
Bonderite M-CR 1001 Aero coating, C50015, solution.
SUBTASK 51-21-41-140-001
(4) Clean the surface with alkaline cleaner, B50094.
(a) Use a clean soft bristle brush, STD-123, or cotton wiper, G00034.
(b) Dry the surface with warm air or rub it.
SUBTASK 51-21-41-100-001
(5) Make sure that there are no particles and corrosion materials on the surface.
SUBTASK 51-21-41-140-002
(6) Use abrasive mat, G00251, or abrasive cloth, B00102, to clean the area to be conversion
coated.
NOTE: The repair area must be free of organic, inorganic, and hydraulic fluid resistant
finishes.
SUBTASK 51-21-41-110-001
WARNING
DO NOT GET SOLVENTS IN YOUR MOUTH, YOUR EYES, OR ON YOUR
SKIN. DO NOT BREATHE THE FUMES FROM SOLVENTS. SOLVENTS ARE
DANGEROUS MATERIALS. SOLVENTS CAN BE FLAMMABLE OR CAUSE
DAMAGE TO THE ENVIRONMENT . REFER TO THE MATERIAL SAFETY DATA
SHEETS (MSDS) AND THE LOCAL SAFETY PRECAUTIONS.
(7) Rub the surface with cotton wiper, G00034.
NOTE: It is permitted to flush the remaining particles and residue with water.
(a) Make cotton wiper, G00034, moist (not soaked) with Series 88 solvent, B01008
(TASK 20-30-88-910-801), or with solvent, B00151.
(b) Clean the surface with a clean cotton wiper, G00034, until no remaining particles and
residue are found.
SUBTASK 51-21-41-110-003
(8) Apply a film of water, G50256, to the part surface.
(a) Inspect the part for a water break-free surface.
51-21-41
SUBTASK 51-21-41-910-001
(9) Let the surface dry.
SUBTASK 51-21-41-100-002
(10) For information on the installation of sealant in the integral fuel tanks (SRM 51-20-05).
E. Procedure - Apply Bonderite M-CR 1001 Aero Solution
NOTE: In areas where the chemical conversion coating may come in contact with BMS 10-20
primer, only Bonderite M-CR 600 Aero coating, C00862, should be used.
NOTE: Keep the room temperature above 65°F (18.3°C). Do not apply in direct sunlight.
NOTE: Before you apply Bonderite M-CR 1001 Aero coating, C50015, you must mix with water to
create a solution.
SUBTASK 51-21-41-370-022
WARNING
DO NOT GET CHEMICAL CONVERSION COATING SOLUTION IN YOUR
MOUTH, IN YOUR EYES, ON YOUR SKIN, OR ON YOUR CLOTHES. DO NOT
BREATHE THE FUMES FROM THIS MATERIAL. AIR MUST FLOW FREELY
THROUGH THE WORK AREA. USE THE NECESSARY RESPIRATORY
PROTECTION. KEEP THIS MATERIAL AWAY FROM SPARKS, FLAME, AND
HEAT . THIS MATERIAL IS POISONOUS AND FLAMMABLE AND CAN CAUSE
INJURY TO PERSONS OR DAMAGE TO EQUIPMENT .
(1) To prepare Bonderite M-CR 1001 Aero coating, C50015 solution, refer to the manufacturers
instructions for the correct mixture ratios.
SUBTASK 51-21-41-370-002
WARNING
DO NOT GET CHEMICAL CONVERSION COATING SOLUTION IN YOUR
MOUTH, IN YOUR EYES, ON YOUR SKIN, OR ON YOUR CLOTHES. DO NOT
BREATHE THE FUMES FROM THIS MATERIAL. AIR MUST FLOW FREELY
THROUGH THE WORK AREA. USE THE NECESSARY RESPIRATORY
PROTECTION. KEEP THIS MATERIAL AWAY FROM SPARKS, FLAME, AND
HEAT . THIS MATERIAL IS POISONOUS AND FLAMMABLE AND CAN CAUSE
INJURY TO PERSONS OR DAMAGE TO EQUIPMENT .
(2) Apply Bonderite M-CR 1001 Aero coating, C50015, solution with a clean cotton wiper, G00034,
medium nylon bristle brush, STD-3911, cellulose sponge, or a mop (cotton).
(a) Keep the part wet with Bonderite M-CR 1001 Aero coating, C50015, for 15-30 seconds
before the color to be produced.
NOTE: Do not let the papers or cloths dry after you apply chemical conversion coating
solutions. They can cause a fire when they are dry. After you use the cloths or
paper soak them in water before you discard them.
NOTE: If the solution breaks up into beads, or if a powdery layer occurs, lightly sand
area with 600-grit paper and apply the coating again.
SUBTASK 51-21-41-170-001
(3) Flush the surface with water or, if parts could catch water, use a cloth or sponge that is moist
with water.
NOTE: Be careful when you rub or dry the surface. Do not remove the coating or cause
scratches on the surface.
51-21-41
SUBTASK 51-21-41-910-002
CAUTION
DO NOT TOUCH THE PART UNTIL IT IS FULLY DRY . THE WET CHEMICAL
CONVERSION COATING SOLUTION IS EASILY DAMAGED.
(4) Air dry the surface.
(a) Touch the dry parts or surface with clean rubber gloves, STD-1086, and keep parts or
surface dry and clean to prevent surface contamination.

WARNING

PROTECTION. KEEP THIS MATERIAL AWAY FROM SPARKS, FLAME, AND

(1) Put Bonderite M-CR 600 Aero coating, C00862, Bonderite M-CR ALCRM1200 Aero coating,
C50357, or Bonderite M-CR 1200S Aero coating, C50005, on paper and use a roller to make
the powder smooth.
SUBTASK 51-21-41-370-005
(2) Mix the solution in a stainless steel container (1 gal) (4 Liter), STD-1118, or acid resistant
container, STD-1052.
NOTE: Do not use lead or glass.
SUBTASK 51-21-41-370-006
(3) For Bonderite M-CR 600 Aero coating, C00862, add 3 oz (85 g) of powder to water for each
1 gal (3.8 l) of final solution.
(a) Stir until the powder is dissolved.
(b) Add up to 2% by volume of Bonderite M-AD 22 Toner Aero surface treatment, C50358,
ammonium hydroxide, or nitric acid as required to adjust the pH to between 1.5 and 2.0.
51-21-41
SUBTASK 51-21-41-370-007
(4) For Bonderite M-CR ALCRM1200 Aero coating, C50357, add 3 oz (85 g) of powder to water
for each 1 gal (3.8 l) of final solution.
(a) Stir until the powder is dissolved.
(b) Keep the pH in the range of 1.50 - 1.90.
(c) Adjust the pH down with nitric acid or adjust the pH up with ammonium hydroxide.
SUBTASK 51-21-41-370-021
(5) For Bonderite M-CR 1200S Aero coating, C50005, add 1.9 oz (54 g) - 2.1 oz (60 g) of powder
to water for each 1 gal (3.8 l) of final solution.
SUBTASK 51-21-41-370-008
(6) Fully mix the solution until the powder is dissolved.
(a) Ignore small quantities of material that do not dissolve.
SUBTASK 51-21-41-910-003
(7) Allow to stand for 1 hour before use.
NOTE: Prepare the solution in small quantities. Discard the solution after you use it if a brush,
sponge, or wiper was put into it or if contamination gets into the solution. Do not add
wetting agents or other materials to this solution.
F . Procedure - Apply Bonderite M-CR 600 Aero, Bonderite M-CR ALCRM 1200 Aero, or Bonderite
M-CR 1200S Aero.
NOTE: In areas where the chemical conversion coating may come in contact with BMS 10-20
primer, only Bonderite M-CR 600 Aero coating, C00862, must be used.
SUBTASK 51-21-41-370-009
WARNING
DO NOT GET CHEMICAL CONVERSION COATING SOLUTION IN YOUR
MOUTH, IN YOUR EYES, ON YOUR SKIN, OR ON YOUR CLOTHES. DO NOT
BREATHE THE FUMES FROM THIS MATERIAL. AIR MUST FLOW FREELY
THROUGH THE WORK AREA. USE THE NECESSARY RESPIRATORY
PROTECTION. KEEP THIS MATERIAL AWAY FROM SPARKS, FLAME, AND
HEAT . THIS MATERIAL IS POISONOUS AND FLAMMABLE AND CAN CAUSE
INJURY TO PERSONS OR DAMAGE TO EQUIPMENT .
(1) Apply Bonderite M-CR 600 Aero coating, C00862, Bonderite M-CR ALCRM1200 Aero coating,
C50357, or Bonderite M-CR 1200S Aero coating, C50005, equally with a medium nylon bristle
brush, STD-3911, or a clean cotton wiper, G00034, or a cellulose sponge, or mop (cotton).
(a) Start from the bottom.
SUBTASK 51-21-41-370-010
(2) Let the solution stay on the surface until a given color is found (approximately 2 - 3 minutes).
NOTE: Do not let the Bonderite M-CR 600 Aero coating, C00862, Bonderite M-CR
ALCRM1200 Aero coating, C50357, or Bonderite M-CR 1200S Aero coating, C50005,
solution dry until the color of the surface changes.
NOTE: If the color does not come, if the solution breaks up into beads, or if a powdery layer
occurs, lightly sand area with 600-grit paper and apply the coating again.
(a) Keep the area wet.
(b) Apply small quantities of solution with a cotton wiper, G00034.
51-21-41
SUBTASK 51-21-41-140-005
(3) Carefully flush the surface with a clean cotton wiper, G00034, that is moist with water.
NOTE: Do not let Bonderite M-CR 600 Aero coating, C00862, Bonderite M-CR 1200S Aero
coating, C50005, Bonderite M-CR 600RTU Aero coating, C50356, or Bonderite M-CR
1201 Aero coating, C50355, solution dry until the color of the surface changes.
(a) Start from the bottom.
SUBTASK 51-21-41-140-006
(4) Lightly rub the surface with a clean dry cotton wiper, G00034, to remove unwanted liquid.
(a) Rub the surface until the liquid is gone.
SUBTASK 51-21-41-100-003
(5) Immediately after use, fully clean the cloths in water and keep them in a container filled with
water.
SUBTASK 51-21-41-910-004
(6) Dry the surface with air that is a temperature of 120°F (49°C) (recommended) with a maximum
of 130°F (54°C) (maximum).
SUBTASK 51-21-41-370-011
(7) Apply the last finish or start adhesive bonding (Chapter 51 of the Structural Repair Manual).
(a) Touch the parts or surface with clean rubber glove, STD-1086, and keep the parts or
surface dry and clean to prevent surface contamination.

C. Prepare the Surface for Aero Application Process
SUBTASK 51-21-41-110-004
WARNING
DO NOT GET ALIPHATIC NAPHTHA OR DRY CLEANING SOLVENT IN YOUR
MOUTH, YOUR EYES, OR ON YOUR SKIN. DO NOT BREATHE THE FUMES
FROM THE ALIPHATIC NAPHTHA OR DRY CLEANING SOLVENT . PUT ON
PROTECTIVE SPLASH GOGGLES AND GLOVES WHEN YOU USE
ALIPHATIC NAPHTHA OR DRY CLEANING SOLVENT . KEEP ALIPHATIC
NAPHTHA OR DRY CLEANING SOLVENT AWAY FROM SPARKS, FLAME,
AND TEMPERATURES ABOVE THE FLASH POINT . ALIPHATIC NAPHTHA OR
DRY CLEANING SOLVENT IS A POISONOUS AND FLAMMABLE SOLVENT
WHICH CAN CAUSE INJURY TO PERSONS AND DAMAGE TO EQUIPMENT .
(1) Clean the surface, do the following steps:
WARNING
DO NOT GET SOLVENTS IN YOUR MOUTH, YOUR EYES, OR ON YOUR
SKIN. DO NOT BREATHE THE FUMES FROM SOLVENTS. SOLVENTS
ARE DANGEROUS MATERIALS. SOLVENTS CAN BE FLAMMABLE.
OBEY THE MATERIAL SAFETY DATA SHEETS (MSDS) FOR SOLVENTS.
OBEY LOCAL REGULATIONS FOR THE CORRECT PROCEDURES TO
USE OR DISCARD SOLVENTS. SOLVENTS CAN CAUSE INJURIES TO
PERSONNEL AND DAMAGE TO EQUIPMENT .
(a) Clean the surface with a cotton wiper, G00034, soft paint brush, STD-1080, or a sponge,
G00116, that is moist with solvent, B00074, or solvent, B00083.
NOTE: A spray gun can be used to help clean the surface.
1) Be careful not to put solvent on an area that is larger than is necessary.
(b) Flush the surface with clean solvent, B00074, or solvent, B00083.
(c) Clean the surface again with a clean cotton wiper, G00034, until no remaining particles or
residue are found.
(d) Let the surface dry for a minimum of 15 minutes.
(e) Apply a thin film of water, G50256, to part surface and inspect for a water break-free
surface.
D. Bonderite M-CR 1132 Aero Application Process
SUBTASK 51-21-41-370-012
(1) Remove cap. Hold Bonderite M-CR 1132 Aero coating, C50079, with applicator tip downwards.
SUBTASK 51-21-41-840-001
(2) Activate the Bonderite M-CR 1132 Aero coating, C50079, pen by pressing tip against a firm
surface. This will open the valve allowing Bonderite M-CR 1132 Aero coating, C50079, to reach
applicator tip. A new unit should charge in 15 to 30 seconds. When the Bonderite M-CR 1132
Aero coating, C50079 wets the tip, release pressure. Unit is ready for use.
SUBTASK 51-21-41-200-001
(3) Ensure that surface to be coated is dry before proceeding.
SUBTASK 51-21-41-370-013
(4) Apply Bonderite M-CR 1132 Aero coating, C50079, to the part surface in smooth even strokes.
Fifty percent overlap of strokes usually gives proper coating weight. Do not allow the Bonderite
M-CR 1132 Aero coating, C50079, to puddle. Puddling will increase the time required for
drying, and thick uneven areas in the coating cause performance failures.
51-21-41
SUBTASK 51-21-41-370-014
(5) Apply the second coat within 5 minutes after the first Bonderite M-CR 1132 Aero coating,
C50079, dries and repeat the application in a cross hatch pattern. Do not wipe. Do not water
rinse. Water rinse degrades corrosion resistance and paint adhesion.
SUBTASK 51-21-41-370-015
(6) Ambient air dry or force air dry coating at 130°F (54°C) maximum sufficiently to evaporate
water.

D. Prepare the Surface for Coating
SUBTASK 51-21-41-950-003
(1) Apply Scotch Flatback Masking T ape 250, G00270 to all adjacent areas. Do not let the coating
fall on surfaces that have no tape.
(a) Attach the tags that follow to the left control wheel in the flight deck.
1) "PITOT PROBES COVERED" tag, G02447
2) "STATIC PORTS COVERED" tag, G02444
3) A red tag labeled “AOA SENSORS COVERED”
(b) Put a pitot probe cover, COM-1503 on the pitot probe.
(c) Apply Scotch Flatback Masking T ape 250, G00270 on the static ports.
(d) Do the steps that follow to cover the angle-of-attack (AOA) sensors.
1) Cover the angle-of-attack sensors with PG-777 solvent-resistant masking tape.
a) Apply PG-777 over the blade, plate, and blade-to-plate gap.
NOTE: This is to allow easier removal of 3M 425 aluminum tape.
2) Cover the PG-777 solvent-resistant masking tape with 3M 425 Aluminum Foil T ape,
G00291.
a) Shingle the aluminum foil tape from the blade-to-plate gap up to the blade tip
until all of the masking tape is covered.
(2) Clean surface in accordance with one of the following:
(a) solvent clean in accordance with SOPM 20-30-03, or
(b) vapor degreasing in accordance with SOPM 20-30-03, or
(c) emulsion clean in accordance with SOPM 20-30-03, or
(d) manual clean in accordance with SOPM 20-30-03, or
(e) alkaline clean in accordance with SOPM 20-30-03.
(3) Wet abrade with a pneumatic sander or hand wet abrade to a dull finish with abrasive pads
(Table 702, see below for definition), and water. Abrade lap and butt splices too if they are to
be subsequently painted. Abrade with caution to meet the surface integrity requirements of
D6–9002.
(4) Do not abrade surfaces that will not be subsequently painted.
(5) After abrading the wetted surface, flush with ample water. Do not allow the surface to dry
before rinsing.
(6) After wet abrade, it is optional to alkaline clean in accordance with SOPM 20-30-03.
51-21-41
(7) Check for a water-break-free surface on all surfaces in accordance with Table 703 (see below).
If water breaks occur, abrade or alkaline clean again.
NOTE: When the aluminum surface is not water-break-free, the surface is not clean.
NOTE: Formation of chemical conversion coating only occurs on a clean surface.
NOTE: Formation of chemical conversion coating is necessary for in-service paint durability.
(8) Apply sol-gel coating, C50157 within 8 hours after manual abrasive pre-treatment.

Company.

bonded, 3M Company.
NOTE: All sizes are permitted. The UPC code for the 9 1/4 inch by 3 2/3 inches size is 48011–27693.
D) Norton Bear-T ex 747 General Purpose Hand Pad, very fine, maroon color, aluminum oxide, Saint Gobain Abrasives,
Inc.
Table 703/51-21-41-993-806 Section 12.2: Water-Break-Free Test
The water-break-free surface check is as follows:
a) Flush surface with water.
b) If surface is clean (free of oils), water will make an unbroken layer and sheet off. If surface is not clean, water will bead
on the surface (droplets) or appear as patchy and non-uniform.
c) After curtains of water have stopped flowing, measure the time that the water is continuous. The surface is
water-break-free if the water is continuous for approximately 30 seconds.
E. Prepare the coating.
SUBTASK 51-21-41-370-016
(1) Prepare the sol-gel coating, C50157 or AC-131-CB metal conversion coating, C50119
according to the manufacturer's kitting procedures.
(a) The kit should be used within 1 year of the date of manufacture on the kit.
(b) Do not treat surfaces with the sol-gel coating, C50157 or AC-131-CB metal conversion
coating, C50119 before the 30-minute induction time.
(c) Do not treat surfaces with the sol-gel coating, C50157 or AC-131-CB metal conversion
coating, C50119 after the 24-hour application time has expired.
(d) Make sure the temperature of the surface you will be treating and the air temperature are
at least 55°F (13°C).

F . Apply the coating.

AC-131-CB metal conversion coating, C50119 from the bottom to the top of the surface.
(b) Apply a continuous layer to sufficiently wet the substrate. It is acceptable to apply a single

CAUTION
DO NOT USE ABRASIVE PADS (SCOTCH-BRITE), OR ABRASIVE
PAPER ON THE ALUMINUM SURFACE UNLESS THE SCRIBE LINE
INSPECTIONS WERE MADE. ABRASIVE PADS CAN SMOOTH THE
ALUMINUM SURFACE AND HIDE THE SCRIBE LINE MARKS. IF YOU
USE ABRASIVE PADS TO REMOVE THE PAINT OR PRIMER
BEFORE YOU DO THE SCRIBE LINE INSPECTIONS, YOU CAN BE

1) If water-breaks occur, use an abrasive pad (Scotch-Brite) to rework the area. Clean
the area and re-apply the sol-gel coating, C50157 or AC-131-CB metal conversion
coating, C50119.
SUBTASK 51-21-41-370-018
(2) Allow the coated surface to drain until excess solution no longer drips.
SUBTASK 51-21-41-370-019
(3) After the surface has drained, use filtered, compressed air to remove surplus sol-gel coating,
C50157 or AC-131-CB metal conversion coating, C50119 that accumulated in crevices,
pockets, drip edges, or fastener holes.
(a) If necessary, a cotton wiper, G00034 can be used to soak up the sol-gel coating, C50157
or AC-131-CB metal conversion coating, C50119. Do not rub the wiper on the surface.
51-21-41
SUBTASK 51-21-41-370-020
(4) Allow the sol-gel coating, C50157 or AC-131-CB metal conversion coating, C50119 to dry until
the surface is visually dry.
NOTE: Until it cures and hardens, sol-gel coating, C50157 or AC-131-CB metal conversion
coating, C50119 can be damaged by contact such as touching, wiping, and rubbing
and by such means as water rinsing, solvent flushing, and masking tape application.
Depending on the cure conditions, the sol-gel coating, C50157 or AC-131-CB metal
conversion coating, C50119 can cure for more than six hours.
(a) Do not apply masking tape to surfaces treated with sol-gel coating, C50157 or
AC-131-CB metal conversion coating, C50119.
(b) Make sure sol-gel coating, C50157 or AC-131-CB metal conversion coating, C50119 has
fully dried before applying primer.
NOTE: Uneven drying of the sol-gel coating, C50157 or AC-131-CB metal conversion
coating, C50119 can leave dark areas on the surface. The dark areas are
acceptable.

2. Airplane Surface Finish Application
WARNING
CLEANING AND CONVERSION COATING SOLUTIONS CONTAIN HAZARDOUS
AND FLAMMABLE MATERIALS. THESE SOLUTIONS CAN CAUSE INJURY TO
PERSONS OR DAMAGE TO EQUIPMENT .
A. Tools/Equipment
Reference Description
STD-123 Brush - Soft Bristle
B. Consumable Materials
Reference Description Specification
G00034 Cotton Wiper - Process Cleaning Absorbent

AMS3819 Class 1
Grade A or B Form 1
(Supersede BMS15-5
CL A)
