G00744 Cloth - Emery
C. Apply the Conversion Coating
SUBTASK 51-21-51-120-001
WARNING
MAGNESIUM POWDER CAN CAUSE A FIRE. INJURY TO PERSONS OR
DAMAGE TO EQUIPMENT CAN OCCUR.
(1) Rub the surface with an emery cloth, G00744, #240 grit.
SUBTASK 51-21-51-100-001
(2) Clean the surface as follows:
(a) Remove all loose particles from the surface with a soft bristle brush, STD-123 or rub the
surface with clean cotton wiper, G00034
CAUTION
PROTECT ALL SURFACES THAT YOU DO NOT WANT TO CLEAN.
CLEANER CAN CAUSE DAMAGE TO CADMIUM OR ALUMINUM
FINISHES.
(b) Apply protection to all areas that you do not want to clean.
NOTE: Use a material that will resist cleaning and conversion coating solutions.
(c) To prepare the cleaning solution, mix 10 volumes of water to 1 volume of Kelite 28.
51-21-51
CAUTION
DO NOT LET THE CLEANING SOLUTION INTO FAYING SURFACES.
DAMAGE TO THE FAYING SURFACES CAN OCCUR IF YOU PERMIT
THE CLEANING SOLUTION TO STAY ON THE SURFACE.
(d) Apply the cleaning solution to the surface using a cotton wiper, G00034, soft bristle
brush, STD-123, or a sponge, G00116
NOTE: Let the solution soak for five minutes.
NOTE: Do not let the cleaner dry on the surface.
(e) Thoroughly flush the surface with a clean wet sponge, G00116 or cotton wiper, G00034 to
completely remove cleaner residue.
(f) Apply a thin film of water to part surface and inspect for a water break-free surface.
NOTE: A water break-free surface will have a continuous layer of water for 30 seconds.
(g) Clean the surface with a mixture of magnesium oxide and water to remove the water
break.
(h) Dry the surface with a clean cotton wiper, G00034.
(i) Flush the surface again with a clean wet cotton wiper, G00034 or sponge, G00116.
SUBTASK 51-21-51-110-001
(3) Mix the Dow 19 conversion coating as follows:
(a) In a clean 1 gallon (3.8 liters) glass or polyethylene container:
1) add 9/10 gallon (3.4 liters) water,
2) add 1 1/3 ounce (0.04 liter) chromic acid,
3) add 1 ounce (0.03 liter) calcium sulfate, and
4) add water to fill the container to the 1 gallon (3.8 liter) level.
(b) Mix the solution for a minimum of 15 minutes.
NOTE: Let the particles in the solution go to the bottom of the container.
NOTE: The solution must be free of all particles when it is applied.
(c) Measure the 'pH' of the solution and check that it is between 1.2 and 1.6.
(d) If the 'pH' is not correct, do the following:
1) Use sulfuric acid to lower the 'pH'.
2) Use sodium hydroxide to increase the 'pH'.
SUBTASK 51-21-51-110-002
(4) Apply the conversion coating solution.
(a) Apply a sufficient quantity of the solution to wet the metal surface until the surface is gold
to dark brown in color.
(b) Flush the surface with clean cold water.
(c) Dry the surface with air that is no more than 180 degrees F (82.2 C).
(d) Make sure that all of the surface is coated.
(e) Make sure there are no scratches, powder or lines on the surface.
(f) If there is any scratches or powder on the surface do the following:
1) Rub the surface with a clean cotton wiper, G00034 and apply the conversion
coating again.
51-21-51
SUBTASK 51-21-51-370-001
(5) As soon as possible, apply the correct protective finish to prevent contamination of the surface
or the start of corrosion.

1) Rub the area until it is clean.
2) Dry the area with a clean cotton wiper, G00034.
NOTE: Make sure the area is clean.
3) Fully clean the area again if it is necessary.
(b) For plastic (thermoplastic) surfaces, apply Cee Bee 280 cleaner, B00005 with a cotton
wiper, G00034.
NOTE: The following cleaners are optional to Cee Bee 280 cleaner, B00005: cleaner,
B00402, Oakite 204 cleaner, B00008, and Calla 301A cleaner, B00015.
NOTE: When a solvent is necessary, use Series 85 solvent, B01005.
1) Rub the area until it is fully clean.
2) Dry the area with a clean cotton wiper, G00034.
NOTE: Make sure the area is clean.
3) Clean the area again if it is not fully clean.
(c) For all other surfaces:
1) Fully clean the surface with a clean cotton wiper, G00034 that is moist with solvent,
B00083.
2) Before the solvent dries, rub dry the surface with a clean dry cotton wiper, G00034.
3) Clean the area again if it is not fully clean.
SUBTASK 51-21-61-350-001
(3) Repair all the damaged hydraulic fluid resistant finish.
(a) Fully clean the area again.
51-21-61
SUBTASK 51-21-61-950-001
(4) Apply Scotch Flatback Masking T ape 250, G00270 to all adjacent areas. Do not let the
chemical conversion coating solution fall on surfaces that have no tape.
NOTE: You do not have to apply tape on anodized surfaces.
(a) Attach the tags that follow to the left control wheel in the flight deck.
1) "PITOT PROBES COVERED" tag, G02447
2) "STATIC PORTS COVERED" tag, G02444
3) A red tag labeled “AOA SENSORS COVERED”
(b) Put a pitot probe cover, COM-1503 on the pitot probe.
(c) Apply Scotch Flatback Masking T ape 250, G00270 on the static ports.
(d) Do the steps that follow to cover the angle-of-attack (AOA) sensors.
1) Cover the angle-of-attack sensors with PG-777 solvent-resistant masking tape.
a) Apply PG-777 over the blade, plate, and blade-to-plate gap.
NOTE: This is to allow easier removal of 3M 425 aluminum tape.
2) Cover the PG-777 solvent-resistant masking tape with 3M 425 Aluminum Foil T ape,
G00291.
a) Shingle the aluminum foil tape from the blade-to-plate gap up to the blade tip
until all of the masking tape is covered.
E. Prepare (Mix) the Hydraulic Fluid Resistant Finish
SUBTASK 51-21-61-390-001
CAUTION
USE CLEAN SOLVENT-RESISTANT CONTAINERS WHEN YOU MIX THE
HYDRAULIC-FLUID-RESISTANT FINISH. DO NOT USE CONTAINERS THAT
CONTAIN ORGANIC COATINGS. DIRTY CONTAINERS AND ORGANIC
COATINGS CAN CAUSE CONTAMINATION OF THE MIXTURE.
(1) Fully mix 1 part (by volume) of the Catalyst (X-310) to 2 parts (by volume) of the base
(683-3-2) to make coating, B00571.
(a) Mix the base component continuously, when you add the catalyst.
(b) Immediately after you mix the compound, identify the container with a label.
NOTE: The compound has a application time of 4 hours at 70°F (21.1°C). Do not use the
compound when its application time has ended. The use of the compound when
its application time has ended, will decrease its performance.
NOTE: Keep the materials that were not mixed, at a temperature between 40°F and
90°F (4.4-32.2°C). You can keep the materials that were not mixed for 12 months
from the date of manufacture.
1) Write the date and the hour when the compound was mixed,
2) Write the date and hour when the application time ends.
F . Apply the Hydraulic Fluid Resistant Finish
SUBTASK 51-21-61-350-002
(1) Do the steps that follow to Apply the Hydraulic Fluid Resistant Finish:
(a) Before you apply the hydraulic fluid resistant finish on sealants, permit the sealant to
become fully dry.
(b) Make sure the surface is fully clean before you apply the hydraulic fluid resistant finish.
51-21-61
(c) Apply the hydraulic fluid resistant finish with a spray gun or brush until the finish is 1 to 2
mils (0.001 to 0.002 inch) (0.025-0.051 mm) thick.
NOTE: The temperature must be more than 65°F (18.3°C) and the relative humidity must
be less than 85 percent for best results.
(d) Apply the hydraulic fluid resistant finish on the decals.
1) Make sure you apply a 1/4 inch (6.35 mm) overlap of sealant to the decal.
a) A 1/4 inch (6.35 mm) overlap of sealant to the decal will seal the edges of the
decal.
(e) Make sure the hydraulic fluid resistant finish is bonded to all applicable surfaces.
SUBTASK 51-21-61-390-002
(2) Let the hydraulic fluid resistant finish dry for 4 hours at 65°F (18.3°C) (minimum), before you
move the airplane to an open area.
NOTE: A time of 14 days at 65°F (18.3°C) (minimum) is necessary before the hydraulic fluid
resistant finish is fully cured.

D. Apply BMS 10-21 Type III Conductive Coating to Specified External Surfaces
SUBTASK 51-21-71-100-001
WARNING
DO NOT GET PAINT MATERIALS AND SOLVENTS IN YOUR MOUTH, IN
YOUR EYES, ON YOUR SKIN, OR ON YOUR CLOTHES. DO NOT BREATHE
THE FUMES FROM THESE MATERIALS. MAKE SURE THE AIR FLOWS
FREELY THROUGH THE WORK AREA. USE THE NECESSARY
RESPIRATORY PROTECTION. KEEP THESE MATERIALS AWAY FROM
SPARKS, FLAME, AND TEMPERATURES ABOVE THE FLASH POINT . THESE
MATERIALS ARE POISONOUS AND FLAMMABLE AND CAN CAUSE INJURY
TO PERSONS OR DAMAGE TO EQUIPMENT .
(1) Do this task: Airplane Surface Preparation for Application of Finish, TASK 51-21-00-100-801.
SUBTASK 51-21-71-370-001
CAUTION
DO NOT USE BMS10-21, TYPE III ON THE RADOME OR THE FIBERGLASS
PANEL FOR THE HF ANTENNA. YOU CAN ONLY USE BMS10-21, TYPE IV ON
THESE. IF YOU USE BMS10-21, TYPE III, THE WEATHER RADAR SYSTEM,
AND THE HF COMMUNICATION SYSTEM WILL NOT OPERATE CORRECTLY .
(2) Prepare the BMS 10-21 Type III conductive coating, C00767, with the steps as follows:
(a) Refer to the manufacturer's instructions to mix the conductive coating.
(b) Fully shake each conductive coating component before you mix it.
NOTE: The conductive coating is supplied in two component kits. The kits have a base
component and a catalyst.
(c) Put a label on the container with, the data that follows, immediately after it is mixed:
1) The date and time it was mixed,
2) The application time expiration time is as follows:
a) 4 hours if the temperature is less than 81°F (27°C)
b) 2 hours if the temperature is 81°F (27°C) - 100°F (38°C).
(d) Do not use the conductive coating for a minimum of 30 minutes after it is mixed.
51-21-71
SUBTASK 51-21-71-370-002
(3) Apply the BMS 10-21 Type III conductive coating, C00767, compound with a paint spray gun
with interchangeable nozzles, STD-1133, or paint brush, STD-1080.
(a) Be sure that the temperature for the application of the coating, C00767, must be 40°F
(4°C) - 100°F (38°C).
NOTE: The recommended temperature is 50°F (10°C) - 90°F (32°C).
(b) The dry film thickness after it has been cured must be 0.0006 ±0.0002 in.
(0.0152 ±0.0051 mm).
SUBTASK 51-21-71-370-003
(4) Cure the BMS 10-21 Type III conductive coating, C00767, with one of the steps that follow:
(a) Let the conductive coating that is in fastener holes cure for at least 20 minutes at 70°F
(21°C) - 90°F (32°C) before you install the fasteners.
(b) Air dry the surface of the conductive coating for 2 hours at 60°F (16°C).
(c) To cure the conductive coating quickly, apply a minimum temperature of 140°F (60°C)
before you do the resistivity measurements (Figure 701).
NOTE: The increased drying time as shown in Figure 701 is not a full cure.
1) The flash-off time necessary before the higher temperature cure is 15 minutes
minimum at 75 ±15°F (24 ±8°C).
2) The cool-off time necessary before subsequent processing the cure is 15 minutes
minimum at 75 ±15°F (24 ±8°C).
SUBTASK 51-21-71-700-001
(5) Do the steps that follow to measure the resistivity of the conductive coating
(Fastener-to-Conductive Surface Test Method):
NOTE: The resistivity of the conductive coating after it is cured must be no more than 300,000
ohms/square.
NOTE: Let the conductive coat cure for the specified time as shown in Figure 701 before you
measure the conductivity.
(a) If the bonding surface and the head of the fastener are covered with a finish, do the steps
as follows (Figure 702):
1) Remove a sufficient quantity of the outer coating or paint to let the probes touch the
conductive finish.
NOTE: If it is necessary, you can push the probes through the outer coating.
2) Put the digital/analog multimeter, COM-1793, with the film resistance measure
probe, SPL-10361, in direct contact with the bonding fastener and the conductive
finish.
a) Put the film resistance measure probe, SPL-10361, as shown in Figure 702.
3) Make a record of the value of the resistivity shown on the multimeter.
a) The maximum permitted resistivity is 300,000 Ohms/Square.
SUBTASK 51-21-71-700-002
(6) Do the steps that follow to measure the resistivity of the conductive coating on uninstalled
panels (Fastener-to-Fastener Test Method):
NOTE: The resistivity of the conductive coating after it is cured must be no more than 300,000
ohms/square
(a) Make sure the conductive coating shows in the fastener location.
51-21-71
(b) Clean the area with solvent, B00083.
(c) Install one temporary fastener at each fastener location to be measured (Figure 702).
NOTE: This procedure can be used for measuring electrical resistivity of panels with
covered (painted) conductive coatings when the ground fastener locations show.
(d) Connect the digital/analog multimeter, COM-1793, probes at the two fastener locations as
shown in Figure 702.
(e) Measure the resistivity with the digital/analog multimeter, COM-1793.
1) The maximum permitted resistivity is 300,000 Ohms/Square.
(f) Remove the temporary fasteners from the surface.
SUBTASK 51-21-71-700-003
(7) Use one of the procedures that follow to measure the resistivity of the conductive coating
(Square Test Method) (Figure 703).
NOTE: The term ohms/square is used to give the resistance in ohms of current passing from
one side of a square region to the opposite side, regardless of the size of the square.
NOTE: Do not use this procedure to measure the resistivity of conductive finishes when the
surface is covered (painted).
(a) If the surface resistivity measurement probe, SPL-7885, is used on the conductive
coating surface, do the steps that follow:
1) Put the digital/analog multimeter, COM-1793, with the surface resistivity
measurement probe, SPL-7885, in direct contact with the conductive coating
surface.
2) Measure the resistivity the digital/analog multimeter, COM-1793.
a) The measured value multiplied by 10 gives the value of resistivity in
ohms/square.
NOTE: The term ohms/square is used because it gives the resistance in
ohms of current passing from one side of a square region to the
opposite side, regardless of the size of the square.
NOTE: The maximum permitted resistivity is 300,000 Ohms/square.
(b) If the 3M 1181 copper foil tape, G50630, is used on the conductive coating surface, do
the steps that follow:
1) Put two pieces of 3M 1181 copper foil tape, G50630, (of equal length (L)) parallel to
each other on the conductive coating surface.
2) The two strips of 3M 1181 copper foil tape, G50630, must be put parallel to each
other a distance of one tenth the length of the two strips (0.1L ±5 percent).
3) Push the two strips of 3M 1181 copper foil tape, G50630, against the conductive
coating.
4) Measure the resistivity with the digital/analog multimeter, COM-1793.
a) The measured value multiplied by 10 gives the value of resistivity in
ohms/square.
NOTE: The term ohms/square is used because it gives the resistance in
ohms of current passing from one side of a square region to the
opposite side, regardless of the size of the square.
NOTE: The maximum permitted resistivity is 300,000 Ohms/square.
5) The measured value multiplied by 10 gives the value of resistivity in ohms/square.
51-21-71
6) Remove the 3M 1181 copper foil tape, G50630, from the surface.
SUBTASK 51-21-71-370-004
(8) Do the steps that follow if the resistivity of the conductive coating is more than 300,000
ohms/square:
(a) Use 240 grit or finer abrasive cloth, B00102, paper to remove the conductive coating.
NOTE: If the abrasive cloth, B00102, is not available, use a abrasive mat, G00251, or
240 grit or finer abrasive paper, G50077, as an alternate.
(b) Remove the remaining particles with a clean cotton wiper, G00034, that is moist with
solvent, B00083.
(c) Dry the surface with a clean cotton wiper, G00034, before the solvent is dry.
(d) Do the steps to apply a new layer of conductive coating.
(e) Do the steps to cure the conductive coating.
(f) Do the steps to measure the resistivity of the new layer of conductive coating.

SUBTASK 51-21-71-100-003
WARNING
DO NOT GET THIS MATERIAL IN YOUR MOUTH, EYES, OR ON YOUR SKIN.
CLEAN BARE SKIN FULLY AFTER YOU USE THIS MATERIAL. THIS
MATERIAL CAN CAUSE INJURIES TO PERSONNEL OR DAMAGE TO
EQUIPMENT .
(1) Do this task: (Airplane Surface Preparation for Application of Finish, TASK 51-21-00-100-801).
SUBTASK 51-21-71-370-010
(2) Prepare the BMS 10–21 Type IV conductive coating, C50219, with the steps as follows:
(a) Refer to the manufacturer's instructions to mix the conductive coating.
(b) Fully shake each conductive coating component before you mix it.
NOTE: The conductive coating is supplied in two component kits. The kits have a base
component and a catalyst.
SUBTASK 51-21-71-370-011
(3) Apply the BMS 10–21 Type IV conductive coating, C50219, compound with a paint spray gun
with interchangeable nozzles, STD-1133, or paint brush, STD-1080.
(a) Be sure that the temperature for the application of the coating, C50219, must be 40°F
(4°C) to 100°F (38°C). The preferred temperature is 50°F (10°C) to 90°F (32°C).
(b) The dry film thickness after it has been cured must be 0.0008 ±0.0002 in.
(0.0203 ±0.0051 mm).
SUBTASK 51-21-71-370-012
(4) Cure the BMS 10–21 Type IV conductive coating, C50219, with the steps that follow:
(a) Allow the conductive coating that is in fastener holes cure for at least 20 minutes at 70°F
(21°C) to 90°F (32°C) before you install the fasteners.
(b) Heat-cure the anti-static coating before taking resistivity measurements or overcoating.
1) The flash-off time necessary before the higher temperature cure is 15 minutes
minimum at 75 ±15°F (24 ±8°C).
2) Apply heat to cure the conductive coating before you do the resistivity
measurements as shown in (Figure 701).
NOTE: The increased drying time as shown in Figure 701 is not a full cure.
a) Do not cure above 160°F (71°C).
b) Do not cure at temperatures of 120°F (49°C) or above for longer than 2 hours.
3) The cool-off time necessary before subsequent processing the cure is 15 minutes
minimum at 75 ±15°F (24 ±8°C).
51-21-71
SUBTASK 51-21-71-700-007
(5) Do the steps that follow to measure the resistivity of the conductive coating
(Fastener-to-Conductive Surface Test Method):
NOTE: The resistivity of the conductive coating after it is cured must be no less than 1
Megohm/square or more than 100 Megohms/square.
NOTE: Let the conductive coat cure for the specified time as shown in (Figure 701) before you
measure the conductivity.
(a) If the bonding surface and the head of the fastener are covered with a finish, (Figure 702)
do the steps as follows:
1) Remove a sufficient quantity of the outer coating or paint to let the probes touch the
conductive finish.
NOTE: If it is necessary, you can push the probes through the outer coating.
2) Put the digital/analog multimeter, COM-1793, with the film resistance measure
probe, SPL-10361, in direct contact with the bonding fastener and the conductive
finish.
a) Put the film resistance measure probe, SPL-10361, as shown in Figure 702.
3) Make a record of the value of the resistivity shown on the multimeter.
a) The minimum permitted resistivity is no less than 1 Megohm/square.
b) The maximum permitted resistivity is 100 Megohms/square.
SUBTASK 51-21-71-700-008
(6) Do the steps that follow to measure the resistivity of the conductive coating on uninstalled
panels (Fastener-to-Fastener Test Method):
NOTE: The resistivity of the conductive coating after it is cured must be no less than 1
Megohm/square or more than 100 Megohms/square.

(b) Clean the area with solvent, B00083.

1) The minimum permitted resistivity is no less than 1 Megohm/square.
2) The maximum permitted resistivity is 100 Megohms/square.

(7) Use one of the procedures that follow to measure the resistivity of the conductive coating
(Square Test Method): (Figure 703)
NOTE: The term ohms/square is used to give the resistance in ohms of current passing from

NOTE: Do not use this procedure to measure the resistivity of conductive finishes when the

51-21-71
(a) If the surface resistivity measurement probe, SPL-7885, is used on the conductive
coating surface, do the steps that follow:
1) Put the digital/analog multimeter, COM-1793, with the surface resistivity
measurement probe, SPL-7885 in direct contact with the conductive coating
surface.
2) Measure the resistivity the digital/analog multimeter, COM-1793.
a) The measured value multiplied by 10 gives the value of resistivity in
ohms/square.
NOTE: The term ohms/square is used because it gives the resistance in
ohms of current passing from one side of a square region to the
opposite side, regardless of the size of the square.
NOTE: The minimum resistivity is no less than 1 Megohm/square or a
maximum resistivity of 100 Megohms/square.
(b) If the 3M 1181 copper foil tape, G50630, is used on the conductive coating surface, do
the steps that follow:
1) Put two pieces of 3M 1181 copper foil tape, G50630, (of equal length (L)) parallel to
each other on the conductive coating surface.
2) The two strips of 3M 1181 copper foil tape, G50630, must be put parallel to each
other a distance of one tenth the length of the two strips (0.1L ±5 percent).
3) Push the two strips of 3M 1181 copper foil tape, G50630, against the conductive
coating.
4) Measure the resistivity with the digital/analog multimeter, COM-1793.
a) The measured value multiplied by 10 gives the value of resistivity in
ohms/square.
NOTE: The term ohms/square is used because it gives the resistance in
ohms of current passing from one side of a square region to the
opposite side, regardless of the size of the square.
NOTE: The minimum resistivity is no less than 1 Megohm/square or a
maximum resistivity of 100 Megohms/square.
5) The measured value multiplied by 10 gives the value of resistivity in ohms/square.
6) Remove the 3M 1181 copper foil tape, G50630, from the surface.
SUBTASK 51-21-71-370-013
(8) Do the steps that follow if the resistivity of the conductive coating is less than 1
Megohm/Square:
(a) Use 240 grit or finer abrasive cloth, B00102, paper to remove the conductive coating.

240 grit or finer abrasive paper, G50077, as an alternate.
(b) Remove the remaining particles with a clean cotton wiper, G00034, that is moist with
solvent, B00083.

51-21-71
SUBTASK 51-21-71-370-014
(9) Do the steps that follow if the resistivity of the conductive coating is more than 100
Megohms/Square:
(a) Cure at 140°F (60°C) to 160°F (71°C) for 60 to 70 minutes.
(b) Remeasure resistivity.
(c) If the resistivity is more then 100 Megohms/Square, do the steps that follow:
1) Use 240 grit or finer abrasive cloth, B00102, paper to remove the conductive
coating.
NOTE: If the abrasive cloth, B00102, is not available, use a abrasive mat, G00251,
or 240 grit or finer abrasive paper, G50077, as an alternate.
2) Remove the remaining particles with a clean cotton wiper, G00034, that is moist
with solvent, B00083.
3) Dry the surface with a clean cotton wiper, G00034, before the solvent is dry.
4) Do the steps to apply a new layer of conductive coating.
5) Do the steps to cure the conductive coating.
6) Do the steps to measure the resistivity of the new layer of conductive coating.

2. BMS10-79 Primer - Application
A. General
(1) This procedure has steps to do the following:

(d) Major rework.

(32°C) while applying BMS10-79 primer.
(a) Contact the paint manufacturer for guidance if you will apply the paint outside the
recommended temperature range.

E. Prepare to Apply the BMS10-79 Primer
SUBTASK 51-21-72-950-001
(1) Mask all areas that will not be coated with BMS10-79 primer.
(a) Attach the tags that follow to the left control wheel in the flight deck.
51-21-72
1) "PITOT PROBES COVERED" tag, G02447
2) "STATIC PORTS COVERED" tag, G02444
3) A red tag labeled “AOA SENSORS COVERED”.
(b) Put a pitot probe cover, COM-1503, on the pitot probe.
(c) Apply Scotch Flatback Masking T ape 250, G00270, on the static ports.
(d) Do the steps that follow to cover the Angle of Attack (AOA) sensors:
1) Cover the AOA sensors with masking tape, G51270.
a) Apply masking tape, G51270, over the blade, plate, and blade-to-plate gap.
NOTE: This is to allow easier removal of 3M 425 Aluminum Foil T ape,
G00291.
2) Cover the masking tape, G51270, with 3M 425 Aluminum Foil T ape, G00291.
a) Shingle the 3M 425 Aluminum Foil T ape, G00291, from the blade-to-plate gap
up to the blade tip until all of the masking tape, G51270, is covered.
SUBTASK 51-21-72-160-009
(2) Prepare unsealed anodized aluminum with the steps that follow:
(a) Remove small amounts of contamination that is oily with the steps that follow:
1) Use a cotton wiper, G00034, wet with water.
2) Dry the surface with a clean, dry cotton wiper, G00034.
(b) Remove small amounts of oily contamination with the steps that follow:
1) Solvent clean the surface.
a) Use a cotton wiper, G00034, wet with Series 84 solvent, B01004.
b) Immediately wipe the surface dry with a clean, dry cotton wiper, G00034.
<1> Make sure that the solvent does not dry on the surface.
SUBTASK 51-21-72-160-010
(3) Prepare sealed anodized aluminum with the steps that follow:
(a) If the surface becomes contaminated, solvent clean the surface.
1) Use a cotton wiper, G00034, wet with Series 84 solvent, B01004.
2) Immediately wipe the surface dry with a clean, dry cotton wiper, G00034.
a) Make sure that the solvent does not dry on the surface.
SUBTASK 51-21-72-160-023
(4) If other than anodized aluminum, do steps that follow:
(a) If the surface becomes contaminated, solvent clean the surface.
1) Use a cotton wiper, G00034, wet with Series 84 solvent, B01004.
2) Immediately wipe the surface dry with a clean, dry cotton wiper, G00034.
a) Make sure that the solvent does not dry on the surface.
SUBTASK 51-21-72-160-021
(5) Prepare surfaces with paint with one of the methods listed in Table 701.

SUBTASK 51-21-72-160-020
(6) Use one of the methods that follow to prepare the surface (Table 701):
(a) Method 1
1) If the contamination is only particulate, blow the surface with clean, compressed air
or wipe the surface with a tack rag, G50123.
2) Solvent clean the surface, if necessary.
a) Use a cotton wiper, G00034, wet with Series 85 solvent, B01005.
b) Immediately wipe the surface with a clean, dry cotton wiper, G00034.
<1> Make sure that the solvent does not dry on the surface.
(b) Method 2
1) Solvent clean the surface.
a) Use a cotton wiper, G00034, wet with Series 85 solvent, B01005.
b) Immediately wipe the surface with a clean, dry cotton wiper, G00034.
<1> Make sure that the solvent does not dry on the surface.
2) Sand the surface to remove gloss with 220-grit or finer sandpaper, G50260,
silicone-free abrasive.
3) Solvent clean the surface to remove sanding dust.
a) Use a cotton wiper, G00034, wet with Series 85 solvent, B01005.
b) Immediately wipe the surface with a clean, dry cotton wiper, G00034.
51-21-72
<1> Make sure that the solvent does not dry on the surface.
(c) Method 3
1) Solvent clean the surface.
a) Use a cotton wiper, G00034, wet with Series 85 solvent, B01005.
b) Immediately wipe the surface with a clean, dry cotton wiper, G00034.
<1> Make sure that the solvent does not dry on the surface.
2) Sand the surface with 320 grit or finer abrasive paper, G50078.
3) Solvent clean the surface to remove sanding dust.
a) Use a cotton wiper, G00034, wet with Series 85 solvent, B01005.
b) Immediately wipe the surface with a clean, dry cotton wiper, G00034.
<1> Make sure that the solvent does not dry on the surface.
(d) Method 4
1) Solvent clean the surface.
a) Use one of the abrasives that follow, wet with Series 85 solvent, B01005:
<1> Scotch-Brite 7447 pad, G50220, very fine, aluminum oxide
<2> Scotch-Brite Clean and Finish abrasive pad, G51319, very fine, Grade
A, aluminum oxide
<3> Bear-T ex 747 General Purpose abrasive hand pad, G51193, very fine,
aluminum oxide.
b) Immediately wipe the surface with a clean, dry cotton wiper, G00034.
<1> Make sure that the solvent does not dry on the surface.
2) Alkaline clean the surface with an abrasive pad wet with one of the cleaners that
follow, diluted with seven parts water, by volume:
a) Ardrox 160-B
b) Pace B-82 alkaline cleaner, B00017
c) Pace MF-30 cleaner, B50172
d) Turco 4215
e) Ridoline 909, B50052
f) Super Bee 300LF .
3) Rinse the surface with large amounts of water.
4) Check for a water-break-free surface.
a) If water breaks occur, alkaline clean the surface again and rinse with more
water.
5) Dry the surface.
SUBTASK 51-21-72-370-001
(7) Mix the primer using the manufacturer's instructions.

(a) Spray or brush apply the primer.

G. Minor Rework
SUBTASK 51-21-72-160-015
(1) For areas that have minor damage and the substrate is not exposed, do the steps that follow:
(a) Solvent clean the area.
1) Use a cotton wiper, G00034, wet with Series 85 solvent, B01005.
2) Immediately wipe the surface dry with a clean, dry cotton wiper, G00034.
a) Make sure that the solvent does not dry on the surface.
(b) Sand the surface where the area is damaged with one of the abrasive materials that
follow:
1) 220-grit or finer sandpaper, G50260, silicone-free abrasive
2) Scotch-Brite 7447 pad, G50220, very fine, aluminum oxide
3) Scotch-Brite Clean and Finish abrasive pad, G51319, very fine, Grade A, aluminum
oxide
4) Bear-T ex 747 General Purpose abrasive hand pad, G51193, very fine, aluminum
oxide.
(c) Solvent clean the area to remove sanding dust.
1) Use a cotton wiper, G00034, wet with Series 85 solvent, B01005.
2) Immediately wipe the surface dry with a clean, dry cotton wiper, G00034.
a) Make sure that the solvent does not dry on the surface.
(d) Apply the primer to the area.
(e) Dry and cure the primer.
SUBTASK 51-21-72-160-016
(2) For areas with minor damage and the composite or plastic substrate is exposed, do the steps
that follow:
(a) Solvent clean the area.
1) Use a cotton wiper, G00034, wet with Series 85 solvent, B01005.
2) Immediately wipe the surface dry with a clean, dry cotton wiper, G00034.
a) Make sure that the solvent does not dry on the surface.
(b) Sand the surface where the area is damaged with one of the abrasive materials that
follow:
51-21-72
1) 220-grit or finer sandpaper, G50260, silicone-free abrasive
2) Scotch-Brite 7447 pad, G50220, very fine, aluminum oxide
3) Scotch-Brite Clean and Finish abrasive pad, G51319, very fine, Grade A, aluminum
oxide
4) Bear-T ex 747 General Purpose abrasive hand pad, G51193, very fine, aluminum
oxide.
(c) Solvent clean the area to remove sanding dust.
1) Use a cotton wiper, G00034, wet with Series 85 solvent, B01005.
2) Immediately wipe the surface dry with a clean, dry cotton wiper, G00034.
a) Make sure that the solvent does not dry on the surface.
(d) Apply the primer to the area.
(e) Dry and cure the primer.
SUBTASK 51-21-72-160-017
(3) For areas with minor damage and the metal substrate is exposed, do the steps that follow:
(a) For pretreated Titanium or Corrosion Resistant Steel (CRES) surfaces, and aluminum
surfaces with chemical conversion coating, do the steps that follow:
1) Solvent clean the area.
a) Use a cotton wiper, G00034, wet with Series 85 solvent, B01005.
b) Immediately wipe the surface dry with a clean, dry cotton wiper, G00034.
<1> Make sure that the solvent does not dry on the surface.
2) Sand the surface where the area is damaged with one of the abrasive materials that
follow:
a) 220-grit or finer sandpaper, G50260, silicone-free abrasive
b) Scotch-Brite 7447 pad, G50220, very fine, aluminum oxide
c) Scotch-Brite Clean and Finish abrasive pad, G51319, very fine, Grade A,
aluminum oxide
d) Bear-T ex 747 General Purpose abrasive hand pad, G51193, very fine,
aluminum oxide.
3) Solvent clean the area to remove sanding dust.
a) Use a cotton wiper, G00034, wet with Series 85 solvent, B01005.
b) Immediately wipe the surface dry with a clean, dry cotton wiper, G00034.
<1> Make sure that the solvent does not dry on the surface.
4) Apply the primer.
5) Dry and cure the primer.
(b) For anodized areas that are less than 5% of the required coating area for the part, do the
steps that follow:
1) Solvent clean the area.
a) Use a cotton wiper, G00034, wet with Series 85 solvent, B01005.
b) Immediately wipe the surface dry with a clean, dry cotton wiper, G00034.
<1> Make sure that the solvent does not dry on the surface.
51-21-72
2) Sand the surface where the area is damaged with one of the abrasive materials that
follow:
a) 220-grit or finer sandpaper, G50260, silicone-free abrasive
b) Scotch-Brite 7447 pad, G50220, very fine, aluminum oxide
c) Scotch-Brite Clean and Finish abrasive pad, G51319, very fine, Grade A,
aluminum oxide
d) Bear-T ex 747 General Purpose abrasive hand pad, G51193, very fine,
aluminum oxide.
3) Solvent clean the area to remove sanding dust.
a) Use a cotton wiper, G00034, wet with Series 85 solvent, B01005.
b) Immediately wipe the surface dry with a clean, dry cotton wiper, G00034.
<1> Make sure that the solvent does not dry on the surface.
4) Apply Bonderite M-CR 600 Aero coating, C00862, to bare areas
(TASK 51-21-41-370-802).
5) Immediately before applying the primer, wipe the surface with a new tack rag,
G50123.
6) Apply the primer.
7) Dry and cure the primer.
H. Major Rework
SUBTASK 51-21-72-160-018
(1) For major rework of composite or plastic surfaces, do the steps that follow:
NOTE: Major rework requires the removal of the primer.
(a) Sand the surface to remove the primer with one of the abrasive materials that follow:
1) 220-grit or finer sandpaper, G50260, silicone-free abrasive
2) Scotch-Brite 7447 pad, G50220, very fine, aluminum oxide
3) Scotch-Brite Clean and Finish abrasive pad, G51319, very fine, Grade A, aluminum
oxide
4) Bear-T ex 747 General Purpose abrasive hand pad, G51193, very fine, aluminum
oxide.
(b) Solvent clean the area.
1) Use a cotton wiper, G00034, wet with Series 87 solvent, B01007.
2) Immediately wipe the surface dry with a clean, dry cotton wiper, G00034.
a) Make sure that the solvent does not dry on the surface.
(c) Prepare the surface, if necessary (TASK 51-21-21-100-802).
(d) Immediately before applying the primer, wipe the surface with a new tack rag, G50123.
(e) Apply the primer.
(f) Dry and cure the primer.
SUBTASK 51-21-72-160-019
(2) For major rework of metal parts, do the steps that follow:
(a) Sand the surface to remove the primer with one of the abrasive materials that follow:
1) 220-grit or finer sandpaper, G50260, silicone-free abrasive
51-21-72
2) Scotch-Brite 7447 pad, G50220, very fine, aluminum oxide
3) Scotch-Brite Clean and Finish abrasive pad, G51319, very fine, Grade A, aluminum
oxide
4) Bear-T ex 747 General Purpose abrasive hand pad, G51193, very fine, aluminum
oxide.
(b) Solvent clean the area.
1) Use a cotton wiper, G00034, wet with Series 87 solvent, B01007.
2) Immediately wipe the surface dry with a clean, dry cotton wiper, G00034.
a) Make sure that the solvent does not dry on the surface.
(c) Prepare the surface, if necessary (TASK 51-21-21-100-802).
(d) Immediately before applying the primer, wipe the surface with a new tack rag, G50123.
(e) Apply the primer.
(f) Dry and cure the primer.

3. BMS10-103, Type 1, Primer - Application
A. General
(1) This procedure has steps to do the following:
(a) Prepare to apply the BMS10-103, Type 1, Primer
(b) Apply the BMS10-103, Type 1, Primer
(c) Minor re-work for composite surfaces (substrate exposed)
(d) Minor re-work for titanium and CRES surfaces (substrate exposed)
(e) Major re-work for composite surfaces
(f) Major re-work for titanium and CRES surfaces.
(2) recommends you apply the BMS10-103, Type 1, primer when the ambient air
temperature is between 40°F (4°C) and 100°F (38°C).
(a) Contact the paint manufacturer for guidance if you will apply the paint outside the
recommended temperature range.

Reference Title

E. Prepare to Apply the BMS10-103, Type 1, Primer
SUBTASK 51-21-72-370-004
(1) Mask all areas that will not be coated with the primer.
(a) Attach the tags that follow to the left control wheel in the flight deck.
1) "PITOT PROBES COVERED" tag, G02447
2) "STATIC PORTS COVERED" tag, G02444
3) A red tag labeled “AOA SENSORS COVERED”.
(b) Put a pitot probe cover, COM-1503, on the pitot probe.
(c) Apply Scotch Flatback Masking T ape 250, G00270, on the static ports.
(d) Do the steps that follow to cover the Angle of Attack (AOA) sensors.
1) Cover the angle-of-attack sensors with PG-777 solvent-resistant masking tape.
a) Apply PG-777 over the blade, plate, and blade-to-plate gap.
NOTE: This is to allow easier removal of 3M 425 aluminum tape.
2) Cover the PG-777 solvent-resistant masking tape with 3M 425 Aluminum Foil T ape,
G00291.
a) Shingle the aluminum foil tape from the blade-to-plate gap up to the blade tip
until all of the masking tape is covered.
SUBTASK 51-21-72-160-001
(2) For bare exterior composite or plastic surfaces, do the steps that follow.
NOTE: These steps are not applicable to BMS8-267 or BMS8-341, Type II, Form 2, surfaces.
51-21-72
(a) Abrade the surface with 100-grit or finer aluminum oxide, silicone free, abrasive paper or
cloth.
1) Use a pneumatic sander to abrade the surface.
(b) Abrade the surface with one of the abrasive pads that follow and a pneumatic sander.
1) Scotch-Brite 7447 pad, G50220
2) Scotch-Brite Clean and Finish, very fine, aluminum oxide
3) Norton Bear-T ex 747 General Purpose Hand Pad, very fine, aluminum oxide.
(c) Remove sanding residue with compressed air, if necessary.
(d) Solvent clean the surface.
1) Wipe the surface to remove loose soil, excess grease, hydraulic fluid, or oil, if
necessary.
2) Use one of the methods that follow to solvent clean the surface.
a) Wet a cotton wiper, G00034, or equivalent with Series 87 solvent, B01007,
and wipe the surface.
b) Wipe the surface with a pre-saturated cloth, G51951, with Series 87 solvent,
B01007.
NOTE: Pre-saturated wipers are only approved if you will paint after final
cleaning.
c) Immediately wipe the surface with a clean, dry cotton wiper, G00034, to
remove excess solvent.
<1> Make sure that the solvent does not dry on the surface.
SUBTASK 51-21-72-160-002
(3) For BMS10-79 or BMS10-103 primed surfaces that have dried for less than 48 hours, do the
steps that follow.
(a) If the surface becomes contaminated, solvent clean the area.
NOTE: If the contamination is particulate, you can use clean, compressed air or a new
tack rag, G50123.
1) Wipe the surface to remove loose soil, excess grease, hydraulic fluid, or oil, if
necessary.
2) Use one of the methods that follow to solvent clean the surface.
a) Wet a cotton wiper, G00034, or equivalent with Series 85 solvent, B01005,
and wipe the surface.
b) Wipe the surface with a pre-saturated cloth, G51951, with Series 85 solvent,
B01005.
c) Wipe the surface with a clean, dry cotton wiper, G00034, to remove excess
solvent.
<1> Make sure that the solvent does not dry on the surface.
SUBTASK 51-21-72-160-003
(4) For BMS10-79 or BMS10-103 primed surfaces that have dried for more than 48 hours, or if the
primer has been force-dried for any amount of time, use one of the methods that follow.
(a) Method 1: BMS10-79 or BMS10-103
1) Solvent clean the surface.
a) Wipe the surface to remove loose soil, excess grease, hydraulic fluid, or oil.
51-21-72
b) Use one of the methods that follow to solvent clean the surface.
<1> Wet a cotton wiper, G00034, or equivalent with Series 81 solvent,
B01001, and wipe the surface.
<2> Wipe the surface with a pre-saturated cloth, G51951, with Series 81
solvent, B01001.
<3> Wipe the surface with a clean, dry cotton wiper, G00034, to remove
excess solvent.
<a> Make sure that the solvent does not dry on the surface.
2) Sand the surface with one of the abrasive pads or abrasive paper that follow.
a) Scotch-Brite 7447 pad, G50220
b) Scotch-Brite Clean and Finish, very fine, aluminum oxide
c) Norton Bear-T ex 747 General Purpose Hand Pad, very fine, aluminum oxide
d) An abrasive cloth, B00102, 100-grit or finer, wet or dry.
3) Wipe the surface to remove loose soil, excess grease, hydraulic fluid, or oil, if
necessary.
4) Use one of the methods that follow to solvent clean the surface.
NOTE: This is to remove sanding dust.
a) Wet a cotton wiper, G00034, or equivalent, or brush, G00215, with Series 85
solvent, B01005, and wipe the surface.
b) Use one of the methods that follow to solvent clean the surface.
<1> Wet a cotton wiper, G00034, or equivalent, or brush, G00215 with
Series 85 solvent, B01005, and wipe the surface.
<2> Wipe the surface with a pre-saturated cloth, G51951, with Series 85
solvent, B01005.
NOTE: Pre-saturated wipers are only approved if you will paint after
final cleaning.
<3> Immediately wipe the surface with a clean, dry cotton wiper, G00034, to
remove excess solvent.
<a> Make sure that the solvent does not dry on the surface.
5) Use compressed air or a new tack rag, G50123, if necessary.
6) If the assembly has bare titanium fastener heads or bare CRES fastener heads, to
the steps that follow.
a) Wipe the surface with a cotton wiper, G00034, wet with alcohol, B50073
(ASTM D770).
b) Wipe the surface with a clean cotton wiper, G00034.
c) Apply conversion coating to the bare fastener heads (CHEMICAL
CONVERSION COATING - CLEANING/PAINTING,
PAGEBLOCK 51-21-41/701).
NOTE: It is acceptable if the conversion coating touches the adjacent
surfaces.
51-21-72
F . Apply the BMS10-103, Type 1, Primer
SUBTASK 51-21-72-160-004
(1) Immediately before applying the primer, wipe the surface with a new tack rag, G50123, or use
clean, compressed air.
NOTE: This is to remove any particulate contamination.
SUBTASK 51-21-72-370-006
(2) Apply the BMS10-103, Type 1, primer, C00766, in a continuous coat that is free of runs,
wrinkles, and pinholes.
SUBTASK 51-21-72-370-008

(a) Air dry the BMS10-103, Grade A primer for a minimum of 30 minutes at a minimum of
65°F (18°C) before over-coating or re-coating.

SUBTASK 51-21-72-110-001

51-21-72

SUBTASK 51-21-72-110-002

the surface.

B01007.

4. BMS10-72 Primer - Application
A. General
(1) This procedure has steps to do the following:
(a) Prepare to apply BMS10-72 primer.
(b) Apply BMS10-72 primer.
(2) recommends that you apply BMS10-72 primer when the ambient air temperature is
between 65°F (18°C) and 95°F (35°C).
51-21-72
(a) Contact the paint manufacturer for guidance if you will apply BMS10-72 primer outside
the recommended temperature range.
(3) recommends that you apply BMS10-72 primer when the relative humidity is less than
70%.
(a) Contact the paint manufacturer for guidance if you will apply BMS10-72 primer when the
relative humidity is more than 70%.
B. Tools/Equipment
NOTE: When more than one tool part number is listed under the same "Reference" number, the
tools shown are alternates to each other within the same airplane series. Tool part numbers
that are replaced or non-procurable are preceded by "Opt:", which stands for Optional.

D. Prepare to the BMS10-72 Primer Application
SUBTASK 51-21-72-950-002
(1) Mask the surface adjacent to the area you will apply primer.
(a) Attach the tags that follow to the left control wheel in the flight deck:
1) "PITOT PROBES COVERED" tag, G02447
2) "STATIC PORTS COVERED" tag, G02444
3) A red tag labeled “AOA SENSORS COVERED”.
(b) Put a pitot probe cover, COM-1503, on the pitot probe.
(c) Apply Scotch Flatback Masking T ape 250, G00270, on the static ports.
(d) Do the steps that follow to cover the Angle-Of-Attack (AOA) sensors:
1) Cover the AOA sensors with PG-777 solvent-resistant masking tape.
a) Apply PG-777 over the blade, plate, and blade-to-plate gap.
NOTE: This is to allow easier removal of 3M 425 aluminum tape.
2) Cover the PG-777 solvent-resistant masking tape with 3M 425 Aluminum Foil T ape,
G00291.
a) Shingle the aluminum foil tape from the blade-to-plate gap up to the blade tip
until all of the masking tape is covered.
SUBTASK 51-21-72-370-019
(2) Mix the BMS10-72, Type VIII primer coating, C50084, or BMS10-72, Type IX primer coating,
C50085, or BMS10-72, Type X primer coating, C50318, using the manufacturer's instructions
(Table 707).
NOTE: The BMS10-72, Type VIII primer has a base component and a curing solution
component.
NOTE: The BMS10-72, Type IX and Type X primers have a base component, a curing solution
component, and a thinner component.

(Table 707).

b) Add the thinner component at a rate less than 15 seconds per gallon.
(c) Mix the primer solution every 30 minutes before you apply the primer.

If monocoat is to be applied, and the primer has been dried for 1 to 4 hours at 105°F (41°C) to 120°F (49°C), then the
total time allowed at temperatures 65°F (18°C) to 89°F (32°C) is 12 hours.
If basecoat is to be applied, and the primer has been dried for 1 to 2 hours at 105°F (41°C) to 120°F (49°C), then the
total time allowed at temperatures 65°F (18°C) to 89°F (32°C) is 24 hours.
(a) Within 2 hours of the application of primer, cure the primer at a minimum of 95°F (35°C)
for a minimum of 60 minutes.
(b) If the dry time to recoat or overcoat is more than the requirements in Table 708, do the
steps that follow to reactivate the surface:

<1> Scotch-Brite 7447 pad, G50220
<2> Scotch-Brite Clean and Finish, very fine, aluminum oxide
<3> Norton Bear-T ex 747 General Purpose Hand Pad, very fine, aluminum
oxide.

<1> Make sure that the solvent does not dry on the surface.

a) Start sanding with one of the abrasives that follow:
51-21-72
<1> Aluminum oxide/zirconium oxide abrasive paper, stearate-free, 100-grit
to 220-grit
<2> Scotch-Brite 7447 pad, G50220, 100-grit to 220-grit

220-grit
<4> Norton Bear-T ex 747 General Purpose Hand Pad, very fine, aluminum
oxide, 100-grit to 220-grit.

<1> Aluminum oxide/zirconium oxide abrasive paper, stearate-free, 220-grit
or finer
<2> Scotch-Brite 7447 pad, G50220, 220-grit or finer
<3> Scotch-Brite Clean and Finish, very fine, aluminum oxide, 220-grit or
finer
<4> Norton Bear-T ex 747 General Purpose Hand Pad, very fine, aluminum
oxide, 220-grit or finer.

NOTE: This is to remove sanding dust.
a) Use a wiper, G50262, or one of the abrasives that follow wet with Series 85
solvent, B01005:
<1> Scotch-Brite 7447 pad, G50220
<2> Scotch-Brite Clean and Finish, very fine, aluminum oxide
<3> Norton Bear-T ex 747 General Purpose Hand Pad, very fine, aluminum
oxide.
b) Immediately wipe the surface dry with a clean, dry wiper, G50262.
<1> Make sure that the solvent does not dry on the surface.
4) Wipe the surface with a tack rag, G50123.
5) Apply a coat of BMS10-72, Type VIII primer coating, C50084, or BMS10-72, Type IX
primer coating, C50085, or BMS10-72, Type X primer coating, C50318, to the
surface.
(c) If the temperature for drying to recoat or overcoat is warmer than 120°F (49°C), do the
steps that follow to reactivate the surface:
1) Solvent clean the surface, if necessary to remove contamination.
a) Use a cotton wiper, G00034, or one of the abrasives that follow wet with

<1> Scotch-Brite 7447 pad, G50220
<2> Scotch-Brite Clean and Finish, very fine, aluminum oxide
<3> Norton Bear-T ex 747 General Purpose Hand Pad, very fine, aluminum
oxide.
b) Immediately wipe the surface dry with a clean, dry cotton wiper, G00034.
<1> Make sure that the solvent does not dry on the surface.
2) Sand the surface.
a) Start sanding with one of the abrasives that follow:
51-21-72
<1> Aluminum oxide/zirconium oxide abrasive paper, stearate-free, 100-grit
to 220-grit
<2> Scotch-Brite 7447 pad, G50220, 100-grit to 220-grit
<3> Scotch-Brite Clean and Finish, very fine, aluminum oxide, 100-grit to

<4> Norton Bear-T ex 747 General Purpose Hand Pad, very fine, aluminum

<2> Scotch-Brite 7447 pad, G50220, 220-grit or finer
<3> Scotch-Brite Clean and Finish, very fine, aluminum oxide, 220-grit or

<4> Norton Bear-T ex 747 General Purpose Hand Pad, very fine, aluminum

3) Solvent clean the surface, if it is necessary to remove contamination.
NOTE: This is to remove sanding dust.
a) Use a cotton wiper, G00034, or one of the abrasives that follow wet with

<1> Scotch-Brite 7447 pad, G50220
<2> Scotch-Brite Clean and Finish, very fine, aluminum oxide
<3> Norton Bear-T ex 747 General Purpose Hand Pad, very fine, aluminum

<1> Make sure that the solvent does not dry on the surface.

<4> Ridoline 909, B50052
<5> Super Bee 300LF
<6> Turco 4215 NC-LT alkaline cleaner, B50240
3) Rinse the surface with large amounts of water.
4) Do a check for a water-break-free surface.
a) If water breaks occur, alkaline clean the surface again and rinse with more
water.

*[1] Dry-to-tape times are approximate. T ape after removal shall have no visible effects on the film.
*[2] When maximum dry times are exceeded, or if topcoat has been heat cured at temperatures warmer than 110°F
(43°C), reactivate the surface.
*[3] Some colors may require longer or shorter overcoat time due to cure characteristics, temperature, and humidity.
*[4] Measurement does not include primer thickness.
*[5] CA8000C1 or CA8000C2 may be substituted for CA8000C for accelerated curing. The thinner blend in the paint mix
must not contain both CA8000C1 and CA8000C2 at the same time. Use of thinners containing CA8000C1 or
CA8000C2 thinner may only be used on areas where the reduced wet edge and time between coats does not
adversely affect the final appearance of the topcoat, including but not limited to rotation areas, speed stripers, door
bands, and outdoor work areas.
*[6] CA8000C4 or CA8000C5 may be substituted 1:1 for CA8000C for accelerated curing. CA8000C4 or CA8000C5 may
only be used for outdoor touch-up or repair. CA8000C4 and CA8000C5 may not be used if the outside air
temperature is warmer than 60°F (16°C).
*[7] TR-111, TR-112, TR-113, or TR-128 may be substituted for TR-109 for accelerated curing. The thinner blend in the
paint mix must not simultaneously contain some combination of TR-111, TR-112, TR-113, and TR-128. Thinners
containing TR-111, TR-112, TR-113, or TR-128 thinner may only be used on areas where the reduced wet edge and
time between coats does not adversely affect the final appearance of the topcoat, including but not limited to rotation
areas, speed stripes, door bands, and outdoor work areas.
*[8] Paint mixed with TR-128 may be heat cured at 90°F (32°C) for 4 hours if subsequent colors are to be applied over the
base coat.
*[9] TR-109 may be blended with TR-112 to achieve a 4-6 hour cure at 90°F (32°C).
*[10] TR-141 alternate thinner may be substituted 1:1 for TR-109 standard thinner. It is intended to increase wet edge time
when the ambient air temperature is 85°F (29°C) to 100°F (38°C).

3. BMS10-60 Topcoat Application
A. General
(1) This procedure has steps for the following.
(a) Prepare the surface
(b) Apply BMS10-60
(2) recommends that you apply the BMS10-60 topcoat when the outside air temperature is
between 65°F (18°C) and 95°F (35°C).
(a) Contact the paint manufacturer for guidance if you will apply the paint outside the
recommended temperature range.
(3) recommends you apply the BMS10-60 topcoat when the relative humidity is less than
70%.
(a) If the relative humidity is more than 70%, you can force cure the coating at temperatures
between 120°F (49°C) and 160°F (71°C).
B. Tools/Equipment
NOTE: When more than one tool part number is listed under the same "Reference" number, the
tools shown are alternates to each other within the same airplane series. Tool part numbers
that are replaced or non-procurable are preceded by "Opt:", which stands for Optional.

D. Prepare the Surface
SUBTASK 51-21-73-950-004
(1) Mask the surface adjacent to the area you will apply topcoat.
(a) Attach the tags that follow to the left control wheel in the flight deck.
1) "PITOT PROBES COVERED" tag, G02447
2) "STATIC PORTS COVERED" tag, G02444
3) A red tag labeled “AOA SENSORS COVERED”
(b) Put a pitot probe cover, COM-1503 on the pitot probe.
(c) Apply Scotch Flatback Masking T ape 250, G00270 on the static ports.
(d) Do the steps that follow to cover the angle-of-attack (AOA) sensors.
1) Cover the angle-of-attack sensors with PG-777 solvent-resistant masking tape.
a) Apply PG-777 over the blade, plate, and blade-to-plate gap.
NOTE: This is to allow easier removal of 3M 425 aluminum tape.
2) Cover the PG-777 solvent-resistant masking tape with 3M 425 Aluminum Foil T ape,
G00291.
a) Shingle the aluminum foil tape from the blade-to-plate gap up to the blade tip
until all of the masking tape is covered.
SUBTASK 51-21-73-370-020
(2) Determine the method you will use to prepare the surface (TABLE, Table 705).

SUBTASK 51-21-73-370-021
(3) Prepare the surface with one of the methods that follow:
(a) Method 1
1) Solvent clean the surface using a wiper, G50262 or one of the abrasives that follow
wet with Series 85 solvent, B01005.
a) Scotch-Brite 7447 pad, G50220
b) Scotch-Brite Clean and Finish, very fine, aluminum oxide
c) Norton Bear-T ex 747 General Purpose Hand Pad, very fine, aluminum oxide
2) Immediately wipe the surface dry with a clean, dry wiper, G50262.
a) Make sure that the solvent does not dry on the surface.
3) Sand the surface with one of the abrasives that follow.
a) 220-grit or finer sandpaper, G50260
b) 240 grit or finer abrasive paper, G50077
c) Aluminum oxide abrasive paper, very fine
d) Scotch-Brite 7447 pad, G50220
e) Scotch-Brite Clean and Finish, very fine, aluminum oxide
4) Solvent clean the surface using a wiper, G50262 wet with Series 85 solvent,
B01005.
5) Immediately wipe the surface dry with a clean, dry wiper, G50262.
a) Make sure that the solvent does not dry on the surface.
6) Repeat the solvent cleaning, if necessary.
(b) Method 2
1) Solvent clean the surface using a wiper, G50262 or one of the abrasives that follow
wet with Series 85 solvent, B01005.
a) Scotch-Brite 7447 pad, G50220
b) Scotch-Brite Clean and Finish, very fine, aluminum oxide
c) Norton Bear-T ex 747 General Purpose Hand Pad, very fine, aluminum oxide
2) Sand the surface with one of the abrasives that follow.
a) 220-grit or finer sandpaper, G50260
b) 240 grit or finer abrasive paper, G50077
c) Aluminum oxide abrasive paper, very fine
d) Scotch-Brite 7447 pad, G50220
e) Scotch-Brite Clean and Finish, very fine, aluminum oxide
3) Solvent clean the surface using a wiper, G50262 wet with Series 85 solvent,
B01005.
51-21-73
4) Immediately wipe the surface dry with a clean, dry wiper, G50262.
a) Make sure that the solvent does not dry on the surface.
5) Repeat the solvent cleaning, if necessary.
6) Apply one coat of the applicable primer.
NOTE: This is the reactivation coat.
(c) Method 3
1) Sand the surface with one of the abrasives that follow.
a) 220-grit or finer sandpaper, G50260
b) 240 grit or finer abrasive paper, G50077

2) Solvent clean the surface using a wiper, G50262 wet with Series 85 solvent,
B01005.
3) Immediately wipe the surface dry with a clean, dry wiper, G50262.
a) Make sure that the solvent does not dry on the surface.
4) Repeat the solvent cleaning, if necessary.

4. BMS10-11 Topcoat Application
A. General
(1) This task gives instructions to do the following.
(a) Prepare to Apply the BMS10-11 Topcoat
(b) Apply the BMS10-11 Topcoat
(2) recommends that you apply the BMS10-11 chemical-resistant and solvent-resistant

(a) Contact the paint manufacturer for guidance if the finish temperature is colder than 50°F
(10°C).
(3) recommends that you apply the BMS10-11 chemical-resistant and solvent-resistant
finish when the relative humidity is less than 85%.
(a) Contact the paint manufacturer for guidance if the relative humidity is more than 85%.

5. Chemical Reactivator Application
A. General
(1) This procedure has steps for the following.
(a) Prepare to apply the Sur-Prep AP-1 chemical reactivator.
(b) Apply the Sur-Prep AP-1 chemical reactivator.
(2) recommends that you apply Sur-Prep AP-1 when the ambient air temperature is
between 65°F (18°C) and 90°F (32°C).
(a) Contact the paint manufacturer for guidance if you will apply Sur-Prep AP-1 outside the
recommended temperature range.
(3) recommends that you apply Sur-Prep AP-1 when the relative humidity is less than
70%.
(a) Contact the paint manufacturer for guidance if you will apply Sur-Prep AP-1 when the
relative humidity is more than 70%.
(4) Do not apply Sur-Prep AP-1 when the relative humidity at the application temperature is below
that in Figure 706.

C. Procedure
SUBTASK 51-21-81-120-002
(1) Prepare the Surface to be Finished.
WARNING
DO NOT GET SOLVENTS IN YOUR MOUTH, YOUR EYES, OR ON YOUR
SKIN. DO NOT BREATHE THE FUMES FROM SOLVENTS. SOLVENTS
ARE DANGEROUS MATERIALS. SOLVENTS CAN BE FLAMMABLE.
OBEY THE MATERIAL SAFETY DATA SHEETS (MSDS) FOR SOLVENTS.
OBEY LOCAL REGULATIONS FOR THE CORRECT PROCEDURES TO
USE OR DISCARD SOLVENTS. SOLVENTS CAN CAUSE INJURIES TO
PERSONNEL AND DAMAGE TO EQUIPMENT .
(a) Clean the surface with a clean cotton wiper, G00034 that is moist with one of the solvents
as follows:
1) alcohol, B00068
2) alcohol, B00130
3) solvent, B00666
(b) Dry the surface with a clean cotton wiper, G00034 before the solvent dries.
CAUTION
ONLY USE APPROVED SCRAPERS ON THE AIRPLANE SKIN.
SCRAPERS THAT ARE NOT APPROVED CAN MAKE SCRATCHES ON
THE SKIN, AND CAUSE FATIGUE CRACKS.
51-21-81
(CAUTION PRECEDES)
CAUTION
DO NOT USE ABRASIVE PADS (SCOTCH-BRITE), OR ABRASIVE PAPER
ON THE ALUMINUM SURFACE UNLESS THE SCRIBE LINE
INSPECTIONS WERE MADE. ABRASIVE PADS CAN SMOOTH THE
ALUMINUM SURFACE AND HIDE THE SCRIBE LINE MARKS. IF YOU
USE ABRASIVE PADS TO REMOVE THE PAINT OR PRIMER BEFORE
YOU DO THE SCRIBE LINE INSPECTIONS, YOU CAN BE REQUIRED TO
DO REPEAT INSPECTIONS BASED ON THE SCRIBE LINE INSPECTION
SERVICE BULLETIN.
(c) Remove all unwanted material from the surface. Use an approved sealant removal tool,
COM-2481 with sealant removal tool handle, COM-4746 to clean around fasteners,
seams, and lap joints.
NOTE: If you use abrasive pads before you do scribe line inspections, you can be
required to do repeat inspections based on the scribe line inspections Service
Bulletin 737-53A1289.
NOTE: An abrasive mat, G00251 or abrasive cloth, B00102 may be used instead of the
scraper.
(d) Clean the surface again with a cotton wiper, G00034 that is moist with the solvent.
(e) Dry the surface with a clean cotton wiper, G00034 before the solvent dries.

NOTE: BMS 10-86, Type I is a non-decorative, sprayable material with a minimum pot life of 2
hours.
(a) Clean all equipment before you mix the contents of the BMS 10–86, Type I coating,
C00304.
(b) Follow the manufacturer’s instructions to mix the contents of the BMS 10–86 Type I
coating, C00304.
(c) Follow the manufacturer’s instructions to achieve the required viscosity of the BMS 10–86
Type I coating, C00304.
(d) Apply Scotch Flatback Masking T ape 250, G00270, on all adjacent areas.
51-21-81
(e) Use a spray gun, STD-9077 to apply the BMS 10-86 Type I coating, C00304.
(f) Let each layer of BMS 10–86 Type I coating, C00304, dry for 10 minutes.
(g) Apply a total of four or five layers.
(h) Follow the manufacturer’s instructions before you touch the surface.
(i) Follow the manufacturer’s instructions to cure the BMS 10–86 Type I coating, C00304.
(j) Remove the Scotch Flatback Masking T ape 250, G00270.

2. Surface Preparation for Corrosion Inhibiting Compound
A. Consumable Materials
Reference Description Specification
B00074 Solvent - Degreasing MIL-PRF-680
(Supersedes P-D-680)
B00093 Solvent - T etrachloroethylene
(Perchloroethylene)

Coatings)
TT-N-95 Type I, ASTM
D-3735 Type I
B00634 Solvent - Stabilized Limonene Cleaner BMS11-10 Type 1, 2, or
3
51-21-91
(Continued)
Reference Description Specification
G00034 Cotton Wiper - Process Cleaning Absorbent
Wiper (Cheesecloth, Gauze)
AMS3819 Class 1
Grade A or B Form 1
(Supersede BMS15-5
CL A)
G00270 T ape - Scotch Flatback Masking 250 ASTM D6123
(Supersedes A-A-883)
G50305 Sheeting - Plastic, Polyethylene
B. Procedure
SUBTASK 51-21-91-620-001
(1) Do these steps to prepare the surface for the corrosion inhibiting compound.
(a) Do these steps to clean the surfaces:
1) Vacuum the surfaces as necessary to remove moisture and other excessive foreign
material.
2) Wipe the surfaces with a cloth (cotton wiper, G00034) as necessary to remove
additional moisture or other foreign materials.
WARNING
DO NOT GET THE SOLVENT IN YOUR MOUTH, EYES, OR ON YOUR
SKIN. DO NOT BREATHE IN THE FUMES FROM THE SOLVENT . MAKE
SURE TO PUT ON PROTECTIVE SPLASH GOGGLES AND GLOVES
WHEN USING THE SOLVENT . KEEP THE SOLVENT AWAY FROM
SPARKS, FLAMES AND HEAT . THE SOLVENT IS POISONOUS AND
FLAMMABLE AND CAN CAUSE INJURY OR DAMAGE IF NOT HANDLED
PROPERLY .
(b) When the area is very dirty, clean the surface with one of these applicable solvents:
NOTE: You must fully clean the surface to help the corrosion inhibiting compound get on
the surface and in the mating surfaces.
1) solvent, B00074.
2) solvent, B00093.
3) solvent, B00316.
4) Limonene solvent, B00634
SUBTASK 51-21-91-600-003
(2) Apply Scotch Flatback Masking T ape 250, G00270 and plastic sheeting, G50305 to the area
where you will not apply the corrosion preventive compound.
SUBTASK 51-21-91-600-002
WARNING
DO NOT APPLY THE CORROSION-INHIBITING COMPOUNDS IN THE AREAS
THAT HAVE OXYGEN SYSTEM COMPONENTS. THE MIXTURE OF
CORROSION-INHIBITING COMPOUNDS, AND OXYGEN CAN CAUSE AN
EXPLOSION. AN EXPLOSION CAN CAUSE INJURIES TO PERSONS, AND
DAMAGE TO EQUIPMENT .
(3) Make sure you keep all corrosion inhibiting compounds out of the oxygen system components
that follow:
NOTE: Masking tape is not sufficient. Use plastic sheets or equivalent covers to fully seal
these components from the compound.
51-21-91
(a) Bottles.
(b) Pressure metering equipment.
(c) Indicating equipment.
(d) Connections.
SUBTASK 51-21-91-280-001
(4) Do not apply corrosion inhibiting compound to the areas that follow:
(a) Near the engines.
(b) Cowlings.
(c) Areas of high temperature or where firewall sealant is used.
NOTE: High temperature causes deterioration in corrosion preventive compounds.
(d) Electrical ground strap/surface interfaces to make sure you have a sufficient ground path.
(e) Bare surfaces that get a layer of primer and paint.
NOTE: It is not easy to apply a primer and paint on the corrosion preventive compound.
The corrosion preventive compound gets in the surface and can bleed-out. This
can cause the primer and paint to have a unsatisfactory bond.
1) Let the primer and enamel dry for a minimum of 8 hours before you apply the
corrosion preventive compound.
(f) Sliding pins and other mechanical joints in a sliding surface contact.
(g) Actuator rods.
NOTE: Corrosion preventive compound such as BMS 3-23 or all other hydrocarbons can
cause damage to the seals that are used with BMS 3-11 hydraulic fluid.
SUBTASK 51-21-91-600-001
(5) The areas that follow must be shielded or protected to make sure the corrosion inhibiting
compound is not applied:
(a) Control cables and pulleys.
(b) Wire bundles, coax connectors.
(c) Mechanical silicone seals.
(d) Insulation blankets.
(e) Bushing and bearings.
(f) Cam surfaces.
(g) Lubricated components.
(h) Pneumatic ducts with surface temperatures that are more than 200°F (93°C) during
normal operations.
(i) Applicable drain valves.
(j) Areas that will be painted or sealed.
(k) High visual items such as doorways, hatches, and window frames.
51-21-91-620-802Application of Corrosion Inhibiting CompoundPDF p. 350
Open original AMM PDF - page 350 ↗ 
3. Application of Corrosion Inhibiting Compound
A. General
WARNING
THE BMS3-23 AND BMS3-29 ARE HAZARDOUS MATERIALS. WHEN YOU
SPRAY THESE COMPOUNDS IN A CLOSED AREA, A GAS CONCENTRATION
OF 500 PPM (PARTS PER MILLION) IS THE MAXIMUM COMFORTABLE AND
SAFE WORKING LIMIT . AT THIS CONCENTRATION, A PERSON CAN WORK
FOR 8 HOURS. GAS LEVELS MORE THAN 500 PPM ARE NOT FULLY
HAZARDOUS, BUT YOU MUST SUPPLY A FLOW OF AIR TO KEEP THE AREA
COMFORTABLE AND SAFE. IF YOU DO NOT FOLLOW THESE
INSTRUCTIONS, INJURY TO PERSONS CAN OCCUR.
WARNING
ALIPHATIC NAPHTHA IS THE RECOMMENDED SOLVENT AND IS ALSO
POISONOUS. IF YOU USE NAPHTHA IN A CONFINED SPACE, MECHANICAL
VENTILATION IS MANDATORY . RESPIRATORY DEVICES AND SKIN
PROTECTION ARE NECESSARY . IF YOU DO NOT FOLLOW THESE
INSTRUCTIONS, INJURY TO PERSONS CAN OCCUR.
(1) This procedure gives instructions to apply the water-displacing corrosion preventive
compound. We recommended that you apply the compound periodically.
NOTE: Each airline must know it's aircraft's environment, the corrosion inhibiting compounds
they use, and when it was applied. If not, the corrosion protection can be
unsatisfactory. recommends BMS3-23, BMS3-26, BMS3-29 and BMS3-35 and
has made them a standard for organic corrosion inhibitors. But, there are other
water-displacing corrosion inhibiting compounds that are satisfactory for use.
WARNING
KEEP BMS3-23 AWAY FROM SURFACES THAT CAN BE 150 DEGREES F (66
DEGREES C) OR MORE DURING USE. THE DRY FILM FLASH POINT IS 500
DEGREES F(260 DEGREES C). THE BMS 3-23 CAN CAUSE A FIRE OR AN
EXPLOSION.
(2) The corrosion preventive compound is not satisfactory for surfaces that can be more than
150°F (66°C) because it contains wax compounds.
B. Consumable Materials

(c) The application temperature for BMS3-23, BMS3-26 Type I and BMS3-29, must be
between 40°F (4°C) and 100°F (38°C).
(d) The application temperature for BMS3-26 Type II must be between 50°F (10°C) and
100°F (38°C).
(e) The application temperature for BMS3-35 must be between 32°F (0°C) and 86°F (30°C).
(f) Spray or brush the BMS3-23 compound to get a continuous coat.
(g) Spray or brush either the BMS3-26, BMS3-29 or BMS3-35 compound to get a bubble
free coat (after it is dry), with a wet or dry thickness as specified in Table 701.
NOTE: When the BMS3-26, BMS3-29 and BMS3-35 compound is not sprayed, shake or
mix the compound before it is used.
(h) When the compound is applied to parts where it cannot be sprayed or brushed (e.g.
slotted tracks), do the step that follows:
1) Fill the area with the compound, and then drain the compound which will give a
protective coat.
(i) When BMS3-29 is specified as an optional material to BMS3-23, use the same
specifications as for the BMS3-29.
(j) Use a cloth (cotton wiper, G00034) to remove the puddles of too much of the compound if
the puddles cause a blockage to the drain holes or cross drains.
51-21-91
(k) Make sure that you have a good flow of air in the areas where the corrosion inhibiting
compound was applied, as follows:
NOTE: The air flow times are recommended minimum times. A longer air flow time to get
a satisfactory evaporation is recommended for lower temperatures, high humidity,
and when too much of the compound is applied.
NOTE: You must keep the temperature within the application limits for the material used.
1) After you apply the BMS3–23 in enclosed areas, apply a good air flow to the area
for a minimum of one hour before you close-up the area.
NOTE: The one hour will let the volatile solvents in the compound to evaporate.
2) After you apply the BMS3-26, BMS3–29 or the BMS3-35 in enclosed areas, apply a
good air flow to the area for a minimum of four hours before you close-up the area.
3) Do not remove the masking tape or applicable covers until the corrosion inhibiting
compound has dried sufficiently. This will make sure that the compound will not rub
off or move to another surface.
a) When the masking tape or covers have been removed, do a touch-up of the
corrosion inhibiting compound where it is necessary to prevent corrosion in the
areas of a structural interface.
(l) Do not contaminate the insulation blankets with the corrosion inhibiting compound.
NOTE: These compounds can decrease the ability of the insulation blankets to repel
water.
WARNING
DO NOT USE DETERGENTS OR SOLVENTS TO CLEAN THE
INSULATION BLANKET . IT CAN REMOVE FLAME RETARDANTS
AND CAUSE FLAMMABLE RESIDUES ON THE INSULATION
BLANKET WHICH INCREASES THE RISK OF FIRE. THIS CAN
CAUSE INJURIES TO PERSONNEL, AND DAMAGE TO EQUIPMENT .
1) If contamination occurs, the blanket must be replaced.

4. Cure of Corrosion Inhibiting Compound
A. General
(1) This task to cure the corrosion inhibiting compound is not necessary when the insulation
blankets will not be touched by the compound.
(2) Cure the corrosion inhibiting compound at the specified application temperature.
B. Consumable Materials
Reference Description Specification
G00252 Film - Polyethylene Film And Sheeting ASTM D2103
(Supersedes L-P-512)
C. Cure of Corrosion Inhibiting Compound
SUBTASK 51-21-91-620-003
(1) Sufficiently cure the corrosion inhibiting compound.
NOTE: This is to make sure the compound does not move from the surface if it is touched by
the insulation blankets.
(a) The test that follows will tell you if the compound is fully cured.
51-21-91
1) Get some polyethylene film, G00252, 0.002 in. (0.051 mm) to 0.006 in. (0.152 mm)
in thickness.
2) Push the polyethylene film, G00252 against the corrosion inhibiting compound with
a light finger pressure.
3) Make a check to see if the corrosion inhibiting compound has moved to the
polyethylene film, G00252 or lifted from the structure.
(b) The table that follows will give you the approximate cure times.
NOTE: Follow the manufacturers instruction for the applicable cure times.
Table 702/51-21-91-993-802
Approximate Cure Times
Compound Time
BMS3-23 *[1] 1 Hour
BMS3-26, Type I 6 Hours
BMS3-26, Type II 6 Hours
BMS3–29 4 Hours
BMS3–35 2 Hours
*[1] BMS3–23 Class 1, will not cure in 1 hour, however, this is a sufficient amount of time to allow solvents to flash off
prior to installation of insulation blankets. BMS3–23 Class 1, does not cure to a tack free coating.

5. Repair of Corrosion Inhibiting Compound Coating
A. General
(1) The limits of the damage or bubbles in the BMS3-23, BMS3-26, BMS3-29 or BMS3-35
corrosion inhibiting compound in the cured coating is as follows and must be repaired:
(a) The coating damage dimension is more than 1 percent of a given 1 ft 2 (929 cm 2).
(b) The coating damage dimension is larger than 1 in 2 (6 cm 2).
(2) All damage or bubbles in the cured BMS3–23, BMS3-26, BMS3-29 and BMS3-35 coating must
be repaired.
NOTE: When BMS3-29 or BMS3-35 is specified as optional to BMS3-23, the damage
permitted will be the same as that necessary for the BMS3-23.

3

2. Seat Track Filler Application
(Figure 201, Figure 202)
A. General
(1) Seat track filler prevents corrosion in wet areas of the passenger compartment.
(2) You can cut and remove parts of the seat track fillers when you remove and install galleys,
lavs, and tie down fittings.
B. Consumable Materials
Reference Description Specification
A02315 Sealant - Low Density, Synthetic Rubber. 2
Part
BMS5-142 Type II
C00308 Compound - Corrosion Preventive, Petrolatum
Hot Application
MIL-C-11796
G02304 T ape - Moisture Barrier, Adhesive One-side BMS8-346
C. Location Zones
Zone Area
200 Upper Half of Fuselage
D. Procedure
SUBTASK 51-21-92-620-001
(1) Apply filler sealant, A02315 in the seat tracks as follows:
(a) Find the seat track locations in the wet areas of the airplane.
(b) Cut the seat track filler [1] to fit in the seat tracks [3].
NOTE: The seat track filler is pre- formed and commercially available.
NOTE: A gap of 0.25 in. (6.35 mm) is permitted at the skin splice areas.
(c) Install the seat track filler [1] in the seat track [3].
(d) Fill the seat track [3] with corrosion preventive compound, C00308.
(e) Install approximately 2.0 in. (5.1 cm) of sealant, A02315 at the end of the compound filled
area to contain the compound.
(f) Make sure the top surface of the seat track [3] is clean and free of compound.
(g) Install moisture barrier tape, G02304 [2] on the compound filled seat track [3].

NOTE: Potlife is 6 hours at 60°F (16°C) to 85°F (29°C) after mixing.
SUBTASK 51-21-93-370-009
(6) Apply the Alexit Suede 404-74 Topcoat coating, C50143 on the primed surface with a spray
gun.
NOTE: Avoid dry spray. Dry spray will result in a rough surface. Additionally, heavy coats may
result in color deviation (depending on pigmentation/color), runs/sagging and cracking
of the dry film. In addition, varying amounts of thinner may cause color deviation.
NOTE: For application of topcoat to rivet heads and screw heads, place stencil flush to the
painted surface having a hole in it equal to the rivet or screw head being painted.
Using an airbrush (or equivalent), spray apply the topcoat through the hole in the
stencil until the dry finish matches the rest of the panel.
SUBTASK 51-21-93-370-010
(7) Cure the Alexit Suede 404-74 Topcoat coating, C50143. Refer to (Figure 701) for the cure
times.
NOTE: Curing at 70°F (21°C) (minimum) is 4 hrs. (minimum).

C. Prepare the Surface
SUBTASK 51-21-94-180-001
(1) Prepare the aluminum surfaces.
(a) Apply a layer of chromic acid anodizing or alodizing to all aluminum surfaces.
(b) Apply a layer of primer, C00259, epoxy primer.
(c) Let the primers dry before you apply the abrasion resistant finish.
1) Let the primers dry for a minimum of 1 hour at room temperature or 15 minutes at
150° ±15°F (65.6° ± 8.3°C).
SUBTASK 51-21-94-180-002
(2) Prepare the painted surfaces.
(a) If it is less than 48 hours since you applied the BMS 10-11 epoxy primer:
1) Clean the surface with solvent, B00094.
2) Dry the surface with a clean, dry cotton wiper, G00034.
(b) If it is more than 48 hours since you applied the BMS 10-11 epoxy primer:
1) Clean the surface with solvent, B00094 or solvent, B00151.
2) Rub the surface with 320-grit (minimum) abrasive cloth, B00102 aluminum oxide
paper.
3) Clean the surface again with Methyl Isobutyl Ketone.
SUBTASK 51-21-94-100-001
(3) Prepare the steel surfaces.
(a) Apply one layer of primer, C00259, epoxy primer on the surface.
51-21-94
D. Apply the Abrasion Resistant Finish
SUBTASK 51-21-94-370-001
WARNING
DO NOT GET THE FINISHES, CLEANERS, THINNERS AND SOLVENTS IN
YOUR MOUTH OR EYES, OR ON YOUR SKIN OR CLOTHES. DO NOT
BREATHE THE FUMES FROM THESE MATERIALS. MAKE SURE THE AIR
FLOWS FREELY THROUGH THE WORK AREA. USE THE NECESSARY
RESPIRATORY PROTECTION. KEEP THESE MATERIALS AWAY FROM
SPARKS, FLAME AND HEAT . THESE MATERIALS ARE POISONOUS AND
FLAMMABLE AND CAN CAUSE INJURY TO PERSONS AND DAMAGE TO
EQUIPMENT .
WARNING
DO NOT GET TERECO 155 ON YOUR SKIN. TERECO 155 CONTAINS
DANGEROUS COMPOUNDS THAT CAN CAUSE INJURY .
(1) Mix the abrasion resistant finish.
NOTE: The abrasion resistant finish has T ereco 155 base material (part A) and T ereco 155
accelerator (part B). Mix each component fully before you mix parts A and B together.
(a) If you apply the finish with a medium nylon bristle brush, STD-3911:
1) Add 25 parts (by weight) of part B to 100 parts (by weight) of part A.
2) To make the solution thin, add 9 parts (by weight) of solvent, B00094, toluene or
solvent, B00344, xylene for each 125 parts (by weight) of the finish mixture.
NOTE: The thinner is added to part B before you mix part A and B.
(b) If you apply the finish with a spray paint spray gun with interchangeable nozzles,
STD-1133:
1) Add 25 parts (by weight) of part B to 100 parts (by weight) of part A.
2) To make the solution thin, add 20 to 30 parts (by weight) of the toluene or xylene for
each 125 parts (by weight) of the finish mixture.
NOTE: The thinner is added to part B before you mix part A and B.
(c) Do not touch or move the mixture for a minimum of 5 minutes before you use it.
(d) Do not use T ereco 155 with the expired application time (Figure 701).
(e) Remove material with the expired application time.
(f) Do not mix more T ereco 155 than you can use (Figure 701).
SUBTASK 51-21-94-950-001
(2) Apply tape or covers on areas adjacent to the work area.
SUBTASK 51-21-94-370-002
(3) Apply the abrasion resistant finish.
(a) Apply one or more layers of T ereco 155 with a brush or a spray gun.
(b) Let the finish dry between each layer.
(c) Make sure the dry layer is 0.003-0.020 inch (0.076-0.508 mm) thick.
(d) Let the finish dry for a minimum of 8 hours at 70° ±5°F (21.1° 2.8°C) before you touch the
surface or part.
(e) Let the finish dry for a minimum of 12 hours before flight.
51-21-94
(f) Before assembly, use the time-temperature conditions shown in Curve A to dry the finish
(Figure 702).
(g) Before functional movement of parts, use the time-temperature conditions shown in
Curve B to dry the finish (Figure 702).
CAUTION
DO NOT USE ABRASIVE PADS (SCOTCH-BRITE), OR ABRASIVE PAPER
ON THE ALUMINUM SURFACE UNLESS THE SCRIBE LINE
INSPECTIONS WERE MADE. ABRASIVE PADS CAN SMOOTH THE
ALUMINUM SURFACE AND HIDE THE SCRIBE LINE MARKS. IF YOU
USE ABRASIVE PADS TO REMOVE THE PAINT OR PRIMER BEFORE
YOU DO THE SCRIBE LINE INSPECTIONS, YOU CAN BE REQUIRED TO
DO REPEAT INSPECTIONS BASED ON THE SCRIBE LINE INSPECTION
SERVICE BULLETIN.
CAUTION
ONLY USE APPROVED SCRAPERS ON THE AIRPLANE SKIN.
SCRAPERS THAT ARE NOT APPROVED CAN MAKE SCRATCHES ON
THE SKIN, AND CAUSE FATIGUE CRACKS.
(h) Remove all of the sharp paint edges with a sealant removal tool, COM-2481 with sealant
removal tool handle, COM-4746.
NOTE: The shelf life of T ereco 155 is 6 months from the manufacture date.
NOTE: If you use abrasive pads before you do scribe line inspections, you can be
required to do repeat inspections based on the scribe line inspection Service
Bulletin 737-53A1289.
(i) Make sure not to cause damage to the aluminum skin.
E. Repair and Rework Damaged Surfaces
SUBTASK 51-21-94-350-001
(1) Rework areas where it is necessary.
(a) Clean the surfaces (Refer to SUBTASK 51-21-94-180-001, SUBTASK 51-21-94-180-002,
or SUBTASK 51-21-94-100-001).
(b) Apply the abrasion resistant finish (Refer to SUBTASK 51-21-94-370-001, SUBTASK
51-21-94-950-001, and SUBTASK 51-21-94-370-002).
(c) Remove all of the sharp paint edges with sealant removal tool, COM-2481 or other
applicable tool.
SUBTASK 51-21-94-350-002
(2) Repair the damaged areas of the abrasion resistant finish.
(a) Rub the surface with 280-grit (minimum) abrasive cloth, B00102 aluminum oxide paper.
1) If primer, C00259, has scratches after this step, apply the primer again.
2) Repair the aluminum surfaces that have scratches (Refer to SUBTASK
51-21-94-370-001, SUBTASK 51-21-94-950-001, SUBTASK 51-21-94-370-002,
SUBTASK 51-21-94-350-001, and SUBTASK 51-21-94-350-002)
(b) Clean the area with a wet cheesecloth that is moist with toluene.
(c) Apply abrasion resistant finish (Refer to SUBTASK 51-21-94-370-001, SUBTASK
51-21-94-950-001, and SUBTASK 51-21-94-370-002).

(4) Remove fastener sealant with solvent, B00666.

SUBTASK 51-21-95-160-001

(a) Use a portable blaster to remove rust from fastener heads and adjacent skin area.
(b) Use a portable blaster to remove corrosion from the skin to an approved depth.

cloth, B00102 paper or cloth.
(d) Use a 5 to 10X magnifying glass to examine the countersunk area around the fastener
heads for corrosion.

CONFIGURATION, REMOVE AND INSTALL EACH SECOND
FASTENERS.
(e) Remove the fasteners with corrosion in the countersunk area.
(f) Remove the corrosion from the fastener to an approved depth.
