D. Prepare the Surface for treatment
NOTE: It is not necessary to clean all areas for chemical conversion coating treatment. Only clean
the areas where skin or fasteners are open to the base metal during the removal of rust and
corrosion.
SUBTASK 51-21-95-580-001
(1) Park the airplane away from the sun and electrically ground (AIRPLANE GROUNDING -
MAINTENANCE PRACTICES, PAGEBLOCK 20-40-11/201).
51-21-95
SUBTASK 51-21-95-160-002
(2) Clean the surface correctly to get a satisfactory coating.
WARNING
DO NOT GET THE ADHESIVES, FINISHES, CLEANERS, THINNERS, AND
SOLVENTS IN YOUR MOUTH, OR EYES, OR ON YOUR SKIN. DO NOT
BREATH THE FUMES FROM THESE MATERIALS. PUT ON
RESPIRATOR, PROTECTIVE SPLASH GOGGLES, AND PROTECTIVE
GLOVES WHEN YOU USE THESE MATERIALS. KEEP THESE
MATERIALS AWAY FROM SPARKS, FLAME, AND HEAT . THESE
MATERIALS CAN CAUSE INJURIES TO PERSONS AND DAMAGE TO
EQUIPMENT .
(a) Prepare the solution as follows:
1) Use one part (by volume) solvent alkaline cleaner to two parts water and five to six
parts BMS 3-2 Type I solvent.
(b) Apply the cleaner solution and let it soak for a minimum of 10 minutes.
NOTE: The solution must be wet on the skins. Apply the cleaner again if it is necessary.
(c) Clean the skins with a soft bristle brush, STD-123.
1) Make sure to clean the countersunk areas and around the rivet heads.
(d) Flush the surface with water (100°F (37.8°C) maximum) at high pressure.
(e) Examine the surface for water breaks.
NOTE: A surface with no water breaks has a continuous layer of water for a minimum of
30 seconds.
(f) Clean the skin again if there are water breaks.
E. Apply the coating treatment
SUBTASK 51-21-95-370-001
(1) Prepare Coating:
NOTE: In areas where the chemical conversion coating may come in contact with BMS 10-20
primer, only Bonderite M-CR 600 Aero coating, C00862, should be used.
NOTE: Keep the room temperature above 65°F (18.3°C).
WARNING
DO NOT GET CHEMICAL CONVERSION COATING SOLUTION IN YOUR
MOUTH, IN YOUR EYES, ON YOUR SKIN, OR ON YOUR CLOTHES. DO
NOT BREATHE THE FUMES FROM THIS MATERIAL. AIR MUST FLOW
FREELY THROUGH THE WORK AREA. USE THE NECESSARY
RESPIRATORY PROTECTION. KEEP THIS MATERIAL AWAY FROM
SPARKS, FLAME, AND HEAT . THIS MATERIAL IS POISONOUS AND
FLAMMABLE AND CAN CAUSE INJURY TO PERSONS OR DAMAGE TO
EQUIPMENT .
(a) Put coating, C00064, on paper and use a roller to make the powder smooth.
(b) Mix the solution in a stainless steel container (1 gal) (4 Liter), STD-1118 or acid resistant
container, STD-1052.
NOTE: Do not use lead or glass.
(c) For Bonderite M-CR 600 Aero coating, C00862: Add 3 oz (85 g) of powder to water for
each gallon (3.8 liters) of final solution.
1) Stir until the powder is dissolved.
51-21-95
2) Add up to 2% by volume of Bonderite M-AD 22 Toner Aero surface treatment,
C50358, or ammonium hydroxide as required to adjust pH to between 1.5 and 2.0.
(d) For Bonderite M-CR ALCRM1200 Aero coating, C50357: 3 oz (85 g) of powder to water
for each gallon (3.8 liters) of final solution.
1) Stir until the powder is dissolved.
2) Keep the pH in the range of 1.50 - 1.90.
3) Adjust the pH down with nitric acid or ammonium hydroxide.
(e) For Bonderite M-CR 1200S Aero coating, C50005: Add 1.9 oz (53.9 g) – 2.1 oz (59.5 g)
of powder to water for each gallon (3.8 liters) of final solution.
(f) For Bonderite M-CR 1600 Aero coating, C50447: Add 0.8 fl-oz (23.7 ml) of additive for
each gallon (3.8 liters) of final solution.
1) Stir well to ensure a uniform mixture.
2) Add up to 2% by volume of Bonderite M-AD 22 Toner Aero surface treatment,
C50358, as required to adjust the pH to between 1.5 - 2.0.
(g) For Bonderite M-AD 1660 Aero coating Additive, C50448: Add 2.6 fl-oz (76.9 ml) of
additive for each gallon (3.8 liters) of final solution.
1) Stir well to ensure a uniform mixture.
2) Add up to 2% by volume of Bonderite M-AD 22 Toner Aero surface treatment,
C50358, as required to adjust the pH to between 1.5 - 2.0.
(h) For other coating, C00064, refer to manufacturer's instructions for the correct preparation.
(i) Fully mix the solution until the powder is dissolved. Ignore small quantities of material
which do not dissolve.
(j) Allow to stand for 1 hour before use.
NOTE: Prepare the solution in small quantities. Discard the solution after you use it if a
brush, sponge, or wiper was put into it or if contamination gets into the solution.
Do not add wetting agents or other materials to this solution.
(2) Apply coating, C00064.
NOTE: In areas where the chemical conversion coating may come in contact with BMS 10-20
primer, only Bonderite M-CR 600 Aero coating, C00862, should be used.
WARNING
DO NOT GET SOLVENTS IN YOUR MOUTH OR EYES, OR ON YOUR
SKIN. DO NOT BREATHE THE FUMES FROM THESE MATERIALS. PUT
ON A PROTECTIVE SPLASH GOGGLE AND GLOVES WHEN YOU USE
THESE MATERIALS. KEEP THESE MATERIALS AWAY FROM SPARKS,
FLAME, AND HEAT . THESE MATERIALS ARE POISONOUS AND
FLAMMABLE AND CAN CAUSE INJURY TO PERSONS OR DAMAGE TO
EQUIPMENT .
(a) apply coating, C00064, equally with a medium nylon bristle brush, STD-3911, or a cotton
wiper, G00034, or a cellulose sponge, or mop (cotton). Start from the bottom.
51-21-95
(b) Let the solution stay on the surface until a given color is found (approximately 2 - 3
minutes). Keep the area wet. Apply small quantities of solution with a cotton wiper,
G00034.
NOTE: Do not let the coating, C00064, solution dry until the color of the surface
changes.
NOTE: If the color does not come, if the solution breaks up into beads, or if a powdery
layer occurs, lightly sand area with 600-grit paper and apply the coating again.
(c) Carefully flush the surface with a cotton wiper, G00034, that is moist with water. Start
from the bottom.
NOTE: Be careful when you rub or dry the surface. Do not remove the coating or cause
scratches on the surface.
(d) Lightly rub the surface with a clean dry cotton wiper, G00034, to remove unwanted liquid.
Rub the surface until the liquid is gone.
(e) Immediately after use, fully clean the cloths in water and keep them in a container filled
with water.
(f) Air dry the surface.
NOTE: Dry at 120°F (48.9°C) (optimum), 130°F (54.4°C) (maximum).
(g) Apply the last finish or start adhesive bonding (Chapter 51 of the Structural Repair
Manual). Touch the parts or surface with rubber glove, STD-1086, and keep the parts or
surface dry and clean to prevent surface contamination.

D. Apply the Fastener Sealant
SUBTASK 51-21-95-110-004
(1) Clean the fasteners.
(a) Remove the lubricant before the installation of solvent, B00148, or solvent, B00090.
SUBTASK 51-21-95-110-005
(2) Clean the countersunk and other rework areas.
WARNING
DO NOT GET THE FINISHES, CLEANERS, THINNERS AND SOLVENTS
IN YOUR MOUTH OR EYES, OR ON YOUR SKIN OR CLOTHES. DO NOT
BREATHE THE FUMES FROM THESE MATERIALS. MAKE SURE THAT
THE AIR FLOWS FREELY THROUGH THE WORK-AREA. USE THE
NECESSARY RESPIRATORY PROTECTION. KEEP THESE MATERIALS
AWAY FROM SPARKS, FLAME AND HEAT . THESE MATERIALS ARE
POISONOUS AND FLAMMABLE AND CAN CAUSE INJURY OR DAMAGE.
(a) Remove the lubricant and other soil with a clean, moist cotton wiper, G00034, that
contains solvent, B00184.
(b) Dry the surfaces before the fastener and sealant installation.
SUBTASK 51-21-95-390-001
(3) Prepare the sealant.
(a) Refer to the manufacturer's instructions to mix the sealant.
(b) Mix the sealants as follows:
NOTE: The life of P/S 890 Class A and Class B is 1/2 or 2 hours. The life of PR 1432 is 1
hour. Mix only the quantity that you can use during the life of the sealant.
1) sealant, A00767 - 1 part A (accelerator) to 10 parts B (base)
2) sealant, A00767 - 1 part A (accelerator) to 7-1/2 parts B (base)
3) sealant, A00247 - 1 part A (accelerator) to 15 parts B (base)
SUBTASK 51-21-95-390-002
(4) Apply sealant, A00767.
51-21-95
(a) Apply a small drop of the mixture to the countersunk area or below the head of the
fastener.
(b) Install the fastener immediately.
WARNING
DO NOT GET SOLVENTS IN YOUR MOUTH OR EYES, OR ON YOUR
SKIN. DO NOT BREATHE THE FUMES FROM THESE MATERIALS. PUT
ON A PROTECTIVE SPLASH GOGGLE AND GLOVES WHEN YOU USE
THESE MATERIALS. KEEP THESE MATERIALS AWAY FROM SPARKS,
FLAME, AND HEAT . THESE MATERIALS ARE POISONOUS AND
FLAMMABLE AND CAN CAUSE INJURY TO PERSONS OR DAMAGE TO
EQUIPMENT .
(c) Remove all unwanted sealant with clean cotton wiper, G00034, that is wet with Series 92
solvent, B01012 (TASK 20-30-92-910-801).
SUBTASK 51-21-95-390-003
(5) Apply sealant, A00767.
(a) Use a brush to apply the sealant, A00767, sealant mixture to the rework area.
(b) Make sure that you apply the sealant into the cracks around the fastener.
NOTE: It is necessary to have a continuous layer of sealant on the rework surface.
SUBTASK 51-21-95-390-004
(6) Apply sealant, A00767, on top of the sealant, A00767.
(a) Use a sealant gun, STD-449, or a hardwood or plastic fillet smoothing spatula, STD-810,
to apply the sealant to the rework areas.
(b) If the sealant, A00767, is easy to apply, do these steps:
1) Apply the sealant until the surface of the sealant is not far below the surface of the
adjacent wing.
2) Use a hardwood or plastic fillet smoothing spatula, STD-810, or a duckbill nozzle to
remove the unwanted sealant.
3) Use a hardwood or plastic fillet smoothing spatula, STD-810, to get the air bubbles
out of the sealant.
(c) If sealant, A00767, is not easy to apply, do these steps:
1) Apply a thick layer of sealant, A00767.
2) Make sure that the sealant surface is above the contour of the wing surface.
3) Let the sealant dry.
4) Use a broad blade putty knife, STD-1053, to remove the unwanted sealant that is
above the wing surface.
SUBTASK 51-21-95-390-005
(7) Apply sealant, A00247.
NOTE: Use PR 1432 sealant only for areas with many holes.
(a) Use a usual spray equipment to apply the sealant, A00247.
NOTE: For a best result, use a spray gun with an orifice diameter of 0.085 in.
(2.159 mm) minimum.
(b) Use atomizing pressure of approximately 35 psi (241 kPa).
(c) Fully fill the rework areas with sealant, A00247, until the sealant extends higher than the
edge.
51-21-95
(d) Feather the surface.
(e) If sealant, A00247, is not easy to apply, do these steps:
1) Add 10 percent of solvent, B00094 (by volume), to the base material.
2) Mix the solution.
3) Add the accelerator.
4) Constantly mix the base and the accelerator to decrease the spray life to 1/4 hour.
5) Keep the remaining life mixture of 1 hour in a cold box at 50°F (10°C).
SUBTASK 51-21-95-390-006
(8) Let the sealant dry as follows:
Table 701/51-21-95-993-801

23.9°C

120°F
48.9°C
50% 18 Hours
SUBTASK 51-21-95-140-002
(9) Lightly rub the sealant smooth with a fine abrasive cloth, B00102 sandpaper, abrasive cloth,
G00363 (aluminum oxide grit), or a gum rubber buffing wheel.
SUBTASK 51-21-95-350-001
(10) Repair the damaged Bonderite M-CR 600 Aero coating, C50104, Bonderite M-CR 1200S Aero
coating, C50005, and Bonderite M-CR ALCRM1200 Aero coating, C50357, if it is necessary.

5. Corrosion Preventive Paint Application
(Figure 702)
A. General
(1) The corrosion control treatment contains the steps to apply the primer before you apply the
EC-843 or EC-843S Corogard or Aeroflex.
(2) On surfaces that paint stripper can remove to bare metal, apply primer, C00259 or primer,
C00319.
(3) On surfaces that paint stripper cannot remove to bare metal, apply primer, C00259.
(4) This task includes these instructions:
(a) Prepare the Surface
(b) Apply the Wash Primer
(c) Apply Zinc chromate primer MIL-P-8585
(d) Apply the BMS 10-11, Type 1 primer
(e) Apply the Corogard EC-843 or EC-843S
(f) Apply the Aeroflex G12E25 Coating
(g) Repair and Rework
(h) Upper Wing Skin Finish
51-21-95
(i) Lower Wing Skin Finish

E. Prepare the Surface
SUBTASK 51-21-95-370-002
(1) On surfaces that paint stripper can remove to bare metal, do these steps immediately before
you apply the primer:
WARNING
DO NOT GET SOLVENTS IN YOUR MOUTH, OR YOUR EYES, OR ON
YOUR SKIN. DO NOT BREATHE THE FUMES FROM SOLVENTS.
SOLVENTS ARE HAZARDOUS MATERIALS. SOLVENTS CAN BE
FLAMMABLE OR HARMFUL TO THE ENVIRONMENT . REFER TO
PRODUCT SAFETY DATA SHEETS (SDS) AND LOCAL REQUIREMENTS
FOR PROPER HANDLING PROCEDURES. IF YOU DO NOT OBEY ,
INJURY TO PERSONNEL AND DAMAGE TO EQUIPMENT CAN OCCUR
(a) Use a clean cotton wiper, G00034 that is moist with Series 84 solvent, B01004
(TASK 20-30-84-910-801).
(b) When the cotton wiper, G00034 gets dirty, change to a clean cotton wiper, G00034.
(c) Dry the surface with a clean, dry cotton wiper, G00034.
51-21-95
SUBTASK 51-21-95-390-007
(2) On surfaces with primer, C00259 and coating, C00307 primer (cannot remove with paint
stripper), prepare the surface as follows:
(a) Do this task: Clean the Surface to be Painted, TASK 51-21-21-100-801.
(b) Do this task: Prepare the Surface to be Painted, TASK 51-21-21-370-801.
F . Apply the Wash Primer (Apply to bare surfaces)
NOTE: Use primer, C00259 or primer, C00319.
SUBTASK 51-21-95-370-003
WARNING
DO NOT GET THE FINISHES, CLEANERS, THINNERS AND SOLVENTS IN
YOUR MOUTH OR EYES, OR ON YOUR SKIN OR CLOTHES. DO NOT
BREATHE THE FUMES FROM THESE MATERIALS. MAKE SURE THAT THE
AIR FLOWS FREELY THROUGH THE WORK-AREA. USE THE NECESSARY
RESPIRATORY PROTECTION. KEEP THESE MATERIALS AWAY FROM
SPARKS, FLAME AND HEAT . THESE MATERIALS ARE POISONOUS AND
FLAMMABLE AND CAN CAUSE INJURY OR DAMAGE.
(1) Mix the wash primer.
(a) Shake the base material and the acid accelerator fully before you mix the primer solution.
NOTE: The wash primer has two components, a resin base material (4 parts by volume)
and an acid accelerator (1 part by volume).
(b) Slowly add a small quantity of the acid accelerator to the base material.
NOTE: Do not add the base material to the acid accelerator.
(c) Fully mix the mixture manually.
(d) Continuously mix the mixture when you add the remaining quantity of the acid
accelerator.
(e) Identify the container with a label.
1) Write the date and hour when the mixture was mixed and when the application time
comes to an end.
NOTE: The wash primer mixture has a application time of 4 hours less than 90°F
(32.2°C) and 2 hours more than 90°F (32.2°C).
(f) Do not touch the wash primer mixture for a minimum of 15 minutes before you use it.
(g) Use equal quantity of alcohol, B00068 and alcohol, B00342 alcohol to make the wash
primer mixture thin.
NOTE: The volume of ethyl/butyl alcohol mixture must not be more than two times the
volume of the acid accelerator used.
SUBTASK 51-21-95-370-004
(2) Apply primer, C00259 or primer, C00319.
(a) Clean the spray equipment before you use it.
(b) Make sure to flush the equipment with alcohol before and after you use it.
(c) Apply one layer of primer, C00259 or primer, C00319 with the spray equipment.
(d) Make sure the continuous thickness of the layer is 0.2-0.4 mils.
1) If the layer is too thick, do these steps:
a) Immediately remove the primer with alcohol.
51-21-95
b) Apply primer, C00259 or primer, C00319 again to get the correct thickness.
(e) Let the primer, C00259 or primer, C00319 dry for not less than 45 minutes and not more
than 4 hours at 65-85°F (18.3-29.4°C).
1) If you let the primer dry for more than 4 hours, do these steps:
a) Remove the primer with alcohol, B00342 or paint stripper.
b) Apply a new layer of primer.
CAUTION
BE CAREFUL WHEN YOU USE BUTYL ALCOHOL TO CLEAN THE
PRIMER. BUTYL ALCOHOL WILL REMOVE THE PRIMER IF YOU DO
NOT USE IT CORRECTLY .
(f) Use varnish or linseed oil rags, or a lightly wet rags with alcohol, B00342 to clean the
primer.
(g) Carefully remove dust, dirt or unwanted paint from the primer.
(h) Rub a 400-grit abrasive cloth, B00102 paper (minimum) on the wash primer to get a
smooth surface.
NOTE: Be careful not to remove the wash primer.
G. Apply Zinc chromate primer MIL-P-8585 (Apply to surfaces with wash primer)
SUBTASK 51-21-95-370-005
(1) Mix the zinc chromate primer.
(a) Add thinner to the primer to make the mixture smooth and constant.
1) Slowly add 2 to 2.5 parts (by volume) of toluene to 1 part (by volume) of primer.
SUBTASK 51-21-95-370-006
(2) Apply the zinc chromate primer.
(a) Apply the zinc chromate primer with a spray gun until the thickness of the layer is 0.2-0.4
mils.
(b) Let the zinc chromate primer dry at 65-85°F (18.3-29.4°C) for not less than 45 minutes
and not more than 72 hours.
1) If after 72 hours and you do not apply the Corogard or Aeroflex, do these steps:
a) Remove the primer.
b) Apply a new layer of the wash primer and the zinc chromate primer to the
surface.
(c) Rub the dry zinc chromate primer lightly with a 400 grit (minimum) abrasive cloth, B00102
paper to get a smooth surface.
(d) Clean the surface with a clean cotton wiper, G00034 that is moist with BMS 3-2 solvent
and solvent, B00083.
H. Apply the BMS 10-11, Type 1 primer (Apply to surfaces with epoxy primer and did not remove
with paint stripper).
SUBTASK 51-21-95-370-007
(1) Apply primer, C00259.
(a) Do this task: Decorative Exterior Paint System Repair, TASK 51-21-99-300-802.
SUBTASK 51-21-95-370-008
(2) Let the primer, C00259 dry before you apply Corogard or Aeroflex.
51-21-95
I. Apply the Corogard EC-843 or EC-843S (Optional)
SUBTASK 51-21-95-370-009
WARNING
IF YOU DO NOT USE IT IMMEDIATELY , MAKE SURE THAT YOU KEEP THE
MIXTURE OF COROGARD AND ALUMINUM POWDER IN THE CORRECT
CONTAINER. WHEN YOU MIX THE COROGARD WITH THE ALUMINUM
POWDER, IT RELEASES FLAMMABLE HYDROGEN GAS. KEEP THE
MIXTURE IN AN OPEN CONTAINER OR A CONTAINER WITH OPENINGS ON
THE COVER. IF YOU DO NOT DO THIS, IT CAN CAUSE INJURY TO
PERSONS AND DAMAGE TO EQUIPMENT .
(1) Prepare the Corogard EC-843 and EC-843-S.
NOTE: Corogard EC-843 and EC-843-S have two components, the resin component and the
aluminum powder component (12.5 ounces (0.37 liter) aluminum powder EC-1101 for
each gallon (3.8 liters) of resin EC-843, or 9.0 ounces (0.27 liter) aluminum powder
EC-1101 for each gallon (3.8 liters) of resin EC-843-S).
(a) Put the resin component through a 60-80 mesh metal sieve into a pressure pot or a
mixer.
(b) Put the aluminum powder component in a different container from the resin component.
(c) Slowly add the methyl isobutyl ketone or the butyl acetate to make the mixture smooth
and constant.
(d) Put the aluminum powder through the 60-80 mesh metal sieve into the pressure pot or
mixer.
(e) Fully mix the mixture manually or with a power mixer.
(f) Make sure the mixture is smooth and constant.
(g) Use one of these materials to make the mixture thin for airspray:
NOTE: The thinner must not be more than 20 percent (by volume) of the base material.
1) Methyl isobutyl ketone
2) Butyl acetate
3) Five parts methyl isobutyl ketone (by volume) to three parts butyl acetate (by
volume)
4) One part methyl isobutyl ketone (by volume) to one part butyl acetate (by volume).
(h) The application time of this material is 16 hours with no temperature limit.
SUBTASK 51-21-95-370-010
(2) Apply the Corogard EC-843 or EC-843S.
(a) Apply one layer of the EC-843 or EC-843S Corogard mixture with a spray gun.
1) Frequently shake the mixture in the pot.
(b) Between the first and subsequent layers, stop for a minimum of 30 minutes before you
apply a new layer.
NOTE: On some surfaces, it is not necessary to let the mixture dry before you can apply
a second layer. For example, horizontal surfaces where there is no runs or sags.
(c) Apply sufficient layers to get a total dry thickness of 2-3 mils.
NOTE: To decrease the effect of bronzing, the Corogard EC-843 and EC-843-S material
has more pigments than usually necessary. The aluminum pigments can rub off
but the quality of the Corogard EC-843 and EC-843-S does not decrease.
51-21-95
(d) Dry the mixture for a minimum of 24 hours at 65 to 85°F (18.3-29.4°C).
NOTE: The dry time can decrease if you apply heat on the surface at 125 (± 5)°F (51.7 ±
2.8°C) for 2 hours.
J. Apply the Aeroflex G12E25 Coating (Preferred)
SUBTASK 51-21-95-370-011
(1) Prepare the aluminized coating Aeroflex G12E25.
(a) Fully mix the Aeroflex G12E25 material for 10-15 minutes manually or use a power mixer.
NOTE: There must be no air bubbles while you mix the Aeroflex.
(b) Make sure that the aluminum pigment is constant in the mixture.
(c) Use the Aeroflex thinner to make the mixture thin.
NOTE: The thinner must not be more than 20 percent (by volume) of the base material.
(d) The application time of this mixture is 4 hours with no temperature limit.
SUBTASK 51-21-95-370-012
(2) Apply the Aeroflex G12E25.
(a) Apply primer, C00259 and coating, C00307 primer on the surface.
(b) Let the primer dry before you apply the mixture.
(c) Put the Aeroflex mixture through a clean cloth or sieve.
(d) Apply the Corogard mixture with a spray gun.
(e) Add sufficient layers to get a total dry thickness of 2-3 mils (0.002 to 0.003 inch)
(0.051-0.076 mm).
NOTE: If the mixture becomes dry when you use Aeroflex G12E25, decrease the
atomizing air pressure . You can also add the C25/90S thinner, C00662 to make
the mixture thin as an alternative.
(f) Dry the mixture at 65­85°F (18.3­29.4°C) for a minimum of 4 hours before you move the
airplane to an open area.
(g) Dry the mixture for 24 hours before flight.
NOTE: The dry time can decrease if you apply heat on the surface at 125 ± 5°F (51.7 ±
2.8°C) for 2 hours.
K. Repair and Rework
SUBTASK 51-21-95-350-002
(1) Clean areas with small damages such as scratches and gouges.
(a) Rub the areas smooth with 280 grit (minimum) abrasive paper.
(b) Clean the areas with clean rags moist with toluene or xylene.
SUBTASK 51-21-95-370-013
(2) Apply the primer and Aeroflex or Corogard.
(a) Feather all the edges.
(b) Use a brush for small damages.
SUBTASK 51-21-95-350-003
(3) Clean areas with EC-843 or EC-843S Corogard or Aeroflex applied.
(a) Fully clean the areas with toluene or xylene.
(b) Rub the areas smooth with 280 grit (minimum) abrasive paper.
51-21-95
(c) Clean the areas again with rags that are moist with toluene or xylene.
(d) Rub the surface dry with a clean cloth.
SUBTASK 51-21-95-220-001
(4) Make sure the repair and overlap thickness is not more than 5.0 mils.
NOTE: The color of the new EC-843 and EC-843S Corogard coating will not be the same as
the adjacent surface. The color will become the same in approximately 60 days. Permit
the coating to dry a minimum of 4 hours at 65-85°F (18.3-29.4°C).
L. Upper Wing Skin Finish
SUBTASK 51-21-95-370-023
(1) Park the airplane away from the sun and electrically ground AIRPLANE GROUNDING -
MAINTENANCE PRACTICES, PAGEBLOCK 20-40-11/201.
SUBTASK 51-21-95-370-024
WARNING
DO NOT GET CLEANING SOLVENT IN YOUR MOUTH OR EYES OR ON
YOUR SKIN. DO NOT BREATHE THE FUMES FROM THE CLEANING
SOLVENT . PUT ON A PROTECTIVE SPLASH GOGGLE AND GLOVES WHEN
YOU USE THE CLEANING SOLVENT . KEEP THE CLEANING SOLVENT AWAY
FROM SPARKS, FLAME AND HEAT . THE CLEANING SOLVENT IS
POISONOUS AND FLAMMABLE AND CAN CAUSE INJURY TO PERSONS OR
DAMAGE TO EQUIPMENT .
(2) Before you apply chemical conversion coating, clean the surface correctly to get a satisfactory
Bonderite M-CR 600 Aero coating, C50104, Bonderite M-CR 1200S Aero coating, C50005 and
Bonderite M-CR ALCRM1200 Aero coating, C50357 treatment. Do the steps that follow:
NOTE: It is not necessary to clean all areas for chemical conversion coating treatment. The
chemical conversion coating is for shaved rivet heads and exposed aluminum.
(a) Use one part (by volume) solvent alkaline cleaner to two parts water and five to six parts
solvent, B00083.
(b) Apply the cleaner solution and let it soak for a minimum of 10 minutes.
NOTE: The solution must be wet on the skins. Apply the cleaner again if it is necessary.
(c) Clean the skins with a soft bristle brush, STD-123.
1) Make sure to clean the countersunk areas and around the rivet heads.
(d) Flush the surface with water (100°F (37.8°C)) at high pressure.
(e) Examine the surface for water breaks.
NOTE: A surface with no water breaks has a continuous layer of water for a minimum of
30 seconds.
(f) Clean the skin again if there are water breaks.
(g) Apply Scotch Flatback Masking T ape 250, G00270 to all adjacent areas.
NOTE: The surface must be free from dirt, dust, grease, oil or moisture before you apply
the tape.
SUBTASK 51-21-95-370-025
(3) Prepare Coating:
NOTE: In areas where the chemical conversion coating may come in contact with BMS 10-20
primer, only Bonderite M-CR 600 Aero coating, C00862 should be used.
NOTE: Keep the room temperature above 65°F (18.3°C).
51-21-95
WARNING
DO NOT GET CHEMICAL CONVERSION COATING SOLUTION IN YOUR
MOUTH, IN YOUR EYES, ON YOUR SKIN, OR ON YOUR CLOTHES. DO
NOT BREATHE THE FUMES FROM THIS MATERIAL. AIR MUST FLOW
FREELY THROUGH THE WORK AREA. USE THE NECESSARY
RESPIRATORY PROTECTION. KEEP THIS MATERIAL AWAY FROM
SPARKS, FLAME, AND HEAT . THIS MATERIAL IS POISONOUS AND
FLAMMABLE AND CAN CAUSE INJURY TO PERSONS OR DAMAGE TO
EQUIPMENT .
(a) Put Bonderite M-CR 600 Aero coating, C00862, Bonderite M-CR ALCRM1200 Aero
coating, C50357 or Bonderite M-CR 1200S Aero coating, C50005 on paper and use a
roller to make the powder smooth.
(b) Mix the solution in a stainless steel container (1 gal) (4 Liter), STD-1118 or acid resistant
container, STD-1052.
NOTE: Do not use lead or glass.
(c) For Bonderite M-CR 600 Aero coating, C00862, add 3 oz (85 g) of powder for each 1 gal
(3.8 l) of final solution.
1) Stir until the powder is dissolved.
2) Add up to 2% by volume of Bonderite M-AD 22 Toner Aero surface treatment,
C50358 or ammonium hydroxide as required to adjust pH to between 1.5 and 2.0.
(d) For Bonderite M-CR ALCRM1200 Aero coating, C50357: Add 3 oz (85 g) of powder to
water for each gallon (3.8 liters) of final solution.
1) Stir until the powder is dissolved.
2) Keep the pH in the range of 1.50 - 1.90.
3) Adjust the pH down with nitric acid or ammonium hydroxide.
(e) For Bonderite M-CR 1200S Aero coating, C50005: Add 1.9 oz (53.9 g) – 2.1 oz (59.5 g)
of powder to water for each gallon of final solution.
(f) Fully mix the solution until the powder is dissolved. Ignore small quantities of material
which do not dissolve.
(g) Allow to stand for 1 hour before use.
NOTE: Prepare the solution in small quantities. Discard the solution after you use it if a
brush, sponge, or wiper was put into it or if contamination gets into the solution.
Do not add wetting agents or other materials to this solution.
SUBTASK 51-21-95-370-026
(4) Apply Bonderite M-CR 600 Aero coating, C00862, Bonderite M-CR ALCRM1200 Aero coating,
C50357 or Bonderite M-CR 1200S Aero coating, C50005.
NOTE: In areas where the chemical conversion coating may come in contact with BMS 10-20
primer, only Bonderite M-CR 600 Aero coating, C00862 should be used.
51-21-95
WARNING
DO NOT GET CHEMICAL CONVERSION COATING SOLUTION IN YOUR
MOUTH, IN YOUR EYES, ON YOUR SKIN, OR ON YOUR CLOTHES. DO
NOT BREATHE THE FUMES FROM THIS MATERIAL. AIR MUST FLOW
FREELY THROUGH THE WORK AREA. USE THE NECESSARY
RESPIRATORY PROTECTION. KEEP THIS MATERIAL AWAY FROM
SPARKS, FLAME, AND HEAT . THIS MATERIAL IS POISONOUS AND

(a) Apply Bonderite M-CR 600 Aero coating, C00862, Bonderite M-CR ALCRM1200 Aero

FREELY THROUGH THE WORK AREA. USE THE NECESSARY

(a) Put Bonderite M-CR 600 Aero coating, C00862, Bonderite M-CR ALCRM1200 Aero

NOTE: Do not use lead or glass.

(3.8 l) of final solution.
1) Stir until the powder is dissolved.

C50358 or ammonium hydroxide as required to adjust pH to between 1.5 and 2.0.
(d) For Bonderite M-CR ALCRM1200 Aero coating, C50357: Add 3 oz (85 g) of powder to
water for each gallon (3.8 liters) of final solution.
1) Stir until the powder is dissolved.
2) Keep the pH in the range of 1.50 - 1.90.
3) Adjust the pH down with nitric acid or ammonium hydroxide.
(e) For Bonderite M-CR 1200S Aero coating, C50005: Add 1.9 oz (53.9 g) – 2.1 oz (59.5 g)
of powder to water for each gallon of final solution.
(f) Fully mix the solution until the powder is dissolved. Ignore small quantities of material
which do not dissolve.
(g) Allow to stand for 1 hour before use.
NOTE: Prepare the solution in small quantities. Discard the solution after you use it if a
brush, sponge, or wiper was put into it or if contamination gets into the solution.
Do not add wetting agents or other materials to this solution.

B. Prepare the Surface
SUBTASK 51-21-96-150-001
CAUTION
DO NOT SAND BLAST TITANIUM DUCTS. IF YOU SAND BLAST THE
TITANIUM DUCTING, YOU CAN CAUSE PERMANENT DAMAGE.
(1) Use sand blast equipment to remove contamination and high temperature coating from parts
that are on the airplane.
(a) Make sure that no sand particles get on the adjacent surfaces.
SUBTASK 51-21-96-950-001
(2) Remove the titanium parts from the airplane if you use paint strippers to remove the high
temperature coating.
SUBTASK 51-21-96-960-001
(3) Replace all parts that have metal corrosion.
SUBTASK 51-21-96-150-002
(4) Remove the hydraulic fluid contamination:
NOTE: A light glossy brown layer, a black layer that is not shiny, or a bare surface, are signs of
hydraulic fluid (Skydrol) contamination. A flat, black inorganic finish supplies protection
for titanium parts in areas with possible contamination to hydraulic fluid. Make sure you
know the difference between the black inorganic finish and the hydraulic fluid
contamination.
(a) Clean the parts with a cotton wiper, G00034 or an aluminum wool that is moist with
Oakite 61.
NOTE: The aluminum wool must be sufficiently rough to get a surface that is not shiny.
(b) Flush the parts with water.
(c) Dry the parts with a clean dry cotton wiper, G00034.
(d) Clean the parts again until you get a surface with no water breaks.
51-21-96
SUBTASK 51-21-96-110-001
(5) Remove all the remaining unwanted material:
(a) Fully flush the area with water.
NOTE: If it is possible, use high pressure water to flush the area.
(b) Clean the area with solvent, B00722.
(c) Flush the area with water until you get a surface with no water breaks.
(d) Dry the surface with a clean dry cotton wiper, G00034.
SUBTASK 51-21-96-150-003
(6) Remove the high temperature coating:
WARNING
DO NOT TOUCH OR HOLD TURCO 4338 WITHOUT THE NECESSARY
PROTECTION. WEAR GOGGLES AND HEAVY RUBBER GLOVES. KEEP
THE TURCO 4338 IN STEEL CONTAINERS. DO NOT MIX TURCO 4338
POWDER WITH ORGANIC SOLVENTS SUCH AS ACETONE OR METHYL
ETHYL KETONE. A MIXTURE OF TURCO 4338 WITH THE ORGANIC
SOLVENTS CAN CAUSE AN EXPLOSION AND INJURY TO PERSONS OR
DAMAGE TO EQUIPMENT CAN OCCUR.
(a) Refer to the manufacturer's instructions to prepare the Turco 4338 stripper, E00003
solution.
(b) Put the parts in the stripper solution for 30 to 40 minutes.
(c) Remove the parts from the stripper solution.
(d) Fully flush the parts with water.
(e) Use an air water spray to remove all unwanted materials.
(f) Clean the parts again until you remove the black coating.

SUBTASK 51-21-96-370-001
CAUTION

COATING.
CAUTION

HIGH TEMPERATURE COATING, THE COATING WILL HAVE UNWANTED AIR
BUBBLES.

SUBTASK 51-21-97-110-001
WARNING
DO NOT GET SOLVENTS IN YOUR MOUTH, IN YOUR EYES, OR ON YOUR
SKIN. DO NOT BREATHE THE FUMES FROM SOLVENTS. PUT ON
PROTECTIVE SPLASH GOGGLES AND GLOVES WHEN YOU USE
SOLVENTS. KEEP SOLVENTS AWAY FROM SPARKS, FLAME, AND
TEMPERATURES ABOVE THE FLASH POINT . SOLVENTS ARE A
POISONOUS AND FLAMMABLE MATERIAL WHICH CAN CAUSE INJURY
PERSONS AND DAMAGE TO EQUIPMENT .
(1) Do these steps to prepare the surfaces that have a coat of primer less than 48 hours old:
(a) Clean the surface with clean cotton wiper, G00034, moist with solvent, B00083, or
solvent, B00094.
(b) If the surface has particle contamination only, clean with tack rag, G50123.
SUBTASK 51-21-97-100-001
(2) Do these steps to prepare the surfaces that have a coat of primer more than 48 hours old:
(a) Clean the surface with clean cotton wiper, G00034, moist with solvent, B00151.
(b) Rub the surface lightly with abrasive cloth, B00102, 100-grit minimum.
(c) Clean the surface with clean cotton wiper, G00034, moist with solvent, B00083, or
solvent, B00094, to remove sanding particles.
(d) Clean the surface with tack rag, G50123, to remove remaining particles.
SUBTASK 51-21-97-370-008
(3) If the primer is removed and aluminum shows, do this task: Decorative Exterior Paint System
Repair, TASK 51-21-99-300-802.
SUBTASK 51-21-97-950-001
(4) Apply Scotch Flatback Masking T ape 250, G00270, on the adjacent areas.
E. Mix the Materials (Spray Application)
SUBTASK 51-21-97-390-002
(1) Make sure that the container used for mixing is clean and solvent-resistant.
SUBTASK 51-21-97-370-016
(2) Agitate sealant, A50193, base material prior to thinning or catalyzing.
SUBTASK 51-21-97-370-017
(3) The mix ratio (by volume) is 100/6.9.
(a) The maximum allowable deviation from the ratio is +/- 5 percent.
SUBTASK 51-21-97-370-019
(4) Slowly add the catalyst.
(a) Agitate the base material while adding the catalyst.
SUBTASK 51-21-97-370-018
(5) If the material needs to be thinned, slowly add the thinner.
(a) Agitate the mixture while adding the thinner.
(b) Thin the spray by adding 20 to 30 percent Series 80 solvent, B01000.
51-21-97
SUBTASK 51-21-97-370-020
(6) Immediately apply the mixture using the High Volume Low Pressure (HVLP) spray gun.
NOTE: Catalyzed Type 46 mixture has a application time of 1.5 hours at 75°F (24°C). If the
material has exceeded this limit, discard the mixture.
(a) Make sure that the material has the following properties:
1) Homogenous
2) Free of skins
3) Free of lumps
4) Free of gelled or coarse particles.
(b) Allow it to dry for a minimum of 2 hours before overcoating.
(c) Make sure that the dry film thickness is 2 mils (0.051 mm) to 3 mils (0.076 mm) thick.
F . Apply the Non-Skid Finish by Spray Application
SUBTASK 51-21-97-370-009
(1) Prepare the non-skid finish.
(a) Mix one of the following coatings according to the manufacturers instructions:
NOTE: The application time for the mixture is 3.5 hours for the white mixture and 2.5
hours for the black mixture.
1) CA8022/B7022 coating, C50018
2) CA8012/B701 coating, C50019
3) CA8020/B702 coating or ECL-SG coating, C50134
4) CA8010/B701 coating or ECL-F-Series coating, C50168.
(b) While continuing mixing the coating, add 0.30 lb (0.14 kg) of Navajo Brand compound,
G50124, to one gallon (3.8 liters) of mixed coating.
NOTE: Addition of Navajo Brand compound, G50124, to the black coating is optional
when the black coating is applied directly over the white coating containing
pumice.
(c) Hand swirl mixture just before application.
1) Make sure that the mixture is applied evenly.
SUBTASK 51-21-97-370-010
(2) Apply the pumice and enamel mixture with a paint spray gun with interchangeable nozzles,
STD-1133, as follows:
(a) Apply one or more layers of the nonskid finish to make sure that the dry thickness is
between 0.005 in. (0.127 mm) - 0.007 in. (0.178 mm) (6 oz (0.170 kg) - 8 oz (0.227 kg))
for 1 yd
2 (8361 cm 2).
NOTE: The dry thickness is the distance from the primer to the external surface.
1) Let each layer of coating dry for 15 minutes (minimum) at 50°F (10°C) or 10 minutes
at 70°F (21°C) or more.
CAUTION
PULL OFF THE MASKING TAPE ADJACENT TO THE OVERWING
EMERGENCY EXIT WALKWAY COATING EDGE BEFORE THE COATING
IS FULLY DRY . THIS PREVENTS LAYERS FROM COMING OFF .
(b) Carefully remove the masking tape before the layers are fully dry.
51-21-97
1) Let the coating air dry for a minimum of 2 hours before removing the masking tape.
(c) Air dry the coating for 4 hours (minimum) of 90°F (32°C) before walking on the surface.
(d) Air dry the coating for 24 hours at 70°F (21°C) before service.
G. Mix the Materials (Roll-on Application)
SUBTASK 51-21-97-370-012
(1) Mix the walkway coating, C50313, as follows:
NOTE: A walkway coating, C50313, kit consists of:
• Base component: CA 9032/B7022 (White), CA 9032/B701 (Black), or CA
9032/B3494 (Yellow), Quantity 1qt (32 fl-oz (946 ml)).
• Curing solution component: CA 9032B, Quantity 1qt (32 fl-oz (946 ml)).
• Reactive thinner component: CA 9032C, Quantity 0.5pt (8 fl-oz (237 ml)).
• Pumice component (for white color only): 920K059, Quantity 0.18 lb (81.65 g).
NOTE: If CA 9032/B7022 (White) is not available, you can use coating, C50165.
If CA 9032/B701 (Black) is not available, you can use coating, C50168.
If CA9032/B3494 (Yellow) is not available, you can use coating, C50237.
NOTE: If the components are sourced individually, use the following mixture ratio (by volume):
• Base component - 4 parts
• Activator Component - 4 parts
• Reactivator/thinner component - 1 part
• Pumice component (for white color only) - 1.235 oz (35 g) for each quart (32 fl-oz
(946 ml)) of mixed paint.
(a) Make sure that the container is clean and solvent resistant.
(b) Put all of the fully mixed base component into a clean and solvent resistant container.
(c) While continuously mixing the base component, slowly add all the curing solution
component.
(d) While continuously mixing the base and curing solution components, slowly add all the
reactive thinner component.
(e) While continuously mixing the base, curing solution and reactive thinner components,
add all of the pumice.
(f) Apply walkway coating, C50313, in 4 hours or less.
H. Apply the Non-Skid Finish by Roll-on Application
NOTE: Roll-on application is not applicable for use with CA8022/B7022 coating, C50018, or
CA8012/B701 coating, C50019.
SUBTASK 51-21-97-370-013
(1) Prepare the non-skid finish.
(a) Mix walkway coating, C50313, according to the manufacturers instructions.
NOTE: The application time for the mixture is 4 hours.
SUBTASK 51-21-97-370-014
(2) Prepare the roller, as follows:
(a) Use the paint roller, STD-14527.
51-21-97
(b) Remove lint from the roller with 2 inch wide masking tape, G50314, or an equivalent, as
follows:
1) Attach the end of the tape to a counter top.
2) Remove approximately 4 ft (1.22 m) of the tape from the roll.
3) With the roller installed on the handle, remove lint from the roller by rolling the nap
across the adhesive side of the tape making sure to move the roller side to side so
that the all of the roller surface has been treated.
SUBTASK 51-21-97-370-015
(3) Apply walkway coating, C50313, with the paint roller, STD-14527, as follows:
(a) Put the mixed white walkway coating, C50313, into a paint roller pan.
(b) Soak the roller with the coating and roll across the roller pan ribs to remove unwanted
coating.
(c) Apply approximately 10 ft
2 (0.93 m 2) of the coating before returning to the roller pan for
additional material.
1) Use a paint stick to shake the paint in the roller pan to keep the pumice fully
dispersed.
(d) Apply one or more layers of the nonskid finish making sure that the dry thickness is
between 5 mils (0.127 mm) to 7 mils (0.1778 mm).
NOTE: The dry thickness is the distance from the primer to the external surface.
1) Let each layer of coating dry for 10 minutes (minimum) at 50°F (10°C) or 5 minutes
(minimum) at 70°F (21°C).
(e) Remove the masking tape adjacent to the walkway before the coating becomes dry.
NOTE: If the coating is fully dry, some coating will come off with the masking tape.
1) Let the coating air dry at a minimum of 70°F (21°C) for 2 hours (minimum) before
removing the masking tape.
(f) Air dry the coating at a minimum of 70°F (21°C) for 2 hours (minimum) before walking on
the surface.
(g) Air dry the coating at a minimum of 70°F (21°C) for 2 hours (minimum) before service.

(c) Smooth and feather the paint edges with 100-grit or finer abrasive cloth, B00102.

51-21-97

E. Apply the Non-Skid Finish (Recommended Procedure)
SUBTASK 51-21-97-370-002
(1) Prepare the non-skid finish.
(a) Mix the coating, C00260 with the catalyst.
NOTE: Follow the manufacturers instructions to mix the enamel.
1) Add the thinner if it is necessary to the get the proper paint viscosity
(b) Add one pound (0.45 kilogram) of No. 1-1/2 grit pumice abrasive, B00166 to one gallon
(3.8 liters) of mixed, ready-to-spray coating, C00260 enamel.
51-21-97
1) Let the mixture stay for one hour from the time you added the catalyst.
(c) Before and during the process, continue to shake the mixture of pumice and enamel.
1) This is to make sure it stays mixed while it is being applied to the surface.
SUBTASK 51-21-97-370-003
(2) Apply the pumice and enamel mixture with a spray paint spray gun with interchangeable
nozzles, STD-1133 as follows:
(a) Apply a cross coat of one layer of the pumice and enamel mixture.
NOTE: Make sure the surface of the layer is smooth.
1) Let the enamel/pumice dry for 60 minutes (minimum) at 70°F or more.
(b) Apply 1-1/2 cross coats (i.e. three wet passes) of coating, C00260 epoxy enamel (without
pumice) with the applicable spray equipment.
1) Let the layers of enamel dry for 24 hours (minimum) at 70°F before you move or
touch the surface.
2) The layers of enamel must dry for 7 days (minimum) before you walk on the
surface.
NOTE: You can make the drying of the last layer shorter when you increase the
temperature. This is after it has dried for a minimum of one hour at 70°F
(21°C).
(c) Carefully remove the masking tape before the layers are fully dry.
F . Apply the non-skid finish (Optional Procedure)
SUBTASK 51-21-97-370-004

(a) Clean and reactivate the primer.
SUBTASK 51-21-97-370-005
(2) Mix the coating, C00260 with the catalyst.
NOTE: Follow the manufacturers instructions to mix the enamel.
(a) Add the thinner if it is necessary to the get the correct paint spray viscosity.
(b) Let the mixed enamel stay for one hour from the time you added the catalyst.
(c) Apply the one wet layer of coating, C00260 with a spray gun paint spray gun with
interchangeable nozzles, STD-1133.
(d) Immediately apply the dry No.1-1/2 pumice abrasive, B00166 to a heavy and equal layer.
1) You can apply the dry pumice grit pumice abrasive, B00166 with a Binks No. 171
spray gun with a No. 58-11 tip, or its equivalent.
(e) Remove the loose pumice.
1) Tilt the panel, if it is possible, to let the loose pumice fall off.
2) Use compressed air with 10 psi or less pressure.
(f) Apply 2 wet layers of coating, C00260 epoxy enamel on the pumice grit with a spray gun
paint spray gun with interchangeable nozzles, STD-1133.
1) Let the layers of the enamel dry for 24 hours (minimum) at 70°F (21°C) before you
move or touch the surface.
51-21-97
2) The layers of enamel must dry for 7 days (minimum) at 70°F (21°C) before you walk
on the surface.
NOTE: You can make the drying of the last layer shorter when you increase the
temperature. This is after it has dried for a minimum of one hour at 70°F
(21°C).

2. Heat, Weather and Oil Resistant Inorganic Protective Coating Application
A. Consumable Materials

B. Prepare the Parts
SUBTASK 51-21-98-150-001
WARNING
DO NOT GET THE FINISHES, CLEANERS, THINNERS AND SOLVENTS IN
YOUR MOUTH OR EYES, OR ON YOUR SKIN OR CLOTHES. DO NOT
BREATHE THE FUMES FROM THESE MATERIALS. MAKE SURE THAT THE
AIR FLOWS FREELY THROUGH THE WORK-AREA. USE THE NECESSARY
RESPIRATORY PROTECTION. KEEP THESE MATERIALS AWAY FROM
SPARKS, FLAME AND HEAT . THESE MATERIALS ARE POISONOUS AND
FLAMMABLE AND CAN CAUSE INJURY OR DAMAGE.
(1) Remove the coating.
(a) Soak the parts fully in Bonderite C-AK 4181 Aero cleaner, B00211 (1 lb (0.5 kg) - 2 lb
(0.9 kg) for each 1 gal (3.8 l) of the solution), (or Bonderite C-AK 4181L Aero cleaner,
B50407 as alternative), at 190°F (87.8°C) to 210°F (98.9°C) until there is no more coating
on the parts.
(b) Flush the parts with water.
(c) Put the parts in an oven at 200 ±25°F (93.3 ±13.9°C) to dry.
SUBTASK 51-21-98-110-001
(2) Clean the parts with solvent.
(a) For parts made from titanium or its alloys, clean with nonchlorinated solvent.
NOTE: Do not use chlorinated solvent on titanium.
(b) For parts made from ferrous alloys, clean with solvent or clean by vapor degreasing.
SUBTASK 51-21-98-140-001
(3) Remove grit with a clean 100 to 250 grit abrasive cloth, B00102, paper.
51-21-98
SUBTASK 51-21-98-160-001
(4) Clean the parts with compressed air to remove dirt and other contamination.
NOTE: After you clean the parts, do not touch the parts with your bare hands. Use clean,
white cotton gloves when you touch the parts.
SUBTASK 51-21-98-950-001
(5) Put a cover on the parts until you apply the coating.
(a) Make sure that you apply the coating in 4 hours.
C. Heat, Weather and Oil Resistant Inorganic Protective Coating Application
SUBTASK 51-21-98-370-001
(1) Apply the inorganic protective coating.
(a) Fully mix the material before you use it.
(b) Use a spray equipment or an electrostatic spray equipment to apply coating, C00251.
(c) Apply coating, C00251, until you get a constant, smooth, gray layer.
(d) Let the parts dry for 30 minutes at ambient temperature.
(e) Put covers on the adjacent areas.
(f) Apply one layer of coating, C00251, catalyst to the surface.
NOTE: Do not mix the coating with the catalyst.
(g) Let the surface dry for 16 hours, or 4 hours at 300 ±50°F (148.9 ±27.8°C).
(h) Make sure that you can see no bubbles or runs on the dry surface.
(i) Make sure that the coating is 0.002 in. (0.051 mm) to 0.003 in. (0.076 mm) thick.
SUBTASK 51-21-98-140-002
(2) Rub the surface with a rigid bristle brush or a soft cloth buffing wheel until you get a smooth,
shiny finish.

51-21-99-300-801Decorative Exterior Paint System ApplicationPDF p. 413
Open original AMM PDF - page 413 ↗ 
2. Decorative Exterior Paint System Application
A. General
(1) Do not get paint or primer on the surfaces of these items:
(a) Decoupled static dischargers or retainers on the wing and empennage.
(b) Rain repellent nozzles that are forward of the windshields.
(c) Rubber mohair seals, rub strips made of stainless steel, anodized handles and door
handle pans, or silicone rubber sealant.
(d) Pressure relief valves or outflow valves.
(e) Circular bands, 1 in. (25.4 mm) in width, around or on the pressure relief valve doors, or
outflow valves.
(f) Door operating mechanisms, hinge assemblies, and equivalent surfaces.
(g) Flight deck and main cabin windows.
(h) Pitot static probes, static ports, Angle of Attack (AOA) vanes, and Total Air T emperature
(TAT) probe.
(i) Exterior Light Lens Covers.
(2) Do not allow solvents, cleaners, or overspray to contact polycarbophil plastic parts.
(3) Paint should meet the requirements and be in the qualified products list (QPL) of BMS10-72 or
AMS3095. In addition to this procedure, SOPM 20-30-03 provides procedures for decorative
exterior paint system application.
B. References
Reference Title

SUBTASK 51-21-99-370-057
(5) If the airplane you are painting had paint peeling from the rivet heads, do these steps to
improve the paint adhesion:
NOTE: This condition is known as “rivet rash”.
(a) Use Cee-Bee Brightener B-55, B50137, to chemically etch the clad aluminum skin.
1) Dilute the Cee-Bee Brightener B-55, B50137, by mixing it with water.
a) Make the solution 10% Cee-Bee Brightener B-55, B50137 and 90% water, by
volume.
2) Apply the solution to the surface.
NOTE: If you are applying the solution to the entire airplane, start at one end and
work towards the other.
a) Allow the solution to remain on the surface from 20-30 minutes.
NOTE: Do not allow the solution to remain on any part of the surface for more
than 30 minutes.
3) Rinse the surface with large amounts of water to remove Cee-Bee Brightener B-55,
B50137.
(b) Apply a chemical conversion coating to the rivet rows (PAGEBLOCK 51-21-41/701).

3. Decorative Exterior Paint System Repair
A. References
Reference Title
20-30-85-910-801 Final Cleaning of All Organic Coatings Prior to Painting (Series
85) (P/B 201)
51-21-11-150-801 Paint Stripping (P/B 701)
B. Consumable Materials
Reference Description Specification
B00102 Abrasive - Aluminum Oxide Coated Cloth
B01005 Solvent - Final Cleaning Of All Organic
Coatings Prior To Painting (AMM 20-30-85) -
Series 85
51-21-99
(Continued)
Reference Description Specification
G00034 Cotton Wiper - Process Cleaning Absorbent
Wiper (Cheesecloth, Gauze)
AMS3819 Class 1
Grade A or B Form 1
(Supersede BMS15-5
CL A)
C. Procedure
SUBTASK 51-21-99-370-006
(1) Repair areas with minor damage (no bare metal) as follows:
(a) Rub the surface smooth with a 240 grit (minimum) wet or dry abrasive cloth, B00102
paper.
(b) Remove the remaining particles with a clean cotton wiper, G00034 that is moist with
Series 85 solvent, B01005, do this task: Final Cleaning of All Organic Coatings Prior to
Painting (Series 85), TASK 20-30-85-910-801.
(c) Dry the surface with a clean cotton wiper, G00034.
(d) Apply the applicable primer and topcoats again, if necessary, as follows:
1) Do this task: Decorative Exterior Paint System Application, TASK 51-21-99-300-801.
(e) Make sure you get a smooth continuous surface.
SUBTASK 51-21-99-370-007
(2) Repair areas with large damage (bare metal) as follows:
CAUTION
DO NOT LET THE PAINT STRIPPER TOUCH FIBERGLASS OR SEAMS
OF ADHESIVE BONDED PARTS. THE PAINT STRIPPER CAN CAUSE
DAMAGE TO FIBERGLASS OR SEAMS OF ADHESIVE BONDED PARTS.
(a) Rub the surface smooth with abrasive cloth, B00102, or, do this task: Paint Stripping,
TASK 51-21-11-150-801.
(b) Feather the edges of the adjacent area with a 240 grit (minimum) abrasive cloth, B00102
paper that is moist with water.
(c) Remove the remaining particles with a clean cotton wiper, G00034 that is moist with
Series 85 solvent, B01005 (TASK 20-30-85-910-801).
(d) Dry the surface with a clean cotton wiper, G00034.
(e) Apply the applicable primer and topcoats as follows:
1) Do this task: Decorative Exterior Paint System Application, TASK 51-21-99-300-801.
(f) Feather all the edges to get a smooth continuous finish.
SUBTASK 51-21-99-370-008
(3) Repair areas with defective paint layers as follows:
(a) Rub the surface with a 240 grit (minimum) wet or dry abrasive cloth, B00102 paper to
remove defects.
(b) Feather the edges into the adjacent areas.
(c) Remove the remaining particles with a clean cotton wiper, G00034 that is moist with
Series 85 solvent, B01005 (TASK 20-30-85-910-801).
(d) Dry the surface with a clean cotton wiper, G00034.
(e) Apply the applicable topcoats per manufacturer's specifications and Table 701.
51-21-99
(f) Feather the edges to get a smooth continuous finish.
51-21-99-370-801Basecoat/Clearcoat Paint System - RepairPDF p. 418

4. Basecoat/Clearcoat Paint System - Repair
A. General
(1) This task has the procedures that follow:
(a) Prepare for the Repair of the Basecoat/Clearcoat Paint System
(b) Repair the Aerodur 3001 Basecoat Surface with Damage or Defects
(c) Repair the Aerodur 3002 Clearcoat Surface with Damage or Defects
(d) Prepare the CRES and Titanium Surfaces for Overcoating
(e) Prepare the Aluminum Surfaces for Overcoating
(f) Apply the Chemical Conversion Coating
(g) Primer BMS10-72, Application
(h) Apply Computer Generated Premasks
(i) Apply Aerodur 3001 Basecoat
(j) Apply the Nonmetallic Special Visual Effects Coating
(k) Apply Stencils
(l) Apply BMS10-26 Markings
(m) Apply BMS10-127, Type I, Grade F
(n) Apply Aerodur 3002 Clearcoat
(o) Final Appearance Requirements
(2) The repair surface must be free from moisture, condensation, and rain from surface
preparation through Dry-to-T ape time of the final coat of the clearcoat.
(3) T ack rags may be used as soon as the conversion coating has dried or 15 minutes has
elapsed, whichever is greater.
(4) One hour after application of the conversion coating, or when dry to the touch (whichever is
greater), masking materials and markers may be used on the surfaces treated with the
conversion coating.
(5) Primer may be applied to surfaces treated with the conversion coating as soon as the coating
has dried or 15 minutes has elapsed, whichever is greater.
(6) Conversion coating-treated surfaces can be overcoated with primer for up to 168 hours for any
combination of ambient or elevated temperature conditions between 55°F (13°C) and 120°F
(49°C).
(a) Surfaces treated with the conversion coating with applied masking tape may be exposed
to higher temperatures.
(b) Dry all airplane surfaces thoroughly before priming.
(7) Definitions
(a) Water-Break-Free Surface
1) If the surface is clean and free from oil, water will lay on the surface in an unbroken
film and sheet off. If the surface is not clean, water will bead on the surface (lay on
the surface in droplets) or appear as patchy and non-uniform.
51-21-99
2) The surface is considered free of water breaks if the continuous film of water is
maintained for approximately 30 seconds.

E. Prepare for the Repair of the Basecoat/Clearcoat Paint System
SUBTASK 51-21-99-280-001
(1) Make sure that the temperature of the repair surface is at least 35°F (2°C).
SUBTASK 51-21-99-200-001
(2) Make sure that the repair surface is free from moisture, condensation, and rain.
SUBTASK 51-21-99-950-012
(3) Mask the surface adjacent to the area you will apply the Basecoat/Clearcoat paint system.
(a) Attach the tags that follow to the left control wheel in the flight deck.
1) "PITOT PROBES COVERED" tag, G02447
2) "STATIC PORTS COVERED" tag, G02444
3) A red tag labeled “AOA SENSORS COVERED”
(b) Put a pitot probe cover, COM-1503, on the pitot probe.
(c) Apply Scotch Flatback Masking T ape 250, G00270, on the static ports.
(d) Do the steps that follow to cover the Angle-Of-Attack (AOA) sensors.
1) Cover the angle-of-attack sensors with PG-777 solvent-resistant masking tape.
a) Apply PG-777 over the blade, plate, and blade-to-plate gap.
NOTE: This is to allow easier removal of 3M 425 aluminum tape.
2) Cover the PG-777 solvent-resistant masking tape with 3M 425 Aluminum Foil T ape,
G00291.
a) Shingle the aluminum foil tape from the blade-to-plate gap up to the blade tip
until all of the masking tape is covered.
F . Repair the Aerodur 3001 Basecoat Surface with Damage or Defects
SUBTASK 51-21-99-370-013
(1) If the damage or defect area is small or if there is a small area of overspray, do the steps that
follow.
(a) If it has been less than 2 hours since the application, wipe the damage or defect area
with isopropyl alcohol (IPA), methyl propyl ketone (MPK) solvent, B00666, or a 70/30
mixture by volume of MPK solvent, B00666/methyl ethyl ketone (MEK) solvent, B00148.
(b) If it has been more than 2 hours since application of Aerodur 3001 Basecoat, or if the
damage or defect cannot be removed by wiping the area, do the steps that follow.
1) Sand the area with 180 to 320 grit aluminum oxide/zirconium oxide (stearate-free)
abrasive paper.
2) Finish sanding with 220 to 320 grit aluminum oxide/zirconium oxide abrasive paper
then one of the very fine abrasive pads that follow.
a) Scotch-Brite General Purpose Hand Pad, 7447, very fine, aluminum oxide.
b) Scotch-Brite Clean and Finish, very fine grade a, aluminum oxide.
c) Norton Bear-T ex 747 General Purpose Hand Pad, very fine, aluminum oxide.
51-21-99
(c) Remove contaminants, if necessary.
1) Solvent wipe the area.
2) Power abrade with one of the fine abrasive pads that follow.
NOTE: This is to remove solvent streaks.
a) Scotch-Brite General Purpose Hand Pad 7447, very fine, aluminum oxide.
b) Scotch-Brite Clean and Finish, very fine grade a, aluminum oxide.
c) Norton Bear-T ex 747 General Purpose Hand Pad, very fine, aluminum oxide.
(d) Remove sanding dust.
1) Wipe the surface with a wiper, G50262, wet with water.
2) Wipe the surface with a clean wiper, G50262.
(e) Wipe the surface with a tack rag, G50123, or G99R17-22 tack rag.
(f) Re-apply the selected BMS10-72 exterior paint system primer (coating, C50085 or
coating, C50318).
(g) Re-apply the computer generated premasks, if necessary.
(h) Re-apply the Aerodur 3001 Basecoat.
1) Make sure that the Aerodur 3001 Basecoat completely covers the primer and the
primer overspray.
G. Repair the Aerodur 3002 Clearcoat Surface with Damage or Defects
SUBTASK 51-21-99-370-014
(1) If the defect can be corrected by buffing, do the steps that follow.
(a) Make sure that the Aerodur 3002 Clearcoat has cured for a minimum of 6 hours at
temperatures warmer than 110°F (43°C).
(b) Buff the Aerodur 3002 Clearcoat with one of the materials that follow.
NOTE: Any abrasive pad, buffing compound, or buffing pads may be used that do not
detract from the overall appearance of the surface after buffing.
1) For the first buffing step, use Finesse-It Finishing Material 13084 White, Easy Clean
Up (3M) with Finesse-It Natural Buffing Pad 81470 (3 inch, 3M) or Perfect-It Wool
Compounding Pad (9 inch, 3M).
2) For the final buffing step, use Finesse-It Polish 51056 (purple, 3M) with Finesse-It
Pad 02627 (3 3/4 inch, orange foam, 3M) or Perfect-It Foam Polishing Pad 05707 (8
inch, 3M) for the final buffing step.
SUBTASK 51-21-99-370-015
(2) If the defect cannot be corrected by buffing, do the steps that follow.
(a) Remove the damage or defect.
1) Sand the area with 180 to 240 grit aluminum oxide/zirconium oxide abrasive paper.
2) Finish sanding the area with one of the very fine abrasive pads that follow.
a) Scotch-Brite General Purpose Hand Pad 7447, very fine, aluminum oxide.
b) Scotch-Brite Clean and Finish, very fine grade a, aluminum oxide.
c) Norton Bear-T ex 747 General Purpose Hand Pad, very fine, aluminum oxide.
(b) Remove contaminants, if necessary.
1) Use solvent on a very fine abrasive pad, white cleaning pad, or wiper, G50262.
51-21-99
2) Make sure that the solvent does not dry on the surface.
3) Immediately wipe the area dry with a clean, dry wiper, G50262.
4) Power abrade with one of the very fine abrasive pads that follow.
NOTE: This is to remove solvent streaks.
a) Scotch-Brite General Purpose Hand Pad 7447, very fine, aluminum oxide.
b) Scotch-Brite Clean and Finish, very fine grade a, aluminum oxide.
c) Norton Bear-T ex 747 General Purpose Hand Pad, very fine, aluminum oxide.
(c) Remove sanding dust.
1) Wipe the surface with a wiper, G50262, wet with water or a tack rag, G50123, tack
rag, G50123, or G99R17-22 tack rag.
(d) Prepare the surface for overcoating, if necessary.
1) See the procedures for preparing CRES and Titanium surfaces or Aluminum
surfaces.
(e) If the sanding broke into the primer, do the steps that follow.
1) Apply the applicable primer (coating, C50085 or coating, C50318) to the area with
no primer.
2) Apply computer generated premasks, if necessary.
3) Apply the Aerodur 3001 Basecoat.
a) Make sure to completely cover the primer and primer overspray.
NOTE: If you do not completely cover the primer and primer overspray, a
yellow edge will show through the Aerodur 3002 Clearcoat.
4) Apply Nonmetallic Special Visual Effects coating, if necessary.
5) Apply stencils and BMS10-26 markings, if necessary.
6) Apply the BMS10-127, Type I, Grade F , if necessary.
7) Apply the Aerodur 3002 Clearcoat.
8) Apply BMS10-26 markings, if necessary.
H. Prepare the CRES and Titanium Surfaces for Overcoating
SUBTASK 51-21-99-950-001
(1) Mask the areas adjacent to the work surface.
(a) Mask the areas that follow if they are adjacent to the work surface.
1) Areas that will not be painted
2) Previously painted surfaces
3) Openings where liquids can be trapped
4) Plastic
5) Fiberglass
6) Sealant
7) Rubber
8) Windows
a) Cover the windows with Protex P-5 10V paper, Protex 20V tape, G00139, or
8216-2L masking material
51-21-99
<1> Make sure that the cover extends over the forging.
b) Use T ape - Masking, G50509, Scotch Brand No.471 tape, G02219, or PG-777
tape to seal the edge of the paper or film to the outer edge of the forging.
<1> Make sure that the tape is at least 1 in. (25 mm) wide.
<2> Use a squeegee to make sure that the tape does not lift from the
forging.
9) Radomes
10) Antenna
11) High strength steels
12) Gaskets
(b) If you will mask surfaces coated with chemical conversion coating, make sure that it has
been 1 hour since the chemical conversion coating was applied or that the surface is dry
to the touch (whichever is greater).
SUBTASK 51-21-99-370-016
(2) Abrade the surface.
(a) Use progressively finer Bear-T ex silicon carbide abrasive discs (or equivalent) or one of
the abrasive pads that follow.
1) Scotch-Brite General Purpose Hand Pad 7447, very fine, aluminum oxide.
2) Scotch-Brite Clean and Finish, very fine grade a, aluminum oxide.
3) Norton Bear-T ex 747 General Purpose Hand Pad, very fine, aluminum oxide.
(b) Finish abrading with 220 grit or finer Bear-T ex silicon carbide abrasive discs (or
equivalent) or one of the abrasive pads that follow.
1) Scotch-Brite General Purpose Hand Pad 7447, 220 grit or finer, aluminum oxide.
2) Scotch-Brite Clean and Finish, 220 grit or finer grade a, aluminum carbide.
3) Norton Bear-T ex 747 General Purpose Hand Pad, 220 grit or finer, aluminum oxide.
(c) Clean the surface using one of the methods that follow.
1) Solvent Clean Method
a) Use solvent on a very fine abrasive pad, white cleaning pad, or wiper, G50262.
b) Make sure that the solvent does not dry on the surface.
c) Immediately wipe the surface dry with a clean, dry wiper.
2) Alkaline Clean Method
a) Use one part alkaline cleaner with seven parts water, by volume.
b) Clean the surface with alkaline cleaner and very fine abrasive pads or white
cleaning pads.
c) Make sure that the alkaline cleaner solution does not dry on the surface.
d) Rinse the surface with a large quantity of water.
<1> Make sure that there is no alkaline cleaner or abrasive residue in the
joint and fastener joints.
e) Check the pH of the surface.
<1> Make sure that the pH is between 4.5 and 8.5.
51-21-99
<2> If the pH is not between 4.5 and 8.5, continue to rinse the surface with
large quantities of water.
(d) Check for water breaks on the surface.
1) If water breaks occur, do the steps to prepare the CRES and Titanium surfaces
again.
I. Prepare Aluminum Surfaces for Overcoating
SUBTASK 51-21-99-370-017
(1) Deoxidize the surface.
(a) Power wet abrade or hand wet abrade the surface with abrasive pads and water.
1) Make sure that the surface ahead of the power abrader is wet with water.
2) Make sure that the surface does not dry before rinsing with water.
(b) Rinse the surface with a large quantity of water.
(c) If the surface will not be alkaline cleaned, check for water breaks on all surfaces.
1) If there are water breaks on the surface, do the wet abrade procedure again.
(d) Alkaline clean the surface (optional).
1) Use one part alkaline cleaner with seven parts water, by volume.
2) Manually clean the surface with very fine abrasive pads or white cleaning pads.
3) Make sure that the alkaline cleaner does not dry on the surface.
4) Rinse the surface with a large quantity of water.
a) Make sure that the surface is clean of all alkaline cleaner and abrasive
residues.
b) Make sure that all joints and fastener areas are clean of all alkaline cleaner
and abrasive residues.
5) Check the pH of the surface.
a) Make sure that the pH is between 4.5 and 8.5.
b) If the pH is not between 4.5 and 8.5, continue to rinse the surface with large
quantities of water.
6) Check for water breaks on the surface.
a) If there are water breaks on the surface, repeat the alkaline cleaning
procedure.

SUBTASK 51-21-99-370-019

(b) sol-gel coating, C50157

SUBTASK 51-21-99-950-004

51-21-99
(a) Make sure to wait at least one hour after application of the chemical conversion coating,

(3) Make sure that the temperature is between 65°F (18°C) and 95°F (35°C).
51-21-99
SUBTASK 51-21-99-370-024

SUBTASK 51-21-99-370-025

SUBTASK 51-21-99-370-026

(8) Apply the primer.
(a) Make sure that there is a continuous layer of primer.
(b) Make sure that the dry film thickness of the chrome primer is 0.4 to 1.0 mil.
(c) Make sure that the dry film thickness of the non-chrome primer is 0.3 to 1.0 mil.

*[1] If the maximum dry time is exceeded, reactivate the surface.
*[2] Dry-to-tape times are approximate. When you remove the tape, there should be no visible effects on the primer film.
*[3] If the temperature is warmer than 120°F (49°C), reactivate the surface.
*[4] If no composition is specified, use Composition C.
*[5] If BMS10-72, Type IX, Composition C (chromated) primers are to be overcoated with BMS10-72, Type IX,
Composition NC (non-chromated) primer, allow the chromated primer to air dry for a minimum of 15 minutes.
*[6] For small area application or small area rework where the risk of appearance problem is low (6 ft
2 (1 m 2) or smaller),
a minimum overcoat time of 30 minutes at ambient conditions is allowed.
(a) If the dry time to recoat or overcoat surface is greater than 48 hours, or if the temperature
is warmer than 120°F (49°C), reactivate the surface with the steps that follow.
1) If there are contaminants on the surface, solvent clean the area.
a) Use solvent on a very fine abrasive pad, white cleaning pad, or wiper, G50262.
b) Make sure that the solvent does not dry on the surface.
c) Immediately wipe the surface dry with a clean, dry wiper, G50262.
2) Sand the surface.
a) Use progressively finer abrasive paper or very fine abrasive pad.
<1> Use one of the materials that follow.
<a> 100 grit or finer wet or dry type aluminum oxide paper.
<b> 100 grit or finer Bear T ex silicon carbide abrasive disc.
<c> Scotch-Brite General Purpose Hand Pad 7447, very fine,
aluminum oxide.
51-21-99
<d> Scotch-Brite Clean and Finish, very fine, grade a, aluminum
oxide.
<e> Norton Bear-T ex 747 General Purpose Hand Pad, very fine,
aluminum oxide.
<f> 100 grit or finer aluminum oxide/zirconium oxide abrasive paper.
<2> If you use a very fine abrasive pad, use a pneumatic sander.
b) Finish sanding with 220 grit or finer abrasive paper or a very fine abrasive pad.
<1> Use one of the materials that follow.
<a> 220 grit or finer wet or dry type aluminum oxide paper.
<b> 220 grit or finer Bear T ex silicon carbide abrasive disc.
<c> Scotch-Brite General Purpose Hand Pad 7447, very fine,
aluminum oxide.
<d> Scotch-Brite Clean and Finish, very fine, grade a, aluminum
oxide.
<e> Norton Bear-T ex 747 General Purpose Hand Pad, very fine,
aluminum oxide.
<f> 220 grit or finer aluminum oxide/zirconium oxide abrasive paper.
<2> If you use a very fine abrasive pad, use a pneumatic sander.
3) Solvent clean the surface.
a) Use solvent on a very fine abrasive pad, white cleaning pad, or wiper, G50262.
b) Make sure that the solvent does not dry on the surface.
c) Immediately wipe the surface dry with a clean, dry wiper, G50262.

angles without magnification at a distance less than 10 ft (3 m) away.

100°F (38°C) for more than 6 hours, the surface must be abraded before application of stencil paint.

temperatures colder than 85°F (29°C) for 8 hours maximum.
*[7] For A9055, heat cure at temperatures warmer than 100°F (38°C) for 6 hours followed by curing at temperatures
colder than 85°F (29°C) for 8 hours maximum.
(b) See Table 709 for the recommended cure times before flight.

S. Final Appearance Requirements
SUBTASK 51-21-99-200-009
(1) If there are visible defects (dry spray, overspray) in the Aerodur 3001 Basecoat, they are
acceptable if they are not visible after you apply Aerodur 3002 Clearcoat.
SUBTASK 51-21-99-700-001
(2) Make sure that you do not do cohesion tests on the Aerodur 3001 Basecoat layer. Do adhesion
tests on the Aerodur 3002 Clearcoat layer.
51-21-99
SUBTASK 51-21-99-200-010
(3) To determine the Aerodur 3001 Basecoat thickness, take the difference between the total
inspection thickness allowed and the average primer thickness that is measured.
SUBTASK 51-21-99-200-011
(4) To determine the Aerodur 3002 Clearcoat thickness, take the difference between the total
inspection thickness allowed and the sum of the primer thickness and the Aerodur 3001
Basecoat thickness.
51-21-99-250-801Decorative Exterior Paint System Thickness Measurement (Eddy Current Testing Method)PDF p. 444
Open original AMM PDF - page 444 ↗ 
5. Decorative Exterior Paint System Thickness Measurement (Eddy Current Testing Method).
A. Procedure (Eddy Current Method)
NOTE: This task is intended to supplement test equipment manufacturers' instructions for the
manual operation of the test equipment, and is not intended to replace them.
SUBTASK 51-21-99-100-002
(1) Prepare for the Inspection.
(a) Get access to the inspection area.
(b) Remove loose paint, dirt and sealant from the surface of the part to be examined.
(c) Make sure that the temperature in the area where the material will be examined is
between 40°F (4°C) and 90°F (32°C) and will not change during the test. Do not do a test
of the material in direct sunlight or in a cool airflow.
NOTE: Changes in material temperature will change the conductivity.
SUBTASK 51-21-99-820-001
(2) Calibrate the Instrument.
NOTE: Different test equipment manufacturers follow different methods of calibration or
adjustment. Verify calibration according to manufacturer's instructions.
(a) To decrease the risk of errors caused by temperature, put the instrument, probe,
reference standards, and the material to be examined at the location where the
inspection will be done.
(b) Use nonconductive thickness shims to verify calibration or test blocks bearing calibrated
nonconductive coatings that are traceable to a suitable National Institute of Standards
and T echnology (NIST) or similar regional standards authority.
(c) Make sure that the instrument, probe, reference standards, and the material to be
examined are at the same temperature.
(d) Measure the thickness of a series of non conductive shims covering the expected range
of coating thickness.
NOTE: Verify the test equipment at regular intervals to prevent an inaccurate reading.
SUBTASK 51-21-99-250-001
(3) Measure the paint thickness as follows:
(a) Inspect the test equipment probe and area to be measured are clean and free of debris.
(b) T ake multiple readings on the on the bare surface (areas free from paint) to establish the
base reference.
(c) Identify the minimum distance the test equipment must be from edges, fasteners, ridges
or other structure and the curvature limit that would cause an abnormal measurement
change.
NOTE: This prevents incorrect indications on the test equipment.
(d) T ake multiple readings on the on the coated surface to establish the paint thickness
measurement.
SUBTASK 51-21-99-970-001
(4) Report the information as follows (if necessary):
(a) Instrument used, serial number.
(b) Range, mean average of the thickness indications.
51-21-99
(c) Record each indication if necessary.
51-21-99-270-801Decorative Exterior Paint System Thickness Measurement (Ultrasonic Testing Method)PDF p. 445
Open original AMM PDF - page 445 ↗ 
6. Decorative Exterior Paint System Thickness Measurement (Ultrasonic Testing Method).
A. Procedure (Ultrasonic Method)
SUBTASK 51-21-99-100-003
NOTE: This task is intended to supplement test equipment manufacturers' instructions for the
manual operation of the test equipment, and is not intended to replace them.
(1) Prepare for the Inspection.
(a) Get access to the inspection area.
(b) Remove loose paint, dirt and sealant from the surface of the part to be examined.
NOTE: Rough surfaces can give incorrect indications. Thus the area must be smooth.
SUBTASK 51-21-99-820-002
(2) Calibrate the Instrument.
NOTE: Different test equipment manufacturers follow different methods of calibration or
adjustment. Verify calibration according to manufacturer's instructions.
(a) A couplant is necessary to transmit the ultrasonic pulse from the test equipment's probe
into the coating. The couplants that follow can possibly be used with the test equipment:
NOTE: For specific information regarding the use of couplants, refer to the test
equipment’s instructions.
1) Water.
a) Ideal for smooth, thin coatings.
2) Glycol gel.
a) For rough coatings, provided it is not a contaminant for the coating to be
measured.
3) Other liquids such as liquid soap may be used.
(b) Use nonconductive thickness shims to verify calibration or test blocks bearing calibrated
nonconductive coatings that are traceable to a suitable National Institute of Standards
and T echnology (NIST) or similar regional standards authority.
(c) Although most ultrasonic instruments are pre-calibrated, accuracy should be verified by
measuring a known thickness of the coating (reference standard) as determined by:
1) A thickness equal to or more than the coating thickness range to be measured.
NOTE: Verify the test equipment at regular intervals to prevent an inaccurate
reading.
SUBTASK 51-21-99-270-001
(3) Measure the paint thickness as follows:
(a) Inspect the test equipment probe and area to be measured are clean and free of debris.
(b) Apply couplant to the area.
(c) Place the instrument’s probe flat on the surface and apply constant pressure. Hold the
probe steady during the measurement.
(d) T ake multiple readings on the on the bare surface (areas free from paint) to establish the
base reference.
51-21-99
(e) Identify the minimum distance the test equipment must be from edges, fasteners, ridges
or other structure and the curvature limit that would cause an abnormal measurement
change.
NOTE: This prevents incorrect indications on the test equipment.
(f) T ake multiple readings on the on the coated surface to establish the paint thickness
measurement.
(g) Remove the couplant used in the process.
SUBTASK 51-21-99-970-002
(4) Report the information as follows (if necessary):
(a) Instrument used, serial number.
(b) Type of base material.
(c) Type of coating.
(d) Range, mean average of the thickness indications.
(e) Record each indication if necessary.
